with the following explicit transformation property:

\displaystyle S^{-1}(\Lambda)\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}S(\Lambda)=\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)\;\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{B}},\quad\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}(x)\to\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{}^{\prime}\;\,\mathbf{M}}(x)=\Lambda_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}(x),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{C}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\Lambda^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}-\Lambda^{\mathbf{B}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{C}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\Lambda^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}}\right)+\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{C}}\Lambda^{\mathbf{B}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{D}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}(x),

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{B}}(x),\quad\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)\in\mbox{SP}(1,D_{h}\mbox{-}1).

(10)

The local
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge transformation is given as follows:

\displaystyle\breve{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)=\check{S}(x)i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\check{S}^{-1}(x)+\check{S}(x)\breve{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\check{S}^{-1}(x),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{{}^{\prime}(q_{c},k)}(x)=\check{S}(x)\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x),\quad\check{S}(x)=e^{i\varpi_{\mathbf{A}}(x)\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}/2}\in W^{1,D_{h}-1},

(11)

with the following explicit form:

\displaystyle\check{S}(x)=1+i\varpi_{\mathbf{A}}(x)\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}/2,\quad{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}}(x)\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{{}^{\prime}(q_{c},k)}(x)=\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)+\check{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x),\quad\check{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)\equiv i\frac{1}{2}\varpi_{\mathbf{A}}(x)\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x),

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)+{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\varpi^{\mathbf{A}}(x),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\varpi^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\equiv\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\varpi^{\mathbf{A}}(x)+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)\varpi^{\mathbf{B}}(x).

(12)

The intrinsic
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin gauge invariance of the action in Eq.(
5
) is easily checked under the following gauge transformation:

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)=\tilde{S}(\Lambda)i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\tilde{S}^{-1}(\Lambda)+\tilde{S}(\Lambda)\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\tilde{S}^{-1}(\Lambda),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{{}^{\prime}(q_{c},k)}(x)=\tilde{S}(\Lambda)\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x),\quad\tilde{S}(\Lambda)=e^{i\varpi_{pq}(x)\tilde{\varSigma}^{pq}/2}\in SP(q_{c}),

(13)

with the explicit forms given by,

\displaystyle\tilde{S}^{-1}(\Lambda)\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}^{p}\tilde{S}(\Lambda)=\Lambda^{p}_{\;\;\;q}(x)\;\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}^{q},\quad\Lambda^{p}_{\;\;q}(x)\in SP(q_{c}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{{}^{\prime}pq}(x)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\Lambda^{p}_{\;\;p^{\prime}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\Lambda^{p^{\prime}q}-\Lambda^{q}_{\;\;p^{\prime}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\Lambda^{p^{\prime}p}\right)+\Lambda^{p}_{\;\;p^{\prime}}\Lambda^{q}_{\;\;q^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}(x),

\displaystyle\phi_{p}(x)\to\phi^{\prime}_{p}(x)=\Lambda_{p}^{\;\;q}(x)\phi_{q}(x).

(14)

In obtaining the above transformation properties, we have used the following identities:

\displaystyle S^{-1}(\Lambda)\varGamma^{\mathbf{A}}S(\Lambda)=\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)\varGamma^{\mathbf{B}},\quad\varGamma^{\mathbf{C}}\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\varGamma_{\mathbf{C}}=(D_{h}-4)\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle S(\Lambda)i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}S^{-1}(\Lambda)=\frac{1}{4}\left(\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{C}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\Lambda^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}-\Lambda^{\mathbf{B}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{C}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\Lambda^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}}\right)\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\tilde{S}^{-1}(\Lambda)\tilde{\varGamma}^{p}\tilde{S}(\Lambda)=\Lambda^{p}_{\;\;q}(x)\tilde{\varGamma}^{q},\quad\tilde{\varGamma}^{p^{\prime}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{pq}\varGamma_{p^{\prime}}=(D_{h}-4)\tilde{\varSigma}_{pq},

\displaystyle\tilde{S}(\Lambda)i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\tilde{S}^{-1}(\Lambda)=\frac{1}{4}\left(\Lambda^{p}_{\;\;\;p^{\prime}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\Lambda^{p^{\prime}q}-\Lambda^{q}_{\;\;p^{\prime}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\Lambda^{p^{\prime}p}\right)\tilde{\varSigma}_{pq},

(15)

where the matrices
S(\Lambda)
and
\tilde{S}(\Lambda)
as well as
\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)
and
\Lambda^{p}_{\;\;\;q}(x)
correspond to local group elements in the qubit-spinor representations as well as adjoin representations of gauge groups SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) and SP(
q_{c}
), which should be manifest as the categoric entangled hyperqubit-spinor field is the presented in qubit-spinor representation and both hyperspin gauge field and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin gauge field belong to the adjoint representations of gauge symmetry groups SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) and SP(
q_{c}
), respectively. The bicovariant vector field and scalar field are in the vector representations of gauge symmetry groups SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) and SP(
q_{c}
), respectively.

From the general covariant derivative
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathbf{M}}
, the field strength of gauge field
\mathfrak{A}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv i[\mathfrak{D}_{\mathbf{M}},\mathfrak{D}_{\mathbf{N}}]={\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}+\check{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}+\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)\equiv{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}}(x)-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)-i[{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x),{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}}(x)],

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)\equiv\check{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{-\mathbf{A}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{N}}(x)-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)-i({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{N}}(x)-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}}(x)\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)),

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)\equiv\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{pq}(x)\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\varSigma}_{pq}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{N}}(x)-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)-i[\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x),\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{N}}(x)],

(16)

In general, the action in Eq.(
5
) possesses the following
joint symmetry
,

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle PO(1,D_{h}-1)\Join WS(1,D_{h}-1)\times SP(q_{c}),

(17)

where PO(1,
D_{h}
-1) remains global inhomogeneous Lorentz-type/Poincaré-type group symmetry,

\displaystyle PO(1,D_{h}-1)\equiv P^{1,D_{h}-1}\ltimes SO(1,D_{h}-1),

(18)

which characterizes symmetry property of globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime.

We would like to address that the global inhomogeneous Lorentz-type/Poincaré-type group PO(1,
D_{h}
-1) and local inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge group WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) do not form a direct product group symmetry as the former is a global symmetry and the later is a local symmetry, they are presented as a
joint symmetry
denoted by the symbol “
\Join
.

II.2
Scaling gauge symmetry based on gauge invariance principle and the gauge covariant equation of motion of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field as gravitational relativistic quantum theory

It can be verified that the action in Eq.(
5
) is invariant under the following global scaling transformation:

\displaystyle x^{\mathbf{M}}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}=\lambda^{-1}\mspace{1mu}x^{\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\to\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x^{\prime})=\lambda\mspace{1mu}\mathfrak{A}_{\mathbf{M}}(x),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)\to\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{{}^{\prime}(q_{c},k)}(x^{\prime})=\lambda^{\frac{D_{h}-1}{2}}\mspace{1mu}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x),

(19)

with
\lambda
a constant scaling factor.

To distinguish scaling symmetry of coordinate system in Minkowski hyper-spacetime from that of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field in Hilbert space, we should extend the global scaling symmetry of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field to be local scaling gauge symmetry based on the gauge invariance principle, which has the following scaling gauge transformation:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)\to\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{{}^{\prime}(q_{c},k)}(x)=\xi^{\frac{D_{h}-1}{2}}(x)\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)\to\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{}^{\prime}\;\mathbf{M}}(x)=\xi(x)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x),

\displaystyle\phi_{p}(x)\to\phi_{p}(x)=\xi(x)\phi_{p}(x),

(20)

where
\xi(x)
is a functional scaling factor. Following along the gauge invariance principle, we should introduce a
scaling gauge field

\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
with the following gauge transformation:

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\to\mathsf{W}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)=\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)-\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\ln\xi(x),

(21)

which was initiated by Weyl
Weyl
to characterize an electromagnetic field though it was turned out to be incorrect.

To preserve the scaling gauge symmetry, the action in Eq.(
5
) is extended in formal to be as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c})}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}}x]\,\chi(x)\,\{\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}(x)i\mathfrak{D}_{\mathbf{M}}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)

(22)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lambda_{q_{c}}\phi_{p}(x)\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}^{p}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)\},

with the generalized covariant derivative,

\displaystyle i\mathfrak{D}_{\mathbf{M}}\equiv i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)+\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)+\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)+\frac{D_{h}-1}{2}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x).

(23)

It is noted that the multiplier factor
\chi(x)
as the inverse of the determinant of bicovaraint vector field
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}(x)
has been introduced by regarding
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}(x)
as a matrix field, i.e.:

\displaystyle\chi(x)\equiv 1/\hat{\chi}(x),\quad\hat{\chi}(x)=\det\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)\neq 0,

(24)

which is multiplied to the action so as to ensure the scaling gauge invariance with the following scaling gauge transformation:

\displaystyle\chi(x)\to\chi^{\prime}(x)=\xi^{-D_{h}}(x)\chi(x).

(25)

From the matrix property,
\chi(x)
can be expressed as the determinant of
dual bicovaraint vector field

\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
which is viewed as an inverse matrix field of the matrix field
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}(x)
. Such a dual bicovaraint vector field is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)\,\hat{\chi}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{B}}(x)=\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{B}\,\mathbf{N}}(x)\eta^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\eta^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)\hat{\chi}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{A}}(x)=\chi_{\mathbf{M}\,\mathbf{A}}(x)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}(x)\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle\chi(x)=\det\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)=\hat{\chi}^{-1}(x),

(26)

where the matrix fields
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
and
\hat{\chi}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{B}}(x)
satisfy orthonormality conditions.

From the self-conjugated chiral property of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field in category-
q_{c}
and the transpose property of
\varGamma
-matrices under complex charge conjugation, i.e.:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)\,c}(x)=C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)\,T}(x)=\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x),

\displaystyle C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}\varGamma^{\mathbf{A}}\left(C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}\right)^{-1}=-\left(\varGamma^{\mathbf{A}}\right)^{T},

\displaystyle C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}\varGamma^{D_{h}+p}\left(C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}\right)^{-1}=\left(\varGamma^{D_{h}+p}\right)^{T},

(27)

together with the chirality property of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field, it can be verified that the gauge interactions of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field with both
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge field
\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
and scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
should be absent in the hermitian action presented in Eq.(
5
).

The gauge invariant hermitian action in Eq.(
5
) can actually be rewritten as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c})}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}^{(q_{c},k)}}x]\,\chi(x)\,\{\,\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}(x)i\hat{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{M}}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)

(28)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lambda_{q_{c}}\phi_{p}(x)\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}^{p}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)\}.

with the definitions,

\displaystyle i\hat{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{M}}\equiv i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}+\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x),\quad\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)+\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x).

(29)

Such an action possesses the following
joint symmetry
:

\displaystyle G_{S}=SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,D_{h}-1)\Join WS(1,D_{h}-1)\rtimes SG(1)\times SP(q_{c}),

(30)

where SC(1) and SG(1) denote
global scaling symmetry
and
local scaling gauge symmetry
, respectively.

From the gauge invariant action in Eq.(
28
), we can derive the following equation of motion for categoric entangled hyperqubit-spinor field:

\displaystyle\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)\left(i\hat{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{M}}-i{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)-\lambda_{q_{c}}\phi_{p}(x)\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}^{p}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)=0,

(31)

where
{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
represents an
induced gauge field
arising from the bi-covariant vector field and inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge field via a hidden gauge formalism, which has the following explicit form:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\chi(x)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}(x)\right){\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\left(\hat{\chi}(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)\right),

(32)

where
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\left(\hat{\chi}(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)\right)
defines the covariant derivative of dual bicovaraint vector field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\left(\hat{\chi}(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)\right)\equiv\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\left(\hat{\chi}(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)\right)+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\,\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{B}}(x)\left(\hat{\chi}(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}}(x)\right).

(33)

It can be checked that
{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
preserves local scaling gauge invariance in the above equation of motion with the following transformation property:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)={\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)+\frac{1}{2}(D_{h}-1)\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\xi(x).

(34)

It can be verified that the gauge covariant equation of motion Eq.(
31
) is local scaling gauge invariant. Let us now derive the quadratic form for the equation of motion Eq.(
31
), which is found to have the following form:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\nabla}_{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}})(\hat{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{N}}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{N}})\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)=\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\cdot\left({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}+\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}-{\cal G}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}i(\hat{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{P}}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{P}})-i{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\,\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\left(\lambda_{q_{c}}^{2}\phi^{2}_{p}+\lambda_{q_{c}}\varGamma^{\mathbf{A}}\tilde{\varGamma}^{p}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(i\tilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{M}}\phi_{p})\,\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x),

(35)

where we have introduced the following definition:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)=\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}(x)\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

(36)

for the symmetric tensor field, and

\displaystyle\hat{\nabla}_{\mathbf{M}}(\hat{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{N}}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{N}})\equiv\hat{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(\hat{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{N}}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{N}})-{\mathit{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}(\hat{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{P}}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{P}}),

\displaystyle{\mathit{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}=\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}(\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\,\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}\,),

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{M}}\phi_{p}\equiv\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\phi_{p}-\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}\,p}^{\;q}\phi_{q},

(37)

for the covariant derivatives
\hat{\nabla}_{\mathbf{M}}
and
\tilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{M}}
. On the right-hand side of Eq.(
II.2
),
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
and
\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
define the field strengths of
hyperspin gauge field

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin gauge field
\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
, respectively, with the following explicit forms:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)\equiv{\cal F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)\,\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}{\cal A}_{\mathbf{N}}(x)-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}{\cal A}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)-i[{\cal A}_{\mathbf{M}}(x),{\cal A}_{\mathbf{N}}(x)],

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)\equiv{\cal F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{pq}(x)\,\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\varSigma}_{pq}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{N}}(x)-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)-i[\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x),\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{N}}(x)],

(38)

and
{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
is the
induced gauge field strength
defined as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\partial_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}.

(39)

The tensor field
{\cal G}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}-{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}

(40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\,\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\,\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x),

which provides the gauge covariant field strength of dual bicovaraint vector field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
with
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
representing the covariant derivative of
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
. It is noticed that the dual bicovaraint vector field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
behaves as a gauge-type vector field and its inverse as bicovaraint vector field
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)
couples to the motion of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field, so that
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
is expected to characterize gravitational interaction in hyper-spacetime. For convenience,
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
is referred to as
hyper-gravigauge field
in hyper-spacetime, which will be demonstrated below for its genesis as gauge field.

The equations of motion presented in Eqs.(
31
) and (
II.2
) for categoric entangled hyperqubit-spinor field
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{E}}}^{(q_{c},k)}(x)
possess global Poincaré-type group symmetry and scaling symmetry jointly with local inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin gauge symmetry as well as local scaling gauge symmetry. The gauge symmetries are described correspondingly by the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin gauge field
\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
as well as hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
and scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}
. Such equations characterize the dynamics of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field, which brings on
gravitational relativistic quantum theory
in hyper-spacetime and is regarded as a generalization to Dirac relativistic quantum theory
DE
. The dynamics of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field is generally governed by the hyperspin gauge field strength
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and hyper-gravigauge field strength
{\cal G}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}
as well as
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin gauge field strength
\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
.

It is noticed that the induced gauge field
{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
and its field strength
{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
distinguish from other gauge fields and field strengths due to the presence of an associated imaginary factor
``i"
, which appears naturally so as to keep the local scaling gauge invariance in the equation of motion of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field. For convenience of mention, we may refer to
{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
as
graviscaling induced gauge field
and
{\mathsf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
as
graviscaling induced gauge field strength
. It is clear that the appearance of graviscaling induced gauge field and its field strength will lead distribution amplitudes of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field to be boosted by the induced scaling gauge factor.

Therefore, the gauge invariance principle as guiding principle brings all symmetries of basic fields in Hilbert space to be local gauge symmetries with the introduction of gauge fields, which enables us to reveal the dynamics of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field as fundamental building block.

III
Hyper-fiber bundle structure of biframe hyper-spacetime with gravitational origin of gauge symmetry and
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field as genesis of hyper-gravigauge field with graviscalar and gravivector fields

The gauge invariance of the action of categoric entangled hyperqubit-spinor field brings on the introduction of bicovariant vector field
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)
. The dual bicovariant vector field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
leads to a gauge-type field strength, which makes
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
to behave as a hyper-gravigauge field in hyper-spacetime. We are going to further demonstrate that such bicovariant vector fields form locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime and bring about the concept of biframe hyper-spacetime. Mathematically, such a biframe hyper-spacetime sets up a hyper-fiber bundle structure with Minkowski hyper-spacetime as base spacetime. Such a locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime is shown to be characterized by non-commutative geometry with Lie algebra structure factor determined by the field strength of hyper-gravigauge field. We will show that it is the hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
that brings on the gravitational origin of gauge symmetry in hyper-spacetime. Furthermore, we will verify that the hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
is identified to be the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field which is introduced via the translation-like
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin Abelian-type gauge symmetry as subgroup gauge symmetry of inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry. Therefore, we get better understanding on the nature of gravity that the gravitational interaction is truly a gauge interaction and also better comprehension on the nature of spacetime that the observable spacetime is actually a biframe spacetime.

III.1
Locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime with emergence of non-commutative geometry and biframe hyper-spacetime with hyper-fiber bundle structure

The Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,
D_{h}
-1) = P
{}^{1,D_{h}\mbox{-}1}

\ltimes
SO(1,
D_{h}
-1) characterizes globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime
M_{h}
which is known to be an
affine spacetime
. The ordinary derivative vector operator
\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\equiv\partial/\partial x^{\mathbf{M}}
at point
x
of
M_{h}
defines a
tangent basis

\{\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\}\equiv\{\partial/\partial x^{\mathbf{M}}\}
for
tangent hyper-spacetime

T_{h}
over
M_{h}
. A displacement vector
dx^{\mathbf{M}}
at point
x
of
M_{h}
provides a
dual tangent basis

\{dx^{\mathbf{M}}\}
for
dual tangent hyper-spacetime

T_{h}^{\ast}
over
M_{h}
. The tangent basis and dual tangent basis satisfy the following dual condition:

\displaystyle\langle dx^{\mathbf{M}},\,\partial/\partial x^{\mathbf{N}}\rangle=\frac{\partial x^{\mathbf{M}}}{\partial x^{\mathbf{N}}}=\eta_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}.

(41)

In Hilbert space, the introduction of bicovaraint vector field
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}(x)
and its dual bicovariant vector field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
not only preserves inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry for characterizing the dynamics of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field, but also allows us to define
field vector operator

\eth_{\mathbf{A}}
and its
dual field vector operator

\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}
at point
x
of
M_{h}
with respective to ordinary coordinate derivative vector operator
\partial_{\mathbf{M}}
and displacement vector operator
dx^{\mathbf{M}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\eth_{\mathbf{A}}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}(x)\partial_{\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}\equiv\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)dx^{\mathbf{M}},

(42)

which forms the field basis
\{\eth_{\mathbf{A}}\}
and dual field basis
\{\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}\}
for locally flat hyper-spacetime over globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime
M_{h}
.

The dual field bases satisfy the following dual condition:

\displaystyle\langle\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}},\eth_{\mathbf{B}}\rangle\equiv\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}(x)\langle dx^{\mathbf{M}},\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\rangle=\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}(x)\eta_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}=\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\,\mathbf{A}}\,.

(43)

Such a pair of dual field bases
\{\eth_{\mathbf{A}}\}
and
\{\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}\}
form a pair of dual locally flat hyper-spacetimes over globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime
M_{h}
. It is easy to verify that the field basis
\{\eth_{\mathbf{A}}\}
does not commute and satisfies the following non-commutation relation:

\displaystyle[i\eth_{\mathbf{A}},\;i\eth_{\mathbf{B}}]=if_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\;\mathbf{C}}(x)\,i\eth_{\mathbf{C}},

\displaystyle f_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\;\mathbf{C}}(x)\equiv-\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}(x)\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}(x),\quad\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}(x)\equiv\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}}(x)-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}}(x).

(44)

which shows that such a non-commutation relation brings on a Lie algebra with non-constant group structure factor
f_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}(x)
characterized by the tensor field
\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}(x)
. Such a tensor field is presented as an Abelian-type field strength of hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}}(x)
. Therefore, the above non-commutation relation brings about a
non-commutative geometry
in locally flat hyper-spacetime, which is described by
Abelian-type hyper-gravigauge field strength

\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}(x)
.

For the convenience of mention, the dual field bases
\{\eth_{\mathbf{A}}\}
and
\{\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}\}
formed by the hyper-gravigauge field are called as a pair of dual
hyper-gravigauge field bases
. Correspondingly, the dual locally flat hyper-spacetimes formed by the dual hyper-gravigauge field bases are referred to as locally flat dual
gravigauge hyper-spacetimes
. Specifically, the gravigauge hyper-spacetime formed from hyper-gravigauge field basis
\{\eth_{\mathbf{A}}\}
is called as tangent-like hyper-spacetime denoted by
G_{h}
, and the dual gravigauge hyper-spacetime formed from dual hyper-gravigauge field basis
\{\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}\}
is called as dual tangent-like hyper-spacetime denoted by
G_{h}^{\ast}
.

In general, the hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
is considered to be sided on the dual tangent Minkowski hyper-spacetime
T_{h}^{\ast}
and valued on the dual gravigauge hyper-spacetime
G_{h}^{\ast}
. The dual hyper-gravigauge field
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)
is defined on the gravigauge hyper-spacetime
G_{h}
and valued on the tangent Minkowski hyper-spacetime
T_{h}
. Namely,
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)
transforms as bicovariant vector field under transformations of both hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) and global Lorentz-type group symmetry SO(1,
D_{h}
-1). The non-commutation relation for hyper-gravigauge field basis
\{\eth_{\mathbf{A}}\}
displays that locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime
G_{h}
is correlated to
non-commutative geometry
.

The gravigauge hyper-spacetime
G_{h}
and tangent Minkowski hyper-spacetime
T_{h}
form a
biframe hyper-spacetime

T_{h}\times G_{h}
associated with a dual
biframe hyper-spacetime

T_{h}^{\ast}\times G_{h}^{\ast}
over coordinate hyper-spacetime
M_{h}
.

Mathematically, globally and locally flat vector spacetimes allow for a canonical identification of vectors in tangent Minkowski hyper-spacetime
T_{h}
at points with vectors in Minkowski hyper-spacetime itself
M_{h}
, and also for a canonical identification of vectors at points with its dual vectors at the same points. For the meaning in physics, either tangent or dual tangent Minkowski hyper-spacetime over globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime is regarded as
free-motion hyper-spacetime

\mathbf{M}_{h}
, while locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime is viewed as
emergent hyper-spacetime

{\mathbf{G}}_{h}
. The canonical identification for the vector spacetimes brings about a simple structure of biframe hyper-spacetime,

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}_{h}=\mathbf{M}_{h}\times\bf{G}_{h},

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{h}\equiv T_{h}\cong T_{h}^{\ast}\cong M_{h},\quad{\mathbf{G}}_{h}\equiv G_{h}\cong G_{h}^{\ast}.

(45)

Such a biframe hyper-spacetime structure forms
gravigauge hyper-fiber bundle

\mathbf{E}_{h}
with locally flat emergent gravigauge hyper-spacetime as a hyper-fiber
{\mathbf{G}}_{h}
and globally flat free-motion Minkowksi hyper-spacetime as a base spacetime
\mathbf{M}_{h}
.

In mathematics, the hyper-fiber bundle
\mathbf{E}_{h}
is related to the product hyper-spacetime
\mathbf{M}_{h}\times{\mathbf{G}}_{h}
as biframe hyper-spacetime via a continuous surjective map
\Pi_{\chi}
, which projects the bundle
\mathbf{E}_{h}
to the base spacetime
\mathbf{M}_{h}
. Geometrically, the
gravigauge hyper-fiber bundle structure
of biframe hyper-spacetime is expressed as
(\mathbf{E}_{h},\mathbf{M}_{h},\Pi_{\chi},{\mathbf{G}}_{h})
. In general, the hyper-fiber bundle
\mathbf{E}_{h}
with the surjective map
\Pi_{\chi}
is expressed as follows with the trivial case:

\displaystyle\Pi_{\chi}:\;\;\mathbf{E}_{h}\to\mathbf{M}_{h},

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{h}\sim\mathbf{B}_{h}=\mathbf{M}_{h}\times{\mathbf{G}}_{h}.

(46)

III.2
Gravitational origin of gauge symmetry and the hyperspin gravigauge and covariant-gauge fields with gauge covariant field strengths

In the usual gauge theory, the internal gauge symmetry is attributed in formal to a pure gauge field, one can always choose a configuration via a gauge transformation to eliminate unphysical part arising from gauge symmetry. By making such a gauge prescription in unitary gauge, the gauge field keeps only independent degrees of freedom. Nevertheless, for hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1), the situation appears to be distinguishable as it is resulted from gauging global hyperspin symmetry which is initiated from a motion-correlation
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin symmetry. In fact, when building the gauge invariant action of hyperqubit-spinor field, we are led to introduce not only the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
but also the bicovariant vector field
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)
. It is conceivable that such a bicovariant vector field must be in correlation to hyperspin gauge symmetry. To corroborate such an idea, let us decompose the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
into two parts
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
and
\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)=\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)+\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

(47)

so that
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
obeys an inhomogeneous transformation of hyperspin gauge symmetry and
\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
transforms homogeneously under hyperspin gauge transformation, i.e.:

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\to\mathsf{\Omega}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)=S(\Lambda)i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}S^{-1}(\Lambda)+S(\Lambda)\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)S^{-1}(\Lambda),

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\to\mathsf{A}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)=S(\Lambda)\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)S^{-1}(\Lambda),\quad S(\Lambda)\in SP(1,D_{h}-1).

(48)

For a usual internal gauge field,
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}
is generally taken as a pure gauge field with vanishing field strength. In the present case, the gauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}
with inhomogeneous gauge transformation is presumed to correlate with the hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
. This is because when turning hyperspin gauge symmetry to be global symmetry with group transformation being coincidental to that of global Lorentz-type group symmetry, the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}
disappears and hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
becomes the constant bicovariant vector
\delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
.

It can be verified that
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}
does correlate to the hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
and its explicit form is uniquely given as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{B}}-\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}-\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}\right),

(49)

where the antisymmetric tensor
\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}
is defined as Abelian-type field strength of hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
shown in Eq.(
III.1
). The gauge transformation of hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
in the vector representation of hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) does lead
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
to get the following proper gauge transformation:

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{{}^{\prime}\;\mathbf{A}}=\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\mathbf{C}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{C}},\quad\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{}^{\prime}\;\mathbf{M}}=\Lambda_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}},\quad\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\mathbf{C}}\in\mbox{SP(1,$D_{h}$-1)},

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\frac{1}{2}(\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\mathbf{C}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\Lambda^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}-\Lambda^{\mathbf{B}}_{\;\mathbf{C}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{C}})+\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\mathbf{C}}\Lambda^{\mathbf{B}}_{\;\mathbf{D}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}},

(50)

where
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
displays an inhomogeneous gauge transformation in the adjoint representation of hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1).

As
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
is completely determined by the hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
, it involves no additional independent degrees of freedom in the decomposition of hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
. For convenience, we may refer to
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
as
hyperspin gravigauge field
and
\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
as
hyperspin covariant-gauge field
.

It is not possible to make a gauge transformation to eliminate the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
, which is completely different from the usual internal gauge field. To check that explicitly, let us decompose the hyperspin gauge field strength into the following two parts:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\,,

(51)

with the explicit forms,

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\mathsf{D}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathsf{D}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}-\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{B}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{C}},

(52)

where
\mathsf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
is purely the field strength of hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
, which is not vanishing.

The decomposition of hyperspin gauge field enables us to obtain two gauge covariant field strengths
\mathsf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and
\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
in correspondence to the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
and hyperspin covariant-gauge field
\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
. The hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
and its gauge covariant field strength
\mathsf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
characterize the gravitational interaction originated from hyper-gravigauge field.

From the above analysis, we come to the statement about the
gravitational origin of gauge symmetry
that the hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) of entangled qubit-spinor field is essentially governed by the hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
with gauge transformation in the vector representation of SP(1,
D_{h}
-1).

III.3
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field as genesis of hyper-gravigauge field in the presence of graviscalar field

The hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
is defined as the dual field of bicovariant vector field
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}(x)
which is introduced when gauging inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry based on the gauge invariance principle as guiding principle. Although
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
behaves as a gauge-type vector field indicated from its field strength shown either in the equation of motion (Eq.(
40
)) or in the non-commutation relation (Eq.(
III.1
)), while it remains inapparent whether
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
can be regarded as a gauge field introduced directly from a gauge symmetry by just following along the gauge invariance principle. For this purpose, it is interesting to notice that the field strength in Eq.(
40
) for
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
has an analogous formalism with the field strength in Eq.(
II.1
) for
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge field
\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
. As
\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
is introduced via the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge symmetry W
{}^{1,D_{h}-1}
which is the subgroup symmetry of inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1), we are motivated to explore the correlation between the hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
and
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge field
\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
.

In analogous to the decomposition of hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
, let us decompose the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge field
\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
into the following two parts:

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\equiv\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{-\mathbf{A}}=(\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x))\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{-\mathbf{A}},

(53)

where
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
is supposed to have inhomogeneous gauge transformation and
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
becomes gauge invariant under the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge transformation shown in Eq.(
II.1
), while they all transform as covariant vector fields under the hyperspin gauge transformation. Their explicit transformations are presented as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)+{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\varpi^{\mathbf{A}}(x),

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x),

(54)

under the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge transformation, and

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{B}}(x),

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\Lambda^{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{B}}(x),

(55)

under the hyperspin gauge transformation.

For convenience of mention,
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
is referred to as
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gravigauge field
and
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
as
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
. In general, the combination of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
and
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
, i.e.,
\breve{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\equiv\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{-\mathbf{A}}
, is regarded as
inhomogeneous hyperspin covariant-gauge field
.

In order to keep the initial degrees of freedom for the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge field, there must exist certain relations among
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
,
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invarian-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
, hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
and hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
. Indeed, it is natural to propose the following relations:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\equiv{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\varkappa^{\mathbf{B}}(x),

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\equiv\phi(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x),

(56)

where
\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
is introduced as a vector field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime and
\phi(x)
is taken as a real
scalar field
to keep a canonical mass dimension. To ensure the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge transformation, the vector field
\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
must transform as follows:

\displaystyle\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\varkappa^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)+\varpi^{\mathbf{A}}(x).

(57)

which is regarded as a local translation of vector field
\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. As the above transformation reflects the symmetry property of translation-like
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin Abelian-type gauge group,
\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
is viewed as a
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin vector field
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

From the above considerations, it is the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field

\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
that brings on the hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
in association with a scalar field
\phi(x)
. Therefore, the hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
arises from the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field associated with scalar field
\phi(x)
. Such a scalar field is considered to be a gravitational scalar field and referred to as
graviscalar field
for short.

It can be checked that the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin vector field
\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
has zero scaling charges for both global and local scaling symmetries SC(1) and SG(1). Under the transformations of SC(1) and SG(1) symmetries, the graviscalar field
\phi(x)
transforms as follows:

\displaystyle\phi(x)\to\phi^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=\lambda\,\phi(x),\quad x^{\prime}=\lambda^{-1}x,\quad\lambda\in SC(1),

\displaystyle\phi(x)\to\phi^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=\xi(x)\phi(x),\quad\xi(x)\in SG(1).

(58)

The field strength of
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge field can also be decomposed into the following two parts:

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\equiv\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)+{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x),

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)={\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)-{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)={\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)\varkappa_{\mathbf{B}}(x),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)={\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)-{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\equiv\phi(x){\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x),

(59)

where
{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
defines a hyperspin gauge and scaling gauge covariant field strength of hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)-{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)=d_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)-d_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{B}}(x)-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{B}}(x)

(60)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{B}}(x)-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{B}}(x),

with
d_{\mathbf{M}}
defined as
scaling gauge covariant derivative
. Where we have introduced the gauge-type field strength of hyper-gravigauge field as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\equiv d_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)-d_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x),

\displaystyle d_{\mathbf{M}}\equiv\partial_{\mathbf{M}}+\mathbf{S}_{\mathbf{M}},\quad\mathbf{S}_{\mathbf{M}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\ln\phi^{2}(x),

(61)

which becomes scaling gauge covariance but not hyperspin gauge covariance. Where
\mathbf{S}_{\mathbf{M}}
is regarded as a
pure graviscaling gauge field
given by the graviscalar field
\phi(x)
.

Therefore, we arrive at the statement that it is the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge symmetry as subgroup gauge symmetry of inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry that brings on the genesis of dual bicovariant vector field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
as hyper-gravigauge field, which provides a natural explanation on the gauge property of hyper-gravigauge field appearing either in the equation of motion shown in Eq.(
40
) or in the non-commutative relation presented in Eq.(
III.1
).

III.4
Biframe displacement correspondence in biframe hyper-spacetime and the dimensionless hyper-gravicoordinate displacement and derivative with respect to hyper-gravivector field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime

The
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge transformation is simply presented by a local translation of
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin vector field
\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime as shown in Eq.(
57
). It is manifest that under the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge transformation,
{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
becomes
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field strength and the field strength
\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
transforms as follows,

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)+{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)\varpi_{\mathbf{B}}(x).

(62)

Let us regard
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin vector field
\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
as
dimensionless hyper-gravivector field
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, so that the local translation can be represented via a local translation operator defined in formal by the following coordinate-like field derivative:

\displaystyle\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{A}}\equiv\frac{\delta}{\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}},

(63)

which is in analogous to the ordinary translation operator ‘
\partial_{\mathbf{M}}
’ of coordinates in globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime. Similarly, we can introduce the coordinate-like field displacement
\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. For convenience of mention, the displacement
\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}
is referred to as
dimensionless hyper-gravicoordinate displacement
and the derivative
\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{A}}
is called as
dimensionless hyper-gravicoordinate derivative
.

As the basic structure of biframe hyper-spacetime is described solely by the hyper-gravigauge field originated from the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field, it is natural to consider
biframe displacement correspondence
which states that the hyper-gravicoordinate displacement of hyper-gravivector field
\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime is directly associated to the ordinary coordinate displacement in globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime through the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field. Namely, when the locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime becomes a globally flat one, the hyper-gravicoordinate displacement
\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}
should be coincidental to the ordinary coordinate displacement. Based on such a biframe displacement correspondence and biframe hyper-spacetime structure, we are able to express the dimensionless hyper-gravicoordinate displacement
\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}
and derivative
\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{A}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)dx^{\mathbf{M}}=\phi(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)dx^{\mathbf{M}}\equiv\phi(x)\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)\partial_{\mathbf{M}}=\phi^{-1}(x)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)\partial_{\mathbf{M}}=\phi^{-1}(x)\eth_{\mathbf{A}},

(64)

with
\phi(x)
the graviscalar field. Where the dimensionless hyper-gravicoordinate displacement
\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}
and derivative
\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{A}}
with respect to hyper-gravivector field are related to the corresponding dimensionful vector field displacement
\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}
and derivative
\eth_{\mathbf{A}}
via the graviscalar field. They are all characterized by the hyper-gravigauge field which is regarded as bicovariant vector field defined in biframe hyper-spacetime.

Once locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime approaches to a globally flat one, we are led to the following special case:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}},\quad\phi(x)\to M_{\kappa},\quad\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}\to\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}dx^{\mathbf{M}}=dx^{\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}\to M_{\kappa}\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}=M_{\kappa}\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}dx^{\mathbf{M}},

(65)

where
M_{\kappa}
is supposed to be a
fundamental mass scale
. Correspondingly,
\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}
and
\eth_{\mathbf{A}}
are considered as dimensionful
hyper-gravicoordinate displacement and derivative
, respectively, in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime with the following forms:

\displaystyle\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}\equiv\phi^{-1}(x)\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)dx^{\mathbf{M}}=\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)dx^{\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\eth_{\mathbf{A}}\equiv\frac{\delta}{\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}}\equiv\phi(x)\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mathbf{M}}}=\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)\partial_{\mathbf{M}}.

IV
Hyperunified field theory for fundamental building block and fundamental interaction based on gauge invariance principle and scaling invariance hypothesis

Following along the maximum entangled qubit-spinor principle as guiding principle, we have shown in the previous paper as part I of the foundation of the hyperunified field theory
FHUFT-I
that the hyperunified qubit-spinor field is obtained as entangled hyperqubit-spinor field with least
q_{c}
-spin charge. The category-0 entangled enneaqubit-spinor field with
q_{c}
-spin charge
q_{c}=0
is regarded as the minimal hyperunified qubit-spinor field, and the category-1 entangled decaqubit-spinor field with
q_{c}
-spin charge
q_{c}=1
is demonstrated to be the hyperunified qubit-spinor field that can unify all known leptons and quarks
QK1
;
QK2
in SM. Based on the gauge invariance principle as guiding principle, we present in previous sections a general analysis that the fundamental interaction is governed by inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1). In particular, the gravitational interaction is shown to be governed by the translation-like
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin Abelian-type gauge symmetry which is the subgroup gauge symmetry of inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry. The hyper-gravigauge field is identified to be the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field in association with the graviscalar field. Therefore, we get a better understanding on the gravitational origin of gauge symmetry and genesis of gravitational interaction. In this section, we are going to build a whole hyperunified field theory for characterizing both fundamental building block and fundamental interaction based on the gauge invariance principle and scaling invariance hypothesis. Specifically, we will take the entangled decaqubit-spinor field as hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)
to be the fundamental building block of nature and meanwhile the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge interaction as hyperunified gauge interaction to be the fundamental interaction mediated via the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge field
\breve{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
in 19-dimensional hyper-spacetime. In addition, we will consider scaling gauge interaction and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar interaction as fundamental interactions mediated through the scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field
\phi_{1}(x)
.

IV.1
Hyperunified qubit-spinor structure of entangled decaqubit-spinor field as fundamental building block and the inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,18) as hyperunified symmetry in gravigauge hyper-spacetime

Let us begin with returning to the entangled decaqubit-spinor field in category-1 with
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin charge

and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge

to be the minimal entangled hyperqubit-spinor field that unifies all known leptons and quarks into a single hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)
. To explicitly reflect the basic properties of chiral type lepton-quark states and mirror lepton-quark states, it is useful to represent it in an alternative qubit-spinor structure as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\binom{\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}^{-}}(x)}{\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}^{+}}(x)}=\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}^{-}}(x)\\
\hat{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}^{-}}(x)\\

\hat{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}^{+}}(x),\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}^{-}}(x)\\

\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{4}}^{-}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}^{+}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{2}}^{+}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{3}}^{+}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{4}}^{+}}(x)\end{pmatrix},

(66)

where

is composed of two ultra-grand unified qubit-spinor fields
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}^{-}}(x)
and

with negative U-parity, which corresponds to four families of chiral type lepton-quark states, and

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}^{-}}(x)=\binom{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}^{-}}(x)}{\hat{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}^{-}}(x)}=\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}^{-}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{2}}^{-}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{3}}^{-}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{4}}^{-}}(x)\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}^{+}}(x)=\binom{\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}^{+}}(x)}{\hat{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}^{+}}(x)}=\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{1}}^{+}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{2}}^{+}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{3}}^{+}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{4}}^{+}}(x)\end{pmatrix}

(67)

where both

and

are regarded as
paired ultra-grand unified qubit-spinor field
with negative U-parity and positive U-parity, respectively.

\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{-}}(x)
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{+}}(x)
(
f=1,2,3,4
) represent four families of grand unified qubit-spinor fields with respective to chiral type lepton-quark states and mirror lepton-quark states, they are explicitly given as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{-}}(x)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{u\,L}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{d\,R\,\bar{c}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{d\,L}(x)\\
-\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{u\,R\,\bar{c}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{u\,R}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{d\,L\,\bar{c}}(x)\\
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{d\,R}(x)\\
-\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{u\,L\,\bar{c}}(x)\end{pmatrix},\quad\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{+}}(x)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{u\,R}(x)\\
\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{d\,L\,\bar{c}}(x)\\
\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{d\,R}(x)\\
-\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{u\,L\,\bar{c}}(x)\\
\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{u\,L}(x)\\
\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{d\,R\,\bar{c}}(x)\\
\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{d\,L}(x)\\
-\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{u\,R\,\bar{c}}(x)\end{pmatrix},

(68)

where
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{q\,L,R}(x)
and

(

with complex charge-conjugated ones

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{q\,L,R}(x)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}Q_{f}^{r}(x)\\
Q_{f}^{b}(x)\\
Q_{f}^{g}(x)\\

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{q\,\bar{c}\,L,R}(x)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}Q^{r\,\bar{c}}_{f}(x)\\
Q^{b\,\bar{c}}_{f}(x)\\
Q^{g\,\bar{c}}_{f}(x)\\
Q^{w\,\bar{c}}_{f}(x)\end{pmatrix}^{q}_{L,R},\quad\tilde{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{C}_{f}}^{4}}^{q\,\bar{c}\,L,R}(x)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{Q}^{r\,\bar{c}}_{f}(x)\\

Explicitly, each family of chiral type lepton-quark states in SM and mirror lepton-quark states beyond SM can be expressed by the so-called westward and eastward entangled hyperqubit-spinor fields

\displaystyle\Psi_{W_{f}}^{T}(x)\equiv\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{-}}^{T}(x)

\displaystyle\;\;(U_{f}^{r},U_{f}^{b},U_{f}^{g},U_{f}^{w},D^{r}_{fc},D^{b}_{fc},D^{g}_{fc},D^{w}_{fc},D_{f}^{r},D_{f}^{b},D_{f}^{g},D_{f}^{w},-U^{r}_{fc},-U^{b}_{fc},-U^{g}_{fc},-U^{w}_{fc})_{R}]^{T},

\displaystyle\;=[(\tilde{U}_{f}^{r},\tilde{U}_{f}^{b},\tilde{U}_{f}^{g},\tilde{U}_{f}^{w},\tilde{D}^{r}_{fc},\tilde{D}^{b}_{fc},\tilde{D}^{g}_{fc},\tilde{D}^{w}_{fc},\tilde{D}_{f}^{r},\tilde{D}_{f}^{b},\tilde{D}_{f}^{g},\tilde{D}_{f}^{w},-\tilde{U}^{r}_{fc},-\tilde{U}^{b}_{fc},-\tilde{U}^{g}_{fc},-\tilde{U}^{w}_{fc})_{R},

with

In such a hyperqubit-spinor structure, the kinetic action of hyperunified qubit-spinor field

and

Such a hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)
satisfies the following self-conjugated chiral conditions:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}^{c}(x)=\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}^{T}(x)=\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x),\quad\tilde{\gamma}_{21}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)=-\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x),

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}}=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes C_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}}}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes C_{D},

(74)

with
C_{D}=-i\sigma_{3}\times\sigma_{2}
defined as the usual charge conjugation matrix in four-dimensional spacetime.

IV.2
Scaling properties of biframe hyper-spacetime and fundamental fields with scaling gauge invariant hyperspin gravigauge field and field strength

To characterize the basic properties of fundamental fields and biframe hyper-spacetime formed from globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime and locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, we are going to assign
global and local scaling charges
for all fundamental fields and biframe hyper-spacetime bases.

The
global scaling charge

\mathcal{C}_{s}
is defined as the power of scaling factor
\lambda
under the following global conformal scaling transformation:

\displaystyle x^{\mathbf{M}}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}=\lambda^{-1}x^{\mathbf{M}},\quad\mathcal{C}_{s}=-1,

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)\to\mathbf{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x^{\prime})=\lambda^{\frac{D_{h}-1}{2}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x),\quad\mathcal{C}_{s}=\frac{D_{h}-1}{2},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x^{\prime})=\lambda{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x),\quad\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\to\mathsf{W}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x^{\prime})=\lambda\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x),\quad\mathcal{C}_{s}=1,

\displaystyle[d^{D_{h}}x]\to[d^{D_{h}}x^{\prime}]=\lambda^{-D_{h}}[d^{D_{h}}x],\quad\mathcal{C}_{s}=-D_{h}.

(75)

Similarly, the
local scaling charge

\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}
is defined as the power of scaling factor
\xi(x)
under the following local scaling gauge transformation:

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{{}^{\prime}\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)=\xi^{-1}(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x),\quad\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=-1,

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)\to\mathbf{\Psi}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)=\xi^{\frac{D_{h}-1}{2}}(x)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x),\quad\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=\frac{D_{h}-1}{2},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x),\quad\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\to\mathsf{W}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)=\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x),\quad\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=0,

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)\to\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{}^{\prime}\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)=\xi(x)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x),\quad\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=1,

\displaystyle\chi(x)\equiv\det\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\chi^{\prime}(x)=\xi^{-D_{h}}(x)\chi(x),\quad\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=-D_{h}.

(76)

The fields and operators with both global and local scaling charges are presented as follows:

\displaystyle\phi_{p}(x)\to\phi^{\prime}_{p}(x^{\prime})=\lambda\phi_{p}(x),\quad\phi_{p}(x)\to\phi^{\prime}_{p}(x)=\xi(x)\phi_{p}(x),\quad\mathcal{C}_{s}=1,\;\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=1,

\displaystyle\phi(x)\to\phi^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=\lambda\,\phi(x),\quad\phi(x)\to\phi^{\prime}(x)=\xi(x)\phi(x),\quad\mathcal{C}_{s}=1,\;\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=1,

\displaystyle\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}\equiv\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)dx^{\mathbf{M}}\to\delta\zeta^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}}=\xi^{-1}(x)\lambda^{-1}\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}},\quad\mathcal{C}_{s}=-1,\;\;\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=-1,

\displaystyle\eth_{\mathbf{A}}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\to\eth^{\prime}_{\mathbf{A}}=\xi(x)\lambda\,\eth_{\mathbf{A}},\quad\mathcal{C}_{s}=1,\;\;\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=1,

\displaystyle[\delta^{D_{h}}\zeta]\to[\delta^{D_{h}}\zeta^{\prime}]=\xi^{-D_{h}}(x)\lambda^{-D_{h}}[\delta^{D_{h}}\zeta],\quad\mathcal{C}_{s}=-D_{h},\;\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=-D_{h},

(77)

where
[\delta^{D_{h}}\zeta]\equiv[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\chi(x)
is regarded as the hyper-gravicoordinate integral measure in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

It is noticed that the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge field
\breve{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)+\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
and scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
are in general invariant under the local scaling transformation, which has zero local scaling charge
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=0
. Nevertheless, when the hyperspin gauge field is decomposed into the hyperspin gravigauge field and covariant-gauge field as shown in Eq.(
47
), the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
in Eq.(
49
) is no longer local scaling gauge invariant, so that the hyperspin covariant-gauge field
\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
is also not local scaling gauge invariant. It is appropriate to decompose the hyperspin gauge field into local scaling gauge invariant hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
and covariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)=\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x),

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{N}}{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{B}}-\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{N}}{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}-\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{Q}}{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\;\,\equiv\frac{1}{2}\left(\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{B}}-\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}-\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}\right),

(78)

where
{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
is local scaling gauge covariant field strength defined in Eq.(
III.3
) for the hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
, which is initiated from the local scaling invariant and
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
with the following relations:

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)\equiv\phi(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x),\quad\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)\equiv\phi^{-1}(x)\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x),

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\equiv\phi(x){\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x),

(79)

where
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}(x)
is viewed as
dual
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
. It becomes manifest that the field strength
\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
is both local scaling invariant and
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge invariant but no longer hyperspin gauge covariant. We have used the different letter style to distinguish
\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
from inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge covariant and local scaling invariant field strength
{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
defined in Eq.(
III.3
).

In terms of the local scaling invariant hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
and covariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
, their corresponding local scaling invariant field strengths are defined as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)\equiv\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)+{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x),

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\mathbf{D}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{D}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{C}},

(80)

It is easy to check that both
\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and
{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
become local scaling gauge invariant field strengths.

IV.3
Hyperunified field theory for fundamental building block and fundamental interaction based on gauge invariance principle and scaling invariance hypothesis

We now turn to build a whole hyperunified field theory for the category-1 entangled decaqubit-spinor field as hyperunified qubit-spinor field with inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) and global inhomogeneous Lorentz-type/Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,
D_{h}
-1) in
D_{h}=19
dimensional biframe hyper-spacetime.

Considering the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)
in 19-dimensional hyper-spacetime as fundamental building block which involves 512 independent degrees of freedom in the qubit-spinor representation of Hilbert space, and taking the inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,18) as gauge symmetry by following along the gauge invariance principle, we are led to introduce the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge field
\breve{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
which mediates the fundamental interaction. Such a gauge field concerns both hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
and
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
, which belong to the adjoint representation and vector representation of hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,18), respectively. The global and local scaling charges corresponding to global scaling symmetry SC(1) and scaling gauge symmetry SG(1) allow us to identify the graviscalar field
\phi(x)
and hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
from the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)\equiv\phi(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
as well as
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field
\phi_{1}(x)
relevant to the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin charge
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}=q_{c}=1
. In addition, the scaling gauge symmetry SG(1) brings on the scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
. For convenience, we should list all relevant fields as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x),\;{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x),\;\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x),\;\phi(x),\;\phi_{1}(x),\;\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}(x),

which are all basic fields concerned in establishing a whole hyperunified field theory. The gravitational origin of gauge symmetry indicates that the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry is governed by hyper-gravigauge field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
via the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
defined in Eq.(
49
) and also by
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
given in Eq.(
III.3
).

The gauge invariant and scaling invariant action for hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)
in category-1 can be read from Eq.(
28
) in 19-dimensional biframe hyper-spacetime. In constructing the action in Eq.(
28
), the scaling invariance for both global and local scaling symmetries plays a crucial role in addition to the gauge invariance principle. To construct the basic action for all fundamental fields, the scaling symmetry is still supposed to play an essential role. For that, we are motivated to make a
scaling invariance hypothesis
which postulates that the action is built to keep scaling invariance for both global and local scaling symmetries in addition to the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry by following along gauge invariance principle. Furthermore, it is supposed that the basic gauge field and scalar field also obey the simplest motion postulate that they all involve just the lowest quadratic derivative in the gauge invariant and scaling invariant action.

Following along the scaling invariance hypothesis and gauge invariance principle together with the maximum coherence motion principle and maximum entangled quibit-spinor principle, we obtain definitively the following basic action for a whole hyperunified field theory:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\chi(x)\,\{\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\phi_{1}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(81)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\phi^{D_{h}-4}[\,-\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}(\,\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\frac{1}{4}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\frac{1}{4}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}g_{W}^{-2}\frac{1}{4}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\,)

\displaystyle+
