\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\phi_{1}^{2}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}(\lambda_{S}^{2}D_{\mathbf{M}}\phi D_{\mathbf{N}}\phi+\lambda_{Q}^{2}D_{\mathbf{M}}\phi_{1}D_{\mathbf{N}}\phi_{1})-\lambda_{D}^{2}\phi_{1}^{4}\mathsf{F}(\frac{\phi_{1}}{\phi})\,]\,\},

with
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{M}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,D_{h}
and
D_{h}=19
. Where
g_{H}
and
g_{W}
are two gauge coupling constants,
\beta_{G}
,
\lambda_{S}
,
\lambda_{Q}
and
\lambda_{D}
are scalar coupling parameters and
\mathsf{F}(\frac{\phi_{1}}{\phi})
represents a general scaling invariant potential relevant to two scalar fields.
\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}
and
\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}
matrices are defined via the
\varGamma
-matrices
\varGamma^{\hat{\mathbf{A}}}=(\varGamma^{a},\varGamma^{A},\varGamma^{20}=\tilde{\varGamma})
(
\hat{\mathbf{A}}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,20
) presented in Eq.(
IV.1
). All definitions in the basic action are already given in previous relevant sections, we represent them as follows just for convenience:

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\equiv i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x),\quad{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}(x)\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{N}}{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{B}}-\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{N}}{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}-\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{Q}}{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}\right)

\displaystyle D_{\mathbf{M}}\phi=(\partial_{\mathbf{M}}+\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}})\phi,\quad D_{\mathbf{M}}\phi_{1}=(\partial_{\mathbf{M}}+\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}})\phi_{1}

(82)

for the covariant derivatives and gauge fields, and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}=\phi^{2}{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}=d_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}-d_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}},\quad d_{\mathbf{M}}\equiv\partial_{\mathbf{M}}+\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\ln\phi,

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}},

(83)

for the gauge covariant field strengths.

The constant tensor factors involved in the action are given by

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}},\quad\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime})}+3\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}]},

(84)

with the definitions,

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime})}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{2}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}+\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}})+\frac{1}{2}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}+\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}})\eta^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}]}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{2}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}-\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}})+\frac{1}{2}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}-\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}})\eta^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}.

(85)

The tensor factor
\tilde{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
may be called as
hypespin gauge invariance tensor
HUFT
as it brings the dynamic term of Abelian-type hyper-gravigauge field strength

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}},

(86)

to be gauge invariant under hyperspin gauge transformation although the Abelian-type hyper-gravigauge field strength
\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}
is not a gauge covariant one. The quadratic interaction term between gauge field and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field
\phi_{1}
is manifestly gauge invariant as it concerns the difference between hyperspin gauge field and hyperspin gravigauge field, i.e.,
({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})
, so that their inhomogeneous gauge transformations cancel each other. Note that such a quadratic term appears as a mass-like term of gauge field.

The action in Eq.(
81
) possesses the following
joint symmetry
:

\displaystyle G_{S}=SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,18)\Join WS(1,18)\rtimes SG(1),

(87)

where SC(1) and SG(1) represent the
global scaling symmetry
and
local scaling gauge symmetry
, respectively.
\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
in Eq.(
IV.3
) defines the field strength of scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}
. PO(1,18) remains global inhomogeneous Lorentz-type/Poincaré-type group symmetry and WS(1,18) represents inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry, which brings about a joint global and local symmetry in biframe hyper-spacetime. Only when locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime goes to be globally flat one, then the motion-correlation inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,18) becomes to be associated with inhomogeneous Lorentz-type/Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,18), so that the group transformations of SP(1,18) and SO(1,18) have to be coincidental, which is realized from their isomorphic property SP(1,18)
\cong
SO(1,18).

V
Hyperunified field theory in hidden scaling gauge formalism with gauge-gravity correspondence and the dynamics of fundamental fields with conserved currents

The action of hyperunified field theory presented in Eq.(
81
) is fully built based on the gauge invariance principle and scaling invariance hypothesis, which enables us to demonstrate the gauge-gravity correspondence. As such a hyperunified field theory is formulated in biframe hyper-spacetime, we are able to derive equations of motion for all fundamental fields by following along the least action principle and obtain various conserved currents with respect to gauge symmetries.

V.1
Hyperunified field theory in hidden scaling gauge formalism with gauge-gravity correspondence and the kinetic motion of fundamental fields as the source of gravitational interaction

It is noticed from the action in Eq.(
81
) that the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge field
\check{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
does not interact with the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
via the gauge covariant derivative as like the usual case just following along the gauge invariance principle, this is because the group generators of
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge symmetry get the nilpotent condition
(\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}})^{2}=0
. In another word, the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge interaction via the gauge covariant derivative becomes vanishing, the reason is that such an interaction term brings on the change of chirality for the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
which is an entangled hyperqubit-spinor field with self-conjugated chiral structure. Instead, the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge field gets to interact with the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
through coupling the dual
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\equiv\phi^{-1}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
to the covariant derivative operator
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}
as kinetic term of hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
, i.e.:

\displaystyle\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}\varSigma_{-\mathbf{A}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}},

(88)

where we have introduced the scaling gauge invariant dual tensors,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}=\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{B}},\quad\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}=\eta_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\equiv\phi^{-2}\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}=\eta^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\mathbf{N}},\quad\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}=\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\equiv\phi^{2}\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}.

(89)

Geometrically, the dual tensors
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
(
\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
) and
\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
(
\hat{\chi}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
) are referred to as dual
hyper-gravimetric tensor fields
.

In the whole hyperunified field theory presented in Eq.(
81
), the bicovariant vector field
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
corresponding to the dual
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\equiv\phi\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
couples to the covariant derivative operator
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}
, which arises not only from supplying both the local hyperspin gauge symmetry and global Lorentz-type group symmetry, but also from preserving the local scaling gauge symmetry. Therefore, the local scaling gauge invariance plays a crucial role in the building of a whole hyperunified field theory.

The gauge symmetry often involves redundant degrees of freedom, which demands to make a gauge prescription with setting up gauge fixing condition. In order to eliminate the redundant degree of freedom arising from local scaling gauge symmetry, let us rewrite the action given in Eq.(
81
) into the following formalism:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\mathfrak{A}(x)\,\mathfrak{L}

(90)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\mathfrak{A}(x)\,\{\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}[\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}-\tilde{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}+\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}g_{W}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\},

with the definitions,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}(x)\equiv\det\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}=\phi^{D_{h}}\det\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}=\phi^{D_{h}}(x)\chi(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{A}}(x)\equiv\det\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}=\phi^{-D_{h}}\det\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}=\phi^{-D_{h}}(x)\hat{\chi}(x)=1/\mathfrak{A}(x),

(91)

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}\equiv\phi(x){\mathbf{G}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}={\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}-{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}}=\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{B}}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{B}},

(92)

where
{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}
defines gauge covariant field strength under hyperspin gauge transformations, which is also local scaling gauge invariant field strength.

We have also made the following definitions:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\to\phi^{\frac{D_{h}-1}{2}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\to\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\ln(\lambda_{S}^{2}\phi^{2}+\lambda_{Q}^{2}\phi_{1}^{2}),

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{S}^{2}}{1+\frac{\lambda_{Q}^{2}\phi_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{S}^{2}\phi^{2}}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\frac{\lambda_{Q}\phi_{1}}{\lambda_{S}\phi})\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\frac{\lambda_{Q}\phi_{1}}{\lambda_{S}\phi})\equiv\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s}(x)\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}(x),

(93)

where the explicit relation between the field
\chi_{s}(x)
and the ratio
\phi_{1}(x)/\phi(x)
is obtained as follows:

\displaystyle\chi_{s}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sinh^{-1}\frac{\lambda_{Q}\phi_{1}(x)}{\lambda_{S}\phi(x)},

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{1}(x)}{\phi(x)}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}(x),\quad\beta_{Q}\equiv\frac{\lambda_{S}}{\lambda_{Q}}.

(94)

The dimensionless field
\chi_{s}(x)
may be referred to as scaling invariant
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
which has zero global and local scaling charges.
{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})
is considered to be a general potential of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field.

The tensor factor
\bar{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}(\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}+\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime})}+\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}]}),

(95)

with the tensors
\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
,
\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime})}
and
\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}]}
given in Eqs.(
IV.3
) and (
IV.3
). Such a constant tensor
\bar{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
couples to the gauge covariant dynamic term of hyper-gravigauge field to reproduce the gauge invariant quadratic interaction term concerning the difference between the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
, i.e.:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\bar{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}).

It will be seen below that the gauge covariant dynamic formalism of hyper-gravigauge field becomes more convenient in deriving the equation of motion of hyper-gravigauge field.

In the action Eq.(
90
), the scaling gauge symmetry becomes a hidden symmetry. Namely, all basic fields have zero local scaling charge
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=0
. In addition, the redefined hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\chi_{s}(x)
get zero global scaling charge
\mathcal{C}_{s}=0
. We may refer to the action in Eq.(
90
) as
hyperunified field theory in hidden scaling gauge formalism
.

It is noticed that the graviscalar field
\phi(x)
does not appear explicitly in the action presented in Eq.(
90
). We arrive at five fundamental fields which include the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
, the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
, the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
, the scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}
and the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\chi_{s}(x)
. Nevertheless, the independent degrees of freedom should be unchanged as the graviscalar field is considered to be absorbed into the scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}
, which appears to be a
massive-like gauge field
.

In such a hidden scaling gauge formalism of hyperunified field theory, the dual
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
couples to either the coordinate derivative operator
\partial_{\mathbf{M}}
that reflects the motion of fundamental fields or the fundamental gauge field that characterizes the interaction of fundamental fields. So that the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
as hyper-gravigauge field with the field strength
\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}
describes the fundamental gravitational interaction for all motional fields and vector fields in Minkowski hyper-spacetime.

Therefore, we come to the statement that the kinetic motion of fundamental fields emerges as the source of gravitational interaction which is truly characterized by the gauge interaction. As a consequence, the fundamental gravitational interaction exists in all motional fields and a whole hyperunified field theory in hidden scaling gauge formalism reveals the
gauge-gravity correspondence
.

V.2
Equations of motion and dynamics of fundamental fields

The action in Eq.(
90
) for the hyperunified field theory enables us to derive equations of motion for all fundamental fields from the least action principle, which can be applied to describe the dynamics of fundamental fields and study gravitational relativistic quantum theory in hyper-spacetime.

Let us first present the equation of motion for the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
,

\displaystyle\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}i\left({\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}=0,

(96)

where
{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}
is regarded as
graviscaling induced gauge field
defined as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}),

(97)

which is local scaling gauge invariant. Where
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}})
concerns the covariant derivative for the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)\equiv\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x)+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\,\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{B}}(x)\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}}(x).

(98)

The quadratic form for the above equation of motion is found to be.

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}})({\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{N}})\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

\displaystyle\;\;=-\left(\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}+\varGamma^{\mathbf{A}}\tilde{\varGamma}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\beta_{Q}\cosh\chi_{s}\,(i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s})\,\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

\displaystyle\;\;+\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}-{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}i({\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{P}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{P}})-i{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\,\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}},

(99)

with the definition for the covariant derivative,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}({\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{N}})\equiv{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}({\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{N}})-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}({\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{P}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{P}}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}(\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\,\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}\,).

(100)

Where
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
is the field strength of hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
and
{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{C}}
the field strength of
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}(x)
presented in Eq.(
III.3
). The graviscaling induced gauge field strength
{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{M}}.

(101)

We now turn to the equation of motion for the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
, which is found to be,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\left(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right)={\mathcal{J}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{M}},

(102)

where
{\mathcal{J}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
is bicovariant tensor current with the following explicit form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}=\mathfrak{A}\,\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\{\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}\;\;\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}+\mathfrak{A}\,\frac{1}{2}\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}.

(103)

We have introduced the tensor
\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
defined as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{B}}-\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\,\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\,),

\displaystyle\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\bar{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}.

(104)

Let us now derive the equation of motion for the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
as hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}=\phi\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
. The explicit form is found to be as follows:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\left(\mathfrak{A}\,\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\right)+{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}})={\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}},

(105)

with
{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
the bicovariant vector current given by,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\mathfrak{L}+\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\{\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(106)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{B}\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}+\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}+\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle g_{W}^{-2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}+\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}\,\},

where the tensors
\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{B}\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}
and
\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{B}\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{2}(\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{B}\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}-\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{B}\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{B}\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\hat{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\,\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\,),

\displaystyle\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\tilde{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}},

(107)

and the tensor
\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
is given in Eq.(
V.2
).

It is straightforward to obtain the equation of motion for the scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\left(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}g_{W}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\right)={\mathsf{J}}^{\mathbf{M}},

(108)

with the vector current

\displaystyle{\mathsf{J}}^{\mathbf{M}}=-\mathfrak{A}\,\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}.

(109)

It is easy to write down the equation of motion for the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
,

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s})={\mathsf{J}}_{s},

(110)

with the scalar current,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{J}}_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{A}\,\beta_{Q}\cosh\chi_{s}\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}+\mathfrak{A}\,\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh\chi_{s}\cosh\chi_{s}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}

(111)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}\,\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\bar{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh\chi_{s}\cosh\chi_{s}\,{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{A}\,\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\chi_{s}).

with

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\chi_{s})\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial\chi_{s}}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s}).

It is noticed that all equations of motion for five fundamental fields are global scaling invariant.

V.3
Conserved currents in correspondence to gauge symmetries in hyperunified field theory

From Noether’s theorem
NT
that for every differentiable symmetry generated by the local action, there exists the corresponding conserved current. We are going to show the conserved currents generated by the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,18) and the scaling gauge symmetry SG(1).

The bicovariant tensor current
{\mathcal{J}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
generated from the hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) is a conserved current with respect to the covariant derivative,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{J}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=0,

(112)

which can be verified from the equation of motion of hyperspin gauge field,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle\quad=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}){\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}){\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle\quad+\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}){\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle\quad=\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}}-{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}})\eta_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}=0.

In obtaining the above identity, the symmetric and antisymmetric properties of the tensor and gauge field strength have been used to bring the cancellation.

From the conserved current of bicovariant tensor current
{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
presented in Eq.(
112
), we are able to derive the following dynamical equation:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=-{\mathcal{J}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]},

(113)

for the bicovariant tensor current
{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and antisymmetric tensor current
{\mathcal{J}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}
with the following definitions:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\mathfrak{A}\,\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\{\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}\;\;\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\equiv\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-\mathfrak{A}\,\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\eta_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{C}},

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}=\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}-\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}},\quad\eta_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}=\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}-\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{B}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\equiv{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\left(\mathfrak{A}\,\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\right).

(114)

where
{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
is regarded as
bicovariant hyperspin angular momentum tensor
.

It is noticed that the dynamics of bicovariant hyperspin angular momentum tensor
{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
is governed by the antisymmetric tensor current
{\mathcal{J}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}
. Such a tensor current is correlated to the hyper-gravigauge field strength and bicovariant vector current
{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
which determines the dynamics of hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
.

The dynamical equation presented in Eq.(
113
) may be referred to as
dynamic evolution equation
for the hyperspin angular momentum tensor. The bicovariant vector current
\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
provides the source for the equation of motion of hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}})=\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}.

(115)

Note that neither
{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
or
\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
is conserved.

It is useful to define the following alternative bicovariant vector current:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\equiv{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}-{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}),

(116)

so that the equation of motion presented in Eq.(
105
) for the hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
can be rewritten into the following simple form:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\left(\mathfrak{A}\,\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\right)={\mathsf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}.

(117)

It can be verified that the bicovariant vector current
{\mathsf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
is conserved due to the following identity:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathsf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\left(\mathfrak{A}\,\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\right)=0,

(118)

which is attributed to the antisymmetric property of the tensor factor
\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
. Such a conserved bicovariant vector current reflects the translation-like
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin Abelian-type gauge symmetry.

The equations of motion shown in Eqs.(
115
) and (
117
) enable us to obtain the following dynamical equations for both bicovariant vector currents
\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
and
{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}=-\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{A}\,\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}=-\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}).

(119)

For convenience of mention,
{\mathsf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
is regarded as
conserved bicovariant vector current
of hyper-gravigauge field,
{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
as
total bicovariant vector current
and
\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
as
twisting bicovariant vector current
which governs the bicovariant hyperspin angular momentum tensor.

The local scaling gauge symmetry leads to the conserved vector current as follows:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathsf{J}}^{\mathbf{M}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}})\equiv 0,

(120)

which provides the following condition for the scaling gauge field when applying it to the explicit form of the current in Eq.(
108
),

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\left(\mathfrak{A}\,(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}\right)=0.

(121)

VI
Conservation law and dynamic evolution equation from global symmetry of Minkowski hyper-spacetime in hyperunified field theory

The hyperunified field theory is formulated in globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime which is regarded as
free-motion hyper-spacetime

\mathbf{M}_{h}
, the action in Eq.(
90
) possesses the global Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,18) and global scaling symmetry SC(1). In the absence of gravitational interaction, it is well known that the global Poincaré symmetry leads to the conservation laws for both energy momentum tensor and total angular momentum tensor, which follows Noether’s theorem
NT
that every differentiable symmetry of the action has the corresponding conservation law. We should apply Noether’s theorem to hyperunified field theory in the presence of gravitational interaction and derive the conservation laws and dynamic evolution equations arising from global Poincaré-type group symmetry and scaling symmetry of Minkowski hyper-spacetime.

VI.1
Conservation law of hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor from translation group symmetry of Minkowski hyper-spacetime in hyperunified field theory

Let us first derive the conservation law for global translation group symmetry. Consider the global translation group transformation of coordinates in globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime as follows:

\displaystyle x^{\mathbf{M}}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}=x^{\mathbf{M}}+a^{\mathbf{M}},

with
a^{\mathbf{M}}
an arbitrary constant vector. When applying for the variational principle, the invariance of the action under such a translation leads to the following relation:

\displaystyle\Delta{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}=\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\partial_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{P}})a^{\mathbf{M}}=0.

(122)

which results in the following conservation law:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{P}}=0,

(123)

where
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{P}}
is referred to as
bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
in hyper-spacetime. From the action of hyperunified field theory given in Eq.(
90
), we obtain the following explicit form for the bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{P}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{A}\,\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}\mathfrak{L}+\mathfrak{A}\,\{\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{A}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(124)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}+\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}+\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle g_{W}^{-2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}+\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\,\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}}\,\},

which is gauge invariant. In deriving such a gauge-invariant formalism, the equations of motion for the fundamental fields
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
,
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
,
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}
and
\chi_{s}
have been used and the surface terms have been ignored.

It is interesting to notice the following relation between the bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor and bicovariant vector current:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\equiv\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}},

(125)

which indicates that the conserved bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}
generated from the global translation group symmetry of coordinates in globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime correlates to the bicovariant vector current
{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}
which governs the dynamics of
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
resulted from the local translation-like
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin group symmetry of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

Therefore, we arrive at the statement that for the hyperunified field theory built based on the gauge invariance principle and scaling invariance hypothesis in light of biframe hyper-spacetime, the gauge-invariant bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{N}}
derived from the translation group symmetry of Minkowski hyper-spacetime is a conserved tensor even in the presence of gravitational gauge interaction.

VI.2
Dynamic evolution equations of hyper-rotation angular momentum tensor from Lorentz-type group symmetry and hyper-conformal scaling momentum tensor from conformal scaling symmetry in hyperunified field theory

We now consider the global Lorentz-type group symmetry in Minkowski hyper-spacetime with the following infinitesimal group transformation of coordinates:

\displaystyle x^{\prime\mathbf{M}}=x^{\mathbf{M}}+\delta L^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\mathbf{N}}\,x^{\mathbf{N}}.

Based on the principle of least action, the invariance of the action under such a transformation leads to the following
dynamic evolution equation
:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\mathcal{T}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]},

(126)

with
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
and
\mathcal{T}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
given explicitly as follows,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\;\,\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\eta^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}x^{\mathbf{N}}-\mathcal{T}^{\;\,\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\eta^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}}x^{\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\;\,\mathbf{N}}-\eta^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}},

(127)

where
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
defines
bicovariant hyper-rotation angular momentum tensor
in association with the bicovariant hyper-stress energy momentum tensor in Minkowski hyper-spacetime. As the antisymmetric part of bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor is not necessary to vanish in the hyperunified field theory due to the presence of antisymmetric hyper-gravigauge field, the covariant hyper-rotation angular momentum tensor obeys a dynamic evolution equation presented in Eq.(
126
) rather than a homogeneously conserved law.

It is useful to express the antisymmetric bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\mathcal{T}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
into the following two parts:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\equiv\mathcal{T}_{a}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}+\mathcal{T}_{s}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{a}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\equiv\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{[\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}]},\quad\mathcal{T}_{s}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\equiv\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{(\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime})},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}-\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}},\quad\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N})}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}+\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\eta_{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}-\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\eta_{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}),

(128)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\equiv{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}},

(129)

which presents an alternative tensor
\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
defined either from the bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}
raised with the tensor
\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}
or from the total bicovariant vector current
{\mathbf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}
projected by the dual
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
.

To distinguish the tensor
\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
from the bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}
, we may refer to
\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
as
covariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
, which can be discriminated from their global scaling charges as
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
have global scaling charges
\mathcal{C}_{s}=D_{h}
and
\mathcal{C}_{s}=D_{h}-2
, respectively. It is useful to decompose the covariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
into antisymmetric part
\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{[\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}]}
and symmetric part
\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{(\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime})}
, which brings the antisymmetric bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\mathcal{T}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
into two parts
\mathcal{T}_{a}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
and
\mathcal{T}_{s}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
.
\mathcal{T}_{a}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
is the antisymmetric bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor associated with antisymmetric covariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
, and
\mathcal{T}_{s}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
is the antisymmetric bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor associated with symmetric covariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N})}
.

In general, the dynamic evolution equation for the bicovariant hyper-rotation angular momentum tensor
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
can be rewritten as follows,

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\mathcal{T}_{a}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}+\mathcal{T}_{s}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]},

(130)

which is governed by two kinds of sources
\mathcal{T}_{a}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
and
\mathcal{T}_{s}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
. It is straightforward to
show that when turning locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime into globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime, the source part
\mathcal{T}_{s}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
goes to be vanishing, i.e.:

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\propto\eta_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}},\quad\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\propto\eta_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}=-\eta_{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{s}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\propto\eta_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\hat{\mathbf{T}}^{(\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime})}=-\mathcal{T}_{s}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\to 0.

(131)

Let us turn to the global conformal scaling symmetry. The invariance of the action leads to the following dynamical equation:

\displaystyle\left(x^{\mathbf{M}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mathbf{M}}}+D_{h}\right)(\mathfrak{A}\,\mathfrak{L})+\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{M}}-\mathcal{T}=0,

(132)

with
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{M}}
and
\mathcal{T}
defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{M}}\equiv\mathcal{T}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{N}}\,x^{\mathbf{N}}\,,\qquad\mathcal{T}\equiv\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{N}}\eta_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}},

(133)

where
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{M}}
is regarded as
hyper-conformal scaling momentum tensor
and
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
is the trace of bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor.

From the property that the integral
\int[d\hat{x}]\lambda^{D_{h}}\,\chi(\lambda x)\,\mathfrak{L}(\lambda x)
is actually independent of
\lambda
, and by taking the differentiation with respect to
\lambda
at
\lambda=1
, we obtain the following identity:

\displaystyle\int[d\hat{x}](x^{\mathbf{M}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mathbf{M}}}+D_{h})(\mathfrak{A}\,\mathfrak{L})=0,

which leads the dynamical equation in Eq.(
132
) to be simplified as follows:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{M}}=\mathcal{T}.

(134)

Such an equation brings on
dynamic evolution equation
for the global conformal scaling symmetry in Minkowski hyper-spacetime. Only when the hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor becomes traceless
\mathcal{T}=0
, the tensor vector becomes conserved, i.e.,
\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{M}}=0
. In general, the existence of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\chi_{s}(x)
and its coupling to the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)
gives a nonzero tensor scalar
\mathcal{T}\neq 0
in hyperunified field theory.

Therefore, the dynamic evolution equation for the bicovariant hyper-rotation angular momentum tensor
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
is in general governed by the antisymmetric bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\mathcal{T}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
, and the dynamic evolution equation for the hyper-conformal scaling momentum tensor
\mathcal{T}^{\mathbf{M}}
is determined by the trace of bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\mathcal{T}
.

VI.3
Dynamic evolution equation for the total bicovariant hyper-angular momentum tensor in hyperunified field theory

In globally flat four-dimensional Minkowski spacetime with the absence of gravitational interaction, the total angular momentum tensor as the sum of rotation and spin angular momentum tensors is known to be a conserved tensor. In the hyperunified field theory, the bicovariant hyper-rotation angular momentum tensor and bicovariant hyperspin angular momentum tensor are shown to obey two independent dynamic evolution equations with respective to global and local symmetries in globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime and locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, respectively. To verify the total hyper-angular momentum conservation law when turning locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime to be globally flat one, it is useful to introduce the total bicovariant hyper-angular momentum tensor in a hidden locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

Let us first make a redefinition for the bicovariant hyperspin angular momentum tensor
{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{P}}_{\;\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
. It is appropriate to project
{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{P}}_{\;\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
into the following tensor form via the dual
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{P}}_{\;\;\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}

(135)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}\,\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\{\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}\;\;\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]},

with
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
defined in Eq.(
VI.2
). In terms of the above tensor form, we are able from the dynamical equation in Eq.(
113
) to derive the following dynamic evolution equation for the bicovariant hyperspin angular momentum tensor:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathcal{T}_{a}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}-\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{S}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}+\mathcal{T}_{\chi_{s}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}+\mathcal{T}_{c}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]},

(136)

with
\mathcal{T}_{a}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
defined in Eq.(
VI.2
). Where we have introduced the following definitions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{S}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\equiv\mathsf{H}_{\;\,\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime\prime}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbf{N}^{\prime\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{S}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}},\quad\hat{\mathbf{S}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\equiv{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{\;\,\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime\prime}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime\prime}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}[\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime\prime}}(\eta_{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{M}}\eta^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime\prime}}-\eta_{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{N}}\eta^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime\prime}})-(\mathsf{H}_{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{M}}\eta_{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbf{N}}-\mathsf{H}_{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{N}}\eta_{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbf{M}})\eta_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime\prime}}],

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbf{N}^{\prime\prime}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime\prime}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime\prime}}(\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}),

(137)

for the antisymmetric bicovariant tensor
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{S}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
which arises from the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge interaction of hyperunified qubit-spinor field, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\chi_{s}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\equiv\bar{\eta}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\frac{1}{4}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\eta_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

(138)

for the antisymmetric bicovariant tensor
\mathcal{T}_{\chi_{s}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
which comes from the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field interacting with the hyperspin gauge field, as well as

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{c}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\equiv\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\hat{\mathsf{T}}^{[\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}]},\quad\hat{\mathsf{T}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}={\mathsf{J}}_{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}-\hat{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}{\mathsf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}),

(139)

for the antisymmetric bicovariant tensor
\mathcal{T}_{c}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
which results from the antisymmetric covariant tensor
\hat{\mathsf{T}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
. Where
\hat{\mathsf{T}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
is defined by the conserved bicovariant vector current
{\mathsf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
with
\hat{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}{\mathsf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}
.

In terms of the bicovariant hyper-rotation and hyperspin angular momentum tensors
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
, we are able to define the
total bicovariant hyper-angular momentum tensor
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}+{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}},

(140)

which satisfies the following dynamic evolution equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{s}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}-\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{S}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}+\mathcal{T}_{\chi_{s}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}+\mathcal{T}_{c}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]},

(141)

which shows that the total hyper-angular momentum tensor is no longer homogeneously conserved due to the presence of gauge invariant gravitational interaction in hidden locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. It is noticed that the source term of antisymmetric bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\mathcal{T}_{a}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
in both
{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
cancels each other due to the sign flip in their dynamic evolution equations.

To verify the conservation law of total bicovariant hyper-angular momentum tensor in globally flat Mincowski hyper-spacetime with the absence of gravitational interaction, let us turn the local inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry into global symmetry by taking gauge fields to approach the following limiting case:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\to 0,\quad\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\to M_{\kappa}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}},

(142)

which leads to the following relations:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{s}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]},\;\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{S}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]},\;\mathcal{T}_{\chi_{s}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]},\;\mathcal{T}_{c}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\to 0,

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=-\mathcal{T}_{a}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]},

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=+\mathcal{T}_{a}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}.

(143)

As a consequence, we arrive at the following equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{P}}{\cal J}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{P}}({\mathcal{L}}^{\mathbf{P}.\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}+{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathbf{P},\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}})=0,

(144)

which does verify the conservation law of total bicovariant hyper-angular momentum tensor in globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime.

Therefore, we come to the observation that for the hyperunified qubit-spinor field as fundamental building block with inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge interaction as fundamental interaction, its covariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor is generally asymmetric. Neither the bicovariant hyper-rotation angular momentum tensor nor the bicovariant hyperspin angular momentum tensor is homogeneously conserved due to the antisymmetric property of the covariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor in hyperunified field theory, they all get dynamic evolution equations which are governed by the antisymmetric bicovariant hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor. Only in the absence of inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge interaction with turning locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime to globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime, the total hyper-angular momentum tensor becomes homogeneously conserved due to the appearance of cancellation, where the bicovariant hyper-rotation angular momentum tensor and hyperspin angular momentum tensor are governed by the same antisymmetric hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor but with an opposite sign.

VII
Hyperunified field theory in hidden gauge formalism with gravity-geometry correspondence and Einstein-Hilbert type action with appearance of Riemann geometry and emergent group symmetry GL(D
h
,R) as generalization of gauge invarance principle under flowing unitary gauge

The basic action of hyperunified field theory presented in Eq.(
90
) is built based on the gauge invariance principle and scaling invariance hypothesis with inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry and global Poincaré-type group symmetry. The
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field as hyper-gravigauge field is taken as a projection operator to define the bicovariant hyperspin angular momentum tensor given in Eq. (
135
). In this section, we are going to define a hyper-spacetime gauge field from the hyperspin gauge field and present an equivalent action of hyperunified field theory in a hidden gauge formalism. The hyper-gravigauge field as projection operator is shown to play an essential role as Goldstone-like boson. Such a hidden gauge formalism of the action enables us to investigate more profound correlations between gravitational interaction and Riemann geometry of hyper-spacetime. We will demonstrate how the dynamics of hyper-gravigauge field can equivalently be characterized by the dynamics of emergent Riemann geometry in light of Einstein-Hilbert type action, which is governed by an emergent general linear group symmetry GL(
D_{h}
, R) and regarded as the generalization of gauge invariance principle. So that we are able to corroborate the gravity-geometry correspondence in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime.

VII.1
Hyper-gravigauge field as Goldstone-like boson and the hyper-spacetime gauge field with gauge covariant field strength in hidden gauge formalism

The
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
as hyper-gravigauge field is a bicovariant vector field spanned in biframe hyper-spacetime, which allows us to project the tensors in the representation of locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime into those in the representation of globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime.

Let us firstly define a hidden gauge invariant field from the hyperspin gauge field by applying for the hyper-gravigauge field as projection operator, i.e.:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}},

(145)

which already appears in the covariant derivative when deriving the quadratic form of equation of motion given in Eq.(
V.2
) for the hyperunified qubit-spinor field, we may refer to such a gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}
as
hyper-spacetime gauge field
.

As the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
exhibits gravitational origin of gauge symmetry by decomposing it into the sum of hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and covariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
presented in Eqs.(
IV.2
)-(
IV.2
), we should also decompose the hyper-spacetime gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}
into the following two parts in an analogous way:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}\equiv\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}},

(146)

with the explicit forms,

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{L}}(\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{L}\mathbf{Q}}+\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{L}\mathbf{M}}-\partial_{\mathbf{L}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}})=\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}},

(147)

which are all local scaling gauge invariant.

The hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
may be regarded as
Goldstone-like boson field
which transforms as bicovariant vector field under hyperspin gauge group and Lorentz-type group transformations. In light of the hyper-spacetime gauge field and field strength, the hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) is transmuted into a hidden gauge symmetry via the Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
.

It is noticed that the symmetric tensor fields
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
and
\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
defined in Eq.(
V.1
) are presented as the product of two hyper-gravigauge fields by contracting the vector indices in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. Such symmetric tensor fields are considered as composite fields with both hidden hyperspin gauge symmetry and scaling gauge symmetry, which are referred to as Goldstone-like dual
hyper-gravimetric fields
geometrically.

The gauge field
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}
is a symmetric gauge field
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}=\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}}
which is completely determined by the dual Goldstone-like hyper-gravimetric fields
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
and
\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
. We may refer to such a gauge field as scaling gauge invariant
hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field
. For convenience of mention, the gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}
is called as
hyper-spacetime covariant-gauge field
.

From the hyper-spacetime gauge field, we are able to define the corresponding field strength as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}=\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}-\nabla_{\mathbf{N}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{L}}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{L}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{L}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{P}}+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{L}},

(148)

which can also be decomposed into the following two parts:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}\equiv\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}+{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{L}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{L}},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}=\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}-\nabla_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{L}}-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{L}},
