\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{P}}+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{L}}.

(149)

Where the tensor fields
\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}
and
{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}
defined in the hidden gauge formalism are referred to as scaling gauge invariant
hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field strength
and
hyper-spacetime covariant-gauge field strength
, respectively.

In obtaining the above field strengths in a hidden gauge formalism, we have used the following identities:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\,\mathbf{P}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\,\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\,\mathbf{P}}+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}=0,

(150)

which enable us to verify the following relations:

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}={\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}}={\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}}={\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}}=-\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}=\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}}=\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}}=\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}}=-\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}},

\displaystyle{\hat{{\mathbf{F}}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}={\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}}={\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}}={\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}}=-{\hat{{\mathbf{F}}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{Q}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}.

(151)

It is seen that the scaling gauge invariant hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field strength
\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}
is described by the scaling gauge invariant Goldstone-like hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
, which defines scaling gauge invariant
Riemann-type curvature tensor
.
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
is regarded as a basic field for hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge interaction in the hidden gauge formalism.

VII.2
Hyperunified field theory in hidden gauge formalism and Einstein-Hilbert type action on gravitational interaction of hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field

To formulate the basic action of hyperunified field theory in the hidden gauge formalism, it is useful to corroborate the following identity:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{A}\,\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}+2\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{A}})=\mathfrak{A}\,\mathbf{R},

(152)

where the second term on the left-hand side is a total derivative. The scalar tensor
\mathbf{R}
on the right-hand side defines scaling gauge invariant
Ricci-type scalar curvature
in hyper-spacetime. It is useful to notice the following general relations for the hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{P}}=\eta_{\;\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\ln\mathfrak{A},\quad\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}=-\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}).

(153)

In general, the scaling gauge invariant
Ricci-type curvature tensor
can uniquely be resulted from contracting the scaling gauge invariant Riemann-type curvature tensor,

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}\eta^{\mathbf{Q}}_{\;\;\mathbf{P}}=-\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}\eta^{\mathbf{Q}}_{\;\;\mathbf{P}}=-\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}},

(154)

which is a symmetric tensor with the following explicit form:

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}}=\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\ln\mathfrak{A}-\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{L}}.

(155)

The scaling gauge invariant Ricci-type scalar curvature in hyper-spacetime is obtained from contracting the Ricci-type curvature tensor,

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}=\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}}=-\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}}.

(156)

It is interesting to notice the following identity:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}})({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}}),

(157)

which brings on the quadratic interaction of hyper-spacetime gauge field.

Applying the identities and relations in Eqs.(
VII.1
)-(
157
), we are able to rewrite the basic action in Eq.(
90
) into the following form formulated in the hidden gauge formalism:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\mathfrak{A}(x)\,\mathfrak{L}=\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\mathfrak{A}(x)\,

(158)

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\{\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{-}^{\mathbf{M}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}}+g_{H}^{-2}\mathbf{R}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}})({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\},

where we have ignored the surface term
2\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{A}})
. The following definitions in the hidden gauge formalism have implicitly adopted:

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\equiv i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}+(\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}+{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]})\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}})

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\;\;\equiv\frac{1}{3}(\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}}\,\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{C}}+\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}}\,\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}+\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}}\,\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{C}}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\equiv\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}},\quad{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\equiv\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{-}^{\mathbf{M}}\equiv\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathbf{M}}\varGamma_{-},\quad\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mathbf{M}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\varGamma^{\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{Q}}\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\frac{i}{4}[\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mathbf{P}},\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mathbf{Q}}],

(159)

where
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}
are total antisymmetric fields since the hyperunified qubit-spinor field couples only to totally antisymmetric gauge fields due to its self-conjugated chiral property.
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}
is referred to as
hyper-spacetime gravigauge field
which characterizes the gravitational origin of gauge symmetry. It is noticed that the above defined
\varGamma
-matrices are no longer constant matrices, the matrices
\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mathbf{M}}
are regarded as
hyper-gravigauge
\Gamma
-matrices
and
\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}
together with
\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{-}^{\mathbf{M}}
are viewed as
hyper-gravigauge inhomogeneous hyperspin group generators
.

In the action shown in Eq.(
158
), the Ricci-type scalar curvature
\mathbf{R}
brings about the scaling gauge invariant
Einstein-Hilbert type action
which describes the gravitational interaction as dynamics of scaling gauge invariant hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
.

VII.3
Hyperunified field theory in hidden gauge formalism with emergent general linear group symmetry GL(
D_{h}
,R) as generalization of gauge invariance principle and the appearance of Riemann geometry in curved hyper-spacetime

The action in hidden gauge formalism shown in Eq.(
158
) is obtained from the action in Eq.(
90
) via projecting locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime into globally flat free-motion hyper-spacetime
\mathbf{M}_{h}
by utilizing the hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
(
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}(x)
) as Goldstone-like boson. The action in Eq.(
90
) is built based on the gauge invariance principle and scaling invariance hypothesis within the framework of quantum field theory, so that it possesses global Poincaré-type group symmetry. In general, the laws of nature should be independent of the choice of coordinate systems, which indicates that the action should hold in any frame of coordinates. To explicitly verify that, let us turn to the geometric analysis.

Geometrically, when considering the hyperspin gauge and scaling gauge invariant tensor field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
as hyper-gravimetric field of hyper-spacetime, the hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}(x)
defined in Eq.(
VII.1
) is regarded as scaling gauge invariant
Levi-Civita hyper-connection
or scaling gauge invariant
Christoffel symbols
of the second kind in hyper-spacetime. By lowering the index of the second kind Christoffel symbols
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}(x)
via the hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
, we obtain scaling gauge invariant Christoffel symbols of the first kind as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{L}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{L}}=\frac{1}{2}(\,\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}}+\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}-\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}\,)

(160)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}}\equiv\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}.

Similarly, by raising the index of the second kind Christoffel symbols
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}(x)
via the dual hyper-gravimetric field
\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
, we obtain the following Christoffel symbols:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}}=\frac{1}{2}(\,\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}}-\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}}-\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\,)

(161)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mathbf{M}[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}-\frac{1}{2}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}}.

Where the antisymmetric part of hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field can simply be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}=-\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}}-\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}}),

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mathbf{M}[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}=\frac{1}{2}(\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}}-\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}}),\quad\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{P}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}.

(162)

Christoffel symbols in hyper-spacetime introduces the principal connection of general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R), which inspires us to generalize global inhomogeneous Lorentz-type transformation of Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,
D_{h}
-1) in globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime to
general coordinate transformation of general linear group symmetry
GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R). The general coordinate transformation is defined as an arbitrary reparametrization of coordinate systems, i.e.:

\displaystyle x^{\mathbf{M}}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}\equiv x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}(x),

(163)

which presents the local transformation for describing a distinct reparametrization at every point in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime. Such an arbitrary reparametrization leads the displacement and derivative of coordinate systems to obey the following transformation laws:

\displaystyle dx^{\mathbf{M}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle dx^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}=T^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{N}}\,dx^{\mathbf{N}}\,,\quad T^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{N}}\equiv\frac{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}}{\partial x^{\mathbf{N}}},

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{M}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\partial^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}=T_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{N}}\,\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\,,\quad T_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\equiv\frac{\partial x^{\mathbf{N}}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}},

(164)

which keeps the following inner product:

\displaystyle\langle\partial^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}},dx^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\rangle=T_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{N}}T^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\langle\partial_{\mathbf{N}},dx^{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\rangle=T_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{N}}T^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}=\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}=\langle\partial_{\mathbf{M}},dx^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\rangle,

(165)

where
\partial_{\mathbf{M}}
is regarded as covariant vector and
dx^{\mathbf{M}}
as contravariant vector.

With the above transformation laws, any covariant vector field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
with a lower index and contravariant vector field
\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathbf{M}}(x)
with an upper index will get the following general transformation properties:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x^{\prime})=T_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\,{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}}(x),\quad\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}(x^{\prime})=T^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{N}}\,\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathbf{N}}(x),

(166)

which leads the scalar product of covariant vector field with contravariant vector field to be invariant, i.e.:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathbf{M}}(x)={\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}(x^{\prime})\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}(x^{\prime}).

(167)

The hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
and its dual vector
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}(x)
correspond to the covariant and contravariant vector fields, respectively, in hyper-spacetime. The hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
defined by the covariant vector field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
is a covariant tensor field with the following transformation law:

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x^{\prime})=\frac{\partial x^{\mathbf{P}}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}}\frac{\partial x^{\mathbf{Q}}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}}}\,\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}(x)=T_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}T_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{Q}}\,\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}(x).

(168)

Similarly, the dual hyper-gravimetric field
\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
defined by the contravariant vector field
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}(x)
is a contravariant tensor with the corresponding transformation law. It can be checked that the Christoffel symbols transform as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\frac{\partial x^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}}\frac{\partial x^{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}}})\left(\,\frac{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}}{\partial x^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}(x)-\frac{\partial^{2}x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}}{\partial x^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\partial x^{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}}\,\right)

(169)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle T_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}T_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}T^{\mathbf{P}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\left(\,\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}(x)-T^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}_{\;\;\,\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}T^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}_{\;\;\,\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\right),

which behaves as local gauge-type transformation of general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R) with appearance of inhomogeneous transformation term.

The covariant derivatives for covariant vector field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}(x)
and contravariant vector field
\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathbf{M}}(x)
obey the general covariance under general coordinate transformations, i.e.:

\displaystyle(\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}})^{{}^{\prime}}=(\frac{\partial x^{\mathbf{P}}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}}\frac{\partial x^{\mathbf{Q}}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}}})(\nabla_{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{Q}})\equiv T_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}T_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{Q}}(\nabla_{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{Q}}),

\displaystyle(\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathbf{N}})^{{}^{\prime}}=(\frac{\partial x^{\mathbf{P}}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}})(\frac{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}}}{\partial x^{\mathbf{N}}})(\nabla_{\mathbf{P}}\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathbf{Q}})\equiv T_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}T^{\mathbf{N}}_{\;\;\mathbf{Q}}(\nabla_{\mathbf{P}}\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathbf{Q}}),

(170)

with the definition of covariant derivatives as follows:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{P}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathbf{N}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathbf{N}}+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{N}}\hat{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathbf{P}}.

(171)

Therefore, the scaling gauge invariant hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field strength
\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}
defines Riemann curvature tensor which characterizes
curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime
with the
emergence of Riemann geometry
. When lowering indices with the hyper-gravimetric field, we can express the Riemann curvature tensor as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}

(172)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}}-\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}+\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{L}\mathbf{L}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{L}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}).

which has the following symmetry properties as both gauge field strength and Riemann curvature tensor:

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}=-\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}},\quad\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}=-\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}=\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}},\quad\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}=\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}},

(173)

and satisfies two kind of Bianchi identities:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{L}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}+\nabla_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}+\nabla_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{L}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}=0,

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}}+\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}+\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}}=0.

(174)

It is straightforward to verify that the action in Eq.(
158
) becomes invariant under the general coordinate transformation defined as an arbitrary reparametrization in coordinate system presented in Eqs.(
163
) and (
VII.3
). The verification can easily be carried out from the property that the transformation laws of tensors and Christoffel symbols as well as covariant derivatives lead the scalar product between covariant tensor and contravariant tensor to be invariant under general coordinate transformation.

It can be proved that the action in Eq.(
158
) brings on the following emergent general linear group symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}=\mbox{GL(}1,D_{h}-1,\mbox{R)},

(175)

which is thought to govern gravitational interaction in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime with emergence of Riemann geometry. Such a group symmetry appears actually as a hidden local symmetry in the action built by following along the gauge invariance principle and scaling invariance hypothesis in biframe hyper-spacetime as shown in Eq.(
81
)

In general, the hyperunified field theory formulated in hidden gauge formalism should possess the following maximal joint symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}=\mbox{GL(}1,D_{h}-1,\mbox{R)}\Join\mbox{WS(}1,D_{h}-1),

(176)

which brings on the extension of global Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,
D_{h}
) in globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime to a general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1,R) in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime. The inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) still holds for the hyperunified qubit-spinor field, while it appears as a hidden gauge symmetry in the action concerning purely bosonic interactions of hyper-spacetime gauge field and scalar field.

Therefore, we come to the statement that
the laws of nature should be independent of the choice of coordinate systems
, which is indicated from emergent general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1,R) which brings on the emergence of Riemann geometry in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetim. So that we achieve a generalization of gauge invariance principle that the gauge invariance principle is not only essential to the fundamental symmetry of basic fields in Hilbert space but also applicable to the fundamental symmetry of coordinates in Minkowski spacetime. In this sense, we may refer to such an extension of gauge invariance principle as
generalized gauge invariance principle
. It is intriguing to notice that when the fundamental symmetry of basic fields in Hilbert space is gauged to be local symmetry by following along the gauge invariance principle together with scaling invariance hypothesis, the fundamental symmetry of coordinates in Minkowski spacetime automatically obeys generalized gauge invariance principle with the emergence of general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1,R) and genesis of Riemann geometry in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime. Such a feature implies a
gravity-geometry correspondence
which will be discussed below.

VII.4
Flowing unitary gauge with symmetric Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field and the gravity-geometry correspondence in hyper-spacetime

In the action formulated in hidden gauge formalism shown in Eq.(
158
), the hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) for the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
is preserved by the scaling gauge invariant totally antisymmetric hyper-spacetime gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}
which is characterized by the Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
. The hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}
as Christoffel symbols decouples from the hyperunified qubit-spinor field due to the self-conjugated chiral spinor representation of hyperunified qubit-spinor field and the symmetric property of Christoffel symbols
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}=\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}}
. To see that explicitly, let us rewrite the action in Eq.(
158
) into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}\equiv\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\mathfrak{A}(x)\,\mathfrak{L}=\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\mathfrak{A}(x)\,

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\{\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{-}^{\mathbf{M}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}(\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}})

\displaystyle\qquad-\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\frac{1}{4}{\hat{{\mathbf{F}}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}+\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}

\displaystyle\qquad+g_{H}^{-2}\mathbf{R}-\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\}+2\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}),

(177)

where we have used the decomposition formulation for the hyper-spacetime gauge field strengths given in Eqs.(
VII.1
) and (
VII.1
). The covariant derivative is given as follows:

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\equiv i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}+(\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}+\mathbf{A}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]})\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}.

(178)

where
\mathbf{A}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}
is a totally antisymmetric hyper-spacetime covariant-gauge field instead of totally antisymmetric hyper-spacetime gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}
.

It is noticed that in the gravitational interaction of hyperunified qubit-spinor field, the hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}
(
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}
) decouples due to its symmetric property and the self-conjugated chiral feature of hyperunified qubit-spinor field. Only the totally antisymmetric hyper-spacetime covariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}
and totally antisymmetric hyper-spacetime gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}
interact with the hyperunified qubit-spinor field. Note that the hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}
characterized by the hyper-gravimetric field reflects the geometric property of gravitational interaction, while
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}
is actually characterized by the hyper-gravigauge field rather than hyper-gravimetric field. Therefore, the gravitational interaction of hyperunified qubit-spinor field is essentially governed by the Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
(
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
) through the hyper-spacetime gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}
and hyper-spacetime group generators
\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{-}^{\mathbf{M}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}
of inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1).

On the other hand, the bosonic hyper-spacetime gauge interaction is described by antisymmetric hyper-spacetime covariant gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}
and symmetric hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}
as Christoffel symbols, which is governed by the emergent general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1,R). Namely, the hyperspin gauge symmetry is completely transmuted into a hidden gauge symmetry in the bosonic interactions. As
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}
is characterized by the symmetric Goldstone-like hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
, it concerns
N_{\mathsf{H}}=D_{h}(D_{h}+1)/2
degrees of freedom.

As the fundamental gravitational field is essentially characterized by the Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
which has
N_{\mathbf{A}}=D_{h}\times D_{h}
degrees of freedom. In comparison to the symmetric hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
, the hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
contains extra degrees of freedom given by
N_{E}=D_{h}^{2}-D_{h}(D_{h}+1)/2=D_{h}(D_{h}-1)/2
, which exactly reflects the equivalence classes of hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) and has the same numbers as those of group parameters of SP(1,
D_{h}
-1), i.e.,
N_{G}=D_{h}(D_{h}-1)/2=N_{E}
.

In general, the presence of gauge symmetry in the action involves redundant degrees of freedom. In order to eliminate the redundant degrees of freedom arising from the gauge symmetry, it is necessary to make a gauge prescription with imposing gauge fixing condition. For the hyperspin gauge symmetry which is unlike usual internal gauge symmetry as it has a gravitational origin characterized by the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
as hyper-gravigauge field, we are able to realize a simple gauge prescription. That is just to take an appropriate hyperspin gauge transformation
\bar{\Lambda}_{\;\,\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}}(x)
to transmute the Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field into symmetric one, i.e.:

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}(x)\bar{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{B}}_{\;\,\mathbf{A}}(x)=\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}(x)\to\hat{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}(x)=\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{M}}(x)\bar{\Lambda}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)=\bar{\hat{\mathbf{A}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x),

(179)

which sets an explicit gauge prescription as a gauge fixing condition. We may refer to such a gauge prescription as
flowing unitary gauge
, which holds locally with a chosen coordinate system at point to point in hyper-spacetime. A detailed discussion on such a flowing unitary gauge will be presented later on.

When fixing the hyperspin gauge symmetry with flowing unitary gauge, both Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)
and hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
are symmetric fields, they involve exactly the same degrees of freedom. Explicitly, they are correlated determinately each other with the following relation:

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{B}}\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{N}}\equiv(\bar{\mathbf{A}})^{2}_{\;\,\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}.

(180)

By adopting a nonlinearly realized exponential representation, we can express them as matrices with the following forms:

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}=\phi(e^{G/M_{\kappa}})_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}\,,\quad G_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}=G_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}=\bar{\mathbf{A}}^{2}=\phi^{2}e^{G/M_{\kappa}}e^{G/M_{\kappa}}=\phi^{2}e^{2G/M_{\kappa}},

(181)

where the matrix
G_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}
is regarded as nonlinearly realized
symmetric Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
and
M_{\kappa}
as basic mass scale.

In such a flowing unitary gauge, both hyper-spacetime gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}
and hyper-gravigauge
\Gamma
-matrices
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathbf{M}}
are described by the symmetric Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}
and its dual field
\hat{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}\to\bar{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}}\,\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{C}}+\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}}\,\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}+\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}}\,\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{C}}),

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{-}^{\mathbf{M}}\to\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{-}^{\mathbf{M}}\equiv\bar{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mathbf{M}}\varGamma_{-},\quad\bar{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mathbf{M}}\equiv\hat{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}\varGamma_{\mathbf{A}}=\hat{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}\varGamma_{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\to\bar{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\equiv\frac{i}{4}[\bar{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mathbf{P}},\bar{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mathbf{Q}}]=\hat{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{Q}}\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{A}}\hat{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{B}}\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}.

(182)

In the hyperunified field theory formulated in a hidden gauge formalism, when setting the gauge fixing condition to be flowing unitary gauge, both hyper-spacetime gravigauge field
\bar{\mathbf{\Omega}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}
and hyper-spacetime gravimetric field
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}
are basically characterized by the symmetric Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}(x)
(or nonlinearly realized symmetric Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
G_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)=G_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}(x)
). Therefore, the independent degrees of freedom concerning the hyper-spacetime gauge interaction are represented by the symmetric Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}(x)
(or equivalently the symmetric Goldstone-like hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)=(\bar{\mathbf{A}})^{2}_{\;\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
) and antisymmetric hyper-spacetime covariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}(x)
. Nevertheless, the total independent degrees of freedom in the hyperunified field theory should remain unchanged as the extra degrees of freedom in a general Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
are considered to be absorbed into the antisymmetric hyper-spacetime covariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}(x)
, which behaves as a
massive-like gauge field
with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field regarded as mass-like term shown from the scalar interaction term
\beta_{G}
in the action given in Eq.(
VII.4
).

As the symmetry groups GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R) and WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) in Eq.(
176
) cannot act as a direct product group, the gauge fixing condition is actually a local one and holds only for a given reparametrization of coordinate systems in hyper-spacetime. To make such a gauge fixing condition valid at any reparametrization of coordinate systems in hyper-spacetime, the gauge fixing operation must always associate with any given reparametrization of coordinate systems and proceed with an appropriate hyperspin gauge transformation at any given reparametrization so as to preserve symmetric hyper-gravigauge field. Such a gauge fixing condition is regarded as
flowing unitary gauge
in the gravigauge hyper-fiber bundle structure when carrying out general transformations under symmetry groups GL(
D_{h}
, R) and WS(1,
D_{h}
-1), which makes the gauge fixing procedure to run with a flowing gauge fixing condition, so that it ensures the symmetric property of hyper-gravigauge field at arbitrary point of coordinate systems in hyper-spacetime. Namely, for every general coordinate transformation defined as an arbitrary reparametrization of coordinate systems,
x^{\prime}\equiv x^{\prime}(x)
, one can always perform an appropriate hyperspin gauge transformation associated with a distinct local reparametrization at every point in hyper-spacetime.

Such a gauge fixing procedure can always be realized with two-step transformations. Namely, carrying out a general coordinate transformation as follows:

\displaystyle dx^{\mathbf{M}}\to dx^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}=T^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\,\mathbf{N}}\,dx^{\mathbf{N}}\,,\quad\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\to\partial^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}=T_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle T_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{N}},\;T^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\,\mathbf{N}}\in GL(1,D_{h}-1,R)

(183)

as the first step, and then performing an appropriate hyperspin gauge transformation at point
x^{\prime}\equiv x^{\prime}(x)
,

\displaystyle\varGamma^{\mathbf{A}}\to S^{\prime}(\bar{\Lambda}^{\prime})\varGamma^{\mathbf{A}}S^{{}^{\prime}-1}(\bar{\Lambda}^{\prime})=\bar{\Lambda}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x^{\prime})\varGamma^{\mathbf{B}}\,,

\displaystyle\bar{\Lambda}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\mathbf{B}}(x^{\prime})\in SP(1,D_{h}\mbox{-}1),

(184)

as the second step, so that we arrive at a symmetric hyper-gravigauge field at every point in hyper-spacetime, i.e.:

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}(x)\to\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x^{\prime})=T_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\,\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}(x)\neq\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}(x^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x^{\prime})=\bar{\Lambda}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{}^{\prime}\;\mathbf{B}}(x^{\prime})\,\mathbf{A}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}(x^{\prime})=\mathbf{A}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}(x^{\prime}).

(185)

For the gravitational interaction, the flowing unitary gauge always enables us to take symmetric Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field as fundamental gravitational field at every point in hyper-spacetime. The introduction of flowing unitary gauge allows us to regard all points in hyper-spacetime to be equivalent when taking gauge fixing condition to be a unitary gauge.

To keep Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)
be always symmetric under flowing unitary gauge for a chosen coordinate system, the action is turned out to possess only an associated symmetry in which global hyperspin symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) of hyperunified qubit-spinor field must correlate with global Lorentz-type group symmetry SO(1,
D_{h}
-1) of coordinates, so that their group transformations have to coincide with each other as indicated from their isomorphic property SP(1,
D_{h}
-1)
\cong
SO(1,
D_{h}
-1).

Therefore, when the hyperunified field theory is formulated in light of hidden gauge formalism and presented under flowing unitary gauge, the gauge interactions for both hyperunified qubit-spinor field and bosonic fields are described by the symmetric Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)
and antisymmetric hyper-spacetime covariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}(x)
. The hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}(x)
as Christoffel symbols characterized by hyper-gravimetric field is completely determined by
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)
via
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)=\bar{\mathbf{A}}^{2}_{\;\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
. Therefore, the geometry and dynamics of hyper-spacetime are essentially governed by symmetric Goldstone-like hyper-gravigauge field
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)
, which leads to the
gravity-geometry correspondence
in hyper-spacetime.

Therefore, for a chosen gauge fixing condition under flowing unitary gauge with the symmetric hyper-gravigauge field
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}(x)
, the action of hyperunified field theory in hidden gauge formalism given in Eq.(
VII.4
) possesses the following associated symmetry with global inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) in association with global Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,
D_{h}
-1), i.e.:

\displaystyle G_{S}=SC(1)\ltimes PO(1,D_{h}-1)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,WS(1,D_{h}-1),

which reproduces the fundamental symmetry in free-motion Minkowski hyper-spacetime, where the transformations of global Lorentz-type group symmetry SO(1,
D_{h}
-1) and hyperspin symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) have to be coincidental as indicated by the symbol ‘
\,\widetilde{\Join}\,
’.

As the flowing unitary gauge is valid locally at point by point, to fix the flowing property of general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R), it remains to impose an additional gauge fixing condition to achieve a whole unitary gauge, which will be discussed later on.

VIII
Hyperunified field theory in hidden coordinate formalism with geometry-gauge correspondence and the dynamics of fundamental fields with hyperspin gravigauge field as auxiliary field and structure factor of non-commutative geometry in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime

The hyperunified field theory is shown to be independent of the choice of coordinate systems and hold in any coordinate systems so as to bring about the generalized gauge invariance principle, which inspires us to reformulate the hyperunified field theory in light of hidden coordinate formalism. We will apply for again the scaling gauge invariant hyper-gravigauge field as Goldstone-like boson to represent the hyperunified field theory in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, where the hyper-spacetime of coordinates becomes a hidden hyper-spacetime. It is intriguing to explore the nature of gravitational interaction in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime and corroborate the geometry-gauge correspondence of gravitational interaction in hidden coordinate formalism. Relative to the gravity-geometry correspondence shown in hidden gauge formalism in which the gravitational interaction can equivalently be characterized by the dynamics of Riemann geometry in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime, we will demonstrate that the geometry of locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime emerges as non-commutative geometry which is characterized by non-Abelian Lie algebra structure via non-commutative relation of hyper-gravicoordinate derivative operator. It is unlike globally flat free-motion hyper-spacetime described by Minkowski hyper-spacetime with commutative geometry, the gravitational interaction in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime appears as an emergent interaction which is characterized through non-commutative geometry. In fact, we will verify that the hyperspin gravigauge field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime behaves as an auxiliary field and determines completely the structure factor of non-Abelian Lie algebra for characterizing non-commutative geometry. So that the dynamics of all fundamental fields is associated with gravitational interaction via emergent non-commutative geometry in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

VIII.1
Dimensionless hyperspin gauge field and field strength in hidden coordinate formalism with zero global and local scaling charges

Taking the scaling gauge invariant hyper-gravigauge field as Goldstone-like boson, it is straightforward to project the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
as vector field in free-motion hyper-spacetime
\mathbf{M}_{h}
into the vector field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime
{\mathbf{G}}_{h}
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}.

(186)

The gravitational origin of hyperspin gauge symmetry shown in Eq.(
IV.2
) enables us to express the above hyperspin gauge field into the following two parts:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

(187)

where
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
define the
dimensionless hyperspin gravigauge field and hyperspin covariant-gauge field
, respectively, in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
reveals the gravitational origin of gauge symmetry, which is explicitly given by dual hyper-gravigauge fields
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{A}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{B}}-\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{M}}\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{A}}-\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}(\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{B}}-\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\,\mathbf{A}})

(188)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{A}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{M}}-\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{B}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}})\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}],

where
\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}
is the dimensionless hyper-gravicoordinate derivative operator defined in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

Correspondingly, the dimensionless field strength of hyperspin gauge field in hidden coordinate formalism can be expressed into the following two parts:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

(189)

with the explicit forms given as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{D}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}}),

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

(190)

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}}\,,

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

(191)

where
\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and
{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
are the dimensionless hyperspin gravigauge field strength and hyperspin covariant-gauge field strength, respectively.
\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}
defines the dimensionless covariant derivative of hyper-gravicoordinate derivative
\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}
under the presence of hyperspin gravigauge field in hidden coordinate formalism. For convenience,
\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}
is mentioned as
dimensionless hyper-gravigauge covariant derivative
.

Similarly, the dimensionless scaling gauge field strength is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}=\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{D}}-\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{D}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}},\quad\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\,\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{D}}=\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{D}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}.

(192)

The hyperspin gravigauge field strength
\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
defines Riemann-like tensor in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, which can be verified from the relation between the field strength of hyperspin gravigauge field defined in hidden coordinate formalism and that defined in hidden gauge formalism, i.e.:

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}_{\mathbf{A}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathbf{B}},

(193)

which enables us to define the symmetric Ricci-like curvature tensor by contracting the Riemann-like tensor in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime,

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}=\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\,\mathbf{D}}=-\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\,\mathbf{D}}=-\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}=\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}},

(194)

and also the Ricci-like scalar curvature given by the trace of Ricci-like curvature tensor,

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}=\eta^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\,\mathbf{D}}=-\eta^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}}\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{D}}.

(195)

From the symmetry properties and identities of Riemann tensors shown in Eqs.(
VII.3
) and (
VII.3
), we arrive at the following analogous relations:

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=-\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},\quad\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=-\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}},\quad\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}},

(196)

for the symmetry properties, and

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}}+\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}}+\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}=0,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{E}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}+\mathbf{D}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}+\mathbf{D}_{\mathbf{D}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}=0,

(197)

for the Bianchi identities.

In general, we have the following relations between the field strength defined in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime and that in biframe hyper-spacetime with gravigauge hyper-fiber bundle structure:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{D}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}};\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{D}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}};\quad\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}};\quad{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}.

(198)

By lowering and raising the indices with the constant metric matrices
\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and
\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
, we can define the following gauge field and field strength in the hidden coordinate formalism:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\eta^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\eta^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}.

(199)

It is interesting to note that all fields defined in the hidden coordinate formalism become dimensionless with zero global and local scaling charges
\mathcal{C}_{s}=0
and
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=0
.

VIII.2
Hyperunified field theory in hidden coordinate formalism with geometry-gauge correspondence and the hyperspin gravigauge field as auxiliary field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime

From the above analysis, we are able to represent the action of hyperunified field theory in hidden coordinate formalism as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\mathfrak{A}(x)\,\mathfrak{L}=\int[\delta^{D_{h}}\varkappa]\,

(200)

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\{\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+g_{H}^{-2}\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\mathsf{F}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\}

with the definitions:

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}\equiv i\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle[\delta^{D_{h}}\varkappa]\equiv[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\mathfrak{A}(x),\quad\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)=\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}dx^{\mathbf{M}},

(201)

where
[\delta^{D_{h}}\varkappa]
is regarded as the integral measure in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, and
\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
is considered as the hyper-gravicoordinate displacement of hyper-gravicoordinate vector field
\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.
\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
is also called as dimensionless hyper-gravivector field for short.

The action in hidden coordinate formalism shown in Eq.(
200
) gets a similar form as the action in hidden gauge formalism presented in Eq.(
158
). Unlike the action in Eq.(
158
), the action in Eq.(
200
) does not explicitly involve the hyper-gravigauge field
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
and hyper-gravimetric field
\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
, all Latin alphabet indices for vectors and tensors are raised and lowered by the constant metric matrices
\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
or
\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\mathop{\rm diag}.(1,-1,\ldots,-1)
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. It is interesting to note that all basic fields defined in hidden coordinate formalism become dimensionless and have zero global and local scaling charges
\mathcal{C}_{s}=0
and
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=0
.

The fundamental gauge interactions in hidden coordinate formalism are governed by the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
. In comparison to the action in hidden gauge formalism given in Eq.(
158
), the dimensionless hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
in hidden coordinate formalism replaces both hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
and hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
to characterize the gravitational interaction in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. The dynamics of
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
is characterized by the Ricci-like scalar tensor
\mathbf{R}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}=\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{D}}
, which is analogous to the scaling gauge invariant Einstein-Hilbert type action in hidden gauge formalism. Nevertheless, they have distinguishable features that the Ricci-like scalar tensor
\mathbf{R}\equiv\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\eta^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}
in hidden coordinate formalism is governed by hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) via hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
, whereas the Ricci-like scalar tensor
\mathbf{R}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}}
in hidden gauge formalism is governed by general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1) via hyper-spacetime gravimetric-gauge field
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}
as Christoffel symbols in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime.

In such a hidden coordinate formalism, the fundamental gravitational interaction is characterized by the gauge interaction through the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
. Therefore, when comparing the action in hidden coordinate formalism in Eq.(
200
) with that in hidden gauge formalism in Eq.(
158
), we come to the
geometry-gauge correspondence
.

To show explicitly such a
geometry-gauge correspondence
, it is useful to corroborate the following relation:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}\,\mathbf{R}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}=\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}}

(202)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}\,\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}=\mathfrak{A}\,\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\eta^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{A}\,\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}-\mathfrak{A}\,\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}+2\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}}),

which enables us to rewrite the action in Eq.(
200
) into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[\delta^{D_{h}}\varkappa]\{\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(203)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-g_{H}^{-2}(\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\mathsf{F}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\},

where we have ignored the surface term
2\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}})
. Such an action represents the hyperunified field theory in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, which explicitly displays the geometry-gauge correspondence for gravitational interaction. It is noticed that when taking the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
as the fundamental field, there appears no kinetic term for the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

Therefore, we come to the observation that the hyperunified field theory formulated in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime as shown in Eq.(
203
) brings on an intriguing feature that
the gravitational interaction appears to decouple from the hyperunified qubit-spinor field and is solely characterized by the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
which behaves as an auxiliary field
.

VIII.3
Emergence of non-commutative geometry with non-Abelian Lie algebra structure and the constraint equation of hyper-gravigauge field as auxiliary field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime

Let us begin with the nontrivial commutation relation of hyper-gravicoordinate derivative operator which forms gravigauge hyper-basis
\{\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\}
for locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime
{\mathbf{G}}_{h}
, i.e.:

\displaystyle[\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\,,\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{D}}]=\hat{f}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mspace{1mu}\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}}\equiv\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}\equiv\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}}\equiv-\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{B}},

(204)

which leads to
non-Abelian Lie algebra
of hyper-gravicoordinate derivative operator
\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}
. Unlike the usual non-Abelian Lie algebra, the structure factor
\hat{f}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}}
for such a non-Abelian Lie algebra is no longer a constant, which is determined by the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
and brings on the
emergence of non-commutative geometry
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime
{\mathbf{G}}_{h}
. To explore the non-commutative geometric property of locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime
{\mathbf{G}}_{h}
, it needs to resolve the dynamics of hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
from the geometry-gauge correspondence.

In the action of hyperunified field theory formulated in hidden coordinate formalism with geometry-gauge correspondence shown in Eq.(
203
), the gravitational interaction is completely characterized by the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}}
. Nevertheless, when taking the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
as fundamental gauge field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}}
should not act as an independent field in order to preserve the total independent degrees of freedom in the hyperunified field theory. In fact,
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}}
couples only to the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and scaling gauge field via dimensionless hyper-gravigauge covariant derivative appearing in their gauge field strengths shown in Eqs.(
VII.1
) and (
VIII.1
) and meanwhile possesses the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar-gauge interaction term indicated by
\beta_{G}^{2}
. To show explicitly the hyperspin gravigauge field interaction, it is useful to rewrite Eqs.(
VII.1
) and (
VIII.1
) into the following forms:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{E}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{D}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{B}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}}),

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\equiv\tilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{E}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{E}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\equiv\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{D}}-\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{D}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}}.

(205)

To study the dynamics of non-commutative geometry through the structure factor
\hat{f}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}}\equiv\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
of non-Abelian Lie algebra for hyper-gravicoordinate derivative operator
\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}
, it is useful to express the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}
appearing in the action (Eq.(
203
) ) as antisymmetric structure factor
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
. For that, we should use the following relations:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}\equiv\eta_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}=\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}\equiv\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\bar{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}},

(206)

with the constant tensors defined as follows:

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{2}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}-\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}=\frac{1}{4}(3\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}+3\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}+\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}),

(207)

which enables us to rewrite the action in Eq.(
203
) into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[\delta^{D_{h}}\varkappa]\{\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(208)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}(\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-2\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle+
