\displaystyle g_{H}^{-2}\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\tilde{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}-\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\left(\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}+({\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}-2\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}){\mathcal{A}}^{[\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\right)\bar{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}(\tilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\tilde{\mathsf{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}-2\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\tilde{\mathsf{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{A}})+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{B}}\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{B}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\},

with the definitions,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}=\bar{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}-\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\;\,=\frac{1}{4}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}+3\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}+\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{A}})-\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}},

(209)

where the tensor
\bar{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}
is presented in Eq.(
VIII.3
).

In the above action, the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
gets no kinetic term and appears as an
auxiliary field
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. From the least action principle, we obtain the following relation:

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\,\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}+\tilde{f}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\,\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}=\tilde{F}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}},

(210)

which presents a
constraint equation of hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
. Where we have introduced the following definitions:

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}-\frac{1}{4}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\;[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}+\frac{g_{H}^{2}}{g_{W}^{2}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{A}}),

\displaystyle\tilde{F}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathcal{A}}^{[\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}+\frac{g_{H}^{2}}{g_{W}^{2}}\tilde{\mathsf{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{A}}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\equiv\tilde{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}-\tilde{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}},\quad\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\;[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}\equiv\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}-\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\;\mathbf{C}},

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\equiv\bar{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}-\bar{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}}.

(211)

It is clear that the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
is no longer an independent gauge field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime when the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
is chosen as fundamental gauge field in hidden coordinate formalism. It is shown from Eqs.(
210
) and (
VIII.3
) that
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
is in generally given by the hyperspin gauge field and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\chi_{s}
as well as scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}}
.
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
in the constraint equation presented in Eq.(
210
) appears in formal to be irrelevant to the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
, while it is indirectly correlated to
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
through the dynamic equations of hyperspin gauge field and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field shown as follows.

Based on the hyperunified field theory in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime with geometry-gauge correspondence as shown in Eq.(
208
), we come to the observation that the gravitational interaction behaves as an emergent interaction described by the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
.
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
as auxiliary field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime is given by the fundamental bosonic fields through the constraint equation presented in Eqs.(
210
) and (
VIII.3
). There are three fundamental bosonic fields in the hyperunified field theory including the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\chi_{s}
as well as scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}}
. The constraint equation for
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
enables us to determine in principle the structure factor of non-Abelian Lie algebra for hyper-gravicoordinate derivative operator
\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}
, which characterizes an emergent non-commutative geometry as genesis of gravitational interaction in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

VIII.4
Dynamics of fundamental fields in hyperunified field theory with emergent gravitational interaction in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime

The hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
characterizing the geometry-gauge correspondence and emergence of non-commutative geometry is verified to be determined by the fundamental bosonic fields through the constraint equation presented in Eqs.(
210
) and (
VIII.3
). The gravitational interaction in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime appears to decouple in formal from the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
when taking the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
as fundamental gauge fields in hidden coordinate formalism of the action shown in Eq.(
208
). We will demonstrate how the dynamics of all fundamental fields gets gravitational effects through emergent gravitational interaction caused from the appearance of non-commutative geometry in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

To derive the dynamic equations of motion for the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
and hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
, it is useful to decompose the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
into the following two orthogonal parts:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}+{\mathcal{A}}_{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}{\mathcal{A}}^{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}=0,

(212)

with the explicit definitions,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}})

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(2{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}}),

(213)

where
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}
denotes
totally antisymmetric hyperspin gauge field
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}
represents
symmetric-antisymmetric mixing hyperspin gauge field
. Correspondingly, the hyperspin gauge field strength
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
can also be decomposed into two orthogonal parts:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}+{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}}+{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}})

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(2{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}}-{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}=0.

(214)

With the above decompositions, the action in Eq.(
203
) can be expressed into the following form,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[\delta^{D_{h}}\varkappa]\{\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(215)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}+{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle g_{H}^{-2}(\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,({\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]})({\mathcal{A}}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,({\mathcal{A}}_{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]})({\mathcal{A}}^{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-\mathbf{\Omega}^{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\mathsf{F}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\,\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\},

with

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}\equiv i\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}.

(216)

It is noticed that in hidden coordinate formalism of the action, only the totally antisymmetric hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}
couples to the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
due to its self-conjugated chiral property.

Based on the least action principle, we are able to derive the dynamic equations of motion for hyperspin gauge field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, which can be written into the following two types:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}=-g_{H}^{2}{\mathcal{J}}^{[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}=-g_{H}^{2}{\mathcal{J}}^{(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}({\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}}+{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}})

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(2{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}}-{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}),

(217)

where
{\mathcal{J}}^{[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}
represents the totally antisymmetric covariant tensor current and
{\mathcal{J}}^{(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}
the symmetric-antisymmetric mixing covariant tensor current. Their explicit forms are given as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}^{[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}=\frac{1}{4}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\{\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{D}},\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,({\mathcal{A}}^{[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}^{(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}=-g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,({\mathcal{A}}^{(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-\mathbf{\Omega}^{(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}).

(218)

The covariant derivative in Eq.(
VIII.4
) is defined as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{C}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{D}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{E}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

(219)

where the hyper-gravigauge covariant derivative
\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}
and additional field strength term
\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{E}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
reflect the geometry-gauge correspondence and the emergence of non-commutative geometry characterized by the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

In general, the above equations of motion in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime can also be formulated from Eq.(
102
) obtained in biframe hyper-spacetime.

For the hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
, the equation of motion is simply given by,

\displaystyle\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}\left(i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}-i{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\,\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}=0,

(220)

with the covariant derivative
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}
given in Eq.(
216
), which appears to be irrelevant to the gravitational interaction. While its quadratic form is found to have the following form:

\displaystyle(\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}})({\mathcal{D}}^{\mathbf{C}}-{\mathbf{V}}^{\mathbf{C}})\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

\displaystyle\;\;=-\left(\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,+\varGamma^{\mathbf{C}}\tilde{\varGamma}\cosh\chi_{s}\,(i\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s})\,\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

\displaystyle\;\;+\varSigma^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-({\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}})i({\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{A}}-{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{A}})-i{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\,\right)\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}},

(221)

which indicates that the quadratic form of equation of motion for the hyperunified qubit-spinor field does generate gravitational effects characterized by the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
due to the emergence of non-commutative geometry in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. Where
{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}}
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}
are graviscaling induced gauge invariant field and field strength defined as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{D}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{D}}),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}=\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{D}}-\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}}=\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{D}}-\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{A}}.

(222)

The equation of motion for the scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}}
can simply be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{F}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}=g_{W}^{2}\chi_{s}^{2}\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{D}},

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{F}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\equiv\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{F}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{F}^{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{D}}\mathsf{F}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}.

(223)

It is straightforward to write down the equation of motion for the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field,

\displaystyle\lambda_{S}^{2}\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}={\mathsf{J}}_{s},

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{C}}\equiv\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{C}}\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}},

(224)

where the scalar current is given by,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{J}}_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\beta_{Q}\cosh\chi_{s}\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}+\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh\chi_{s}\cosh\chi_{s}\,\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{C}}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\chi_{s})

(225)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh\chi_{s}\cosh\chi_{s}\,({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}).

To associate with the relation given in Eq.(
210
) for the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
, we can adopt the following identities:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}]}\equiv\eta_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}]}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}]\mathbf{A}},\quad\mathbf{\Omega}_{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}]}\equiv\eta_{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}]}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}]\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\eta_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}]}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}+\eta_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}+\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle\eta_{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}]}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(2\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-\eta_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}).

(226)

Therefore, the hyperunified field theory formulated in light of hidden coordinate formalism exhibits the gravitational geometry-gauge correspondence. When taking the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
as fundamental gauge field, the gravitational interaction in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime appears in formal to decouple from the hyperunified qubit-spinor field. Meanwhile, the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
or
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
behaves as an auxiliary field, which is shown to be presented by fundamental bosonic fields through the constraint equation given in Eq.(
210
). Nevertheless, the dynamics of all fundamental fields turns out to get gravitational effects due to the emergence of non-commutative geometry characterized by the hyperspin gravigauge field via the non-Abelian Lie algebra of hyper-gravicoordinate derivative operator in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

IX
Hyperunified field theory in framework of GQFT with fundamental symmetry under entirety unitary gauge and the dynamics of electromagnetic-like gravitational field with gauge-type and Einstein-type gravitational equations and first order gravitational differential equation based the gauge-geometry duality

In this section, we are going to further investigate the foundation of the hyperunified field theory within the framework of gravitational quantum field theory (GQFT)
GQFT
by making an appropriate gauge prescription under entirety unitary gauge. With the demonstration of gauge-geometry duality in biframe hyper-spacetime, we will present a detailed discussion on the dynamics of hyper-gravigauge field and hyper-gravimetric gauge field with electric-like and magnetic-like gravitational interactions in both gauge-type and geometric Einstein-type gravitational equations.

IX.1
Hyperunified field theory within framework of GQFT and gauge-geometry duality with respect to hyper-gravigauge field and hyper-gravimetric gauge field

It has been shown that the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge group symmetry W
{}^{1,D_{h}-1}
that causes a sign flip in chirality for the transformed hyperunified qubit-spinor field brings on the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
. Such an entanglement-correlated translation-like
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge group W
{}^{1,D_{h}-1}
represents the Abelian subgroup of inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1), so that the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
as hyper-gravigauge field reveals the genesis of gravitational interaction and elaborates the gauge-gravity correspondence. In the hyperunified field theory with the action formulated in hidden gauge formalism, the gravitational interaction is shown to be described by the symmetric hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
which is composed of hyper-gravigauge field, i.e.,
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
. When making the gauge prescription to flowing unitary gauge, the symmetric hyper-gravimetric field is determined directly by the symmetric hyper-gravigauge field
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}=\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}
, i.e.,
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=(\bar{\mathbf{A}})^{2}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
, with
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}
representing physical degrees of freedom under flowing unitary gauge, which exposits the gravity-geometry correspondence in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime. Furthermore, in the hyperunified field theory with the action formulated in hidden coordinate formalism, the gravitational interaction appears to be an emergent one characterized via the emergent non-commutative geometry in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. Such a non-commutative geometry is described by the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{A}}
which behaves as an auxiliary field determined through the constraint equation concerning only basic bosonic fields, which elucidates the geometry-gauge correspondence.

Let us now turn to consider the hyperunified field theory within the framework of GQFT. By noticing the following relations,

\displaystyle\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}=\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\;\;+\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}(2\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-4\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}})\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{N}}),

(227)

we are able to rewrite the basic action for the hyperunified field theory as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\mathfrak{A}(x)\{\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(228)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle g_{H}^{-2}\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle g_{H}^{-2}\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-g_{H}^{-2}\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}})\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{N}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\},

with the definitions,

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}=i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{C}},\quad\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}=\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{E}}\left(\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{E}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{Q}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}-\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{E}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{Q}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{C}}-\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{Q}}\eta_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{C}}\right),

(229)

where the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
are taken to be the fundamental gauge fields, which is governed by the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry and also exposits the gauge-gravity correspondence.

It is interesting to notice that the gravitational interaction of all fundamental bosonic fields involves solely the dual Goldstone-like hyper-gravimetric field
\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
. Inspired from the gauge-gravity and gravity-geometry correspondences, it is useful to take the hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
and hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
as physical degrees of freedom in the gravigauge hyper-fiber bundle structure of biframe hyper-spacetime. In general, we can prove the following relations:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{A}\,\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}-2\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{A}}),

(230)

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{A}\,\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\mathfrak{A}\frac{1}{8}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{A}\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})+\mathfrak{A}\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\mathfrak{A}^{2}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{P}})\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{A}^{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}})-\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{A}^{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\left(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}})-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}})\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}\right)

\displaystyle+\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\left(\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\ln\mathfrak{A}\right)-2\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\left(\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{N}}\right)+\frac{3}{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\left(\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\right),

(231)

with
\hat{\mathfrak{A}}=1/\mathfrak{A}
and
\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\partial_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}
. Note that the last four terms appear as surface terms in the action. Where we have introduced the following Abelian-type field strength:

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}-\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{P}},

(232)

which indicates that the hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}
behaves as an Abelian-type gauge field. To be physically more meaningful, the hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}
and field strength
\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}
may be referred to as
hyper-gravimetric gauge field
and
hyper-gravimetric gauge field strength
, which brings on useful duality relations with respective to the hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
and field strength
\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}
.

The relations in Eqs.(
230
) and (
IX.1
) not only explicate the gauge-gravity and gravity-geometry as well as geometry-gauge correspondences, but also corroborate that the geometric hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}
in itself can be regarded as an Abelian-type gauge field, which reveals in general
gauge-geometry duality
.

With such a gravitational gauge-geometry duality, we can take the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and hyper-gravimetric gauge field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}
as fundamental gauge fields to express the action of the hyperunified field theory in light of gravigauge hyper-fiber bundle structure of biframe hyper-spacetime. Explicitly, such an action can be represented within the framework of GQFT as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\mathfrak{A}(x)\{\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(233)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\frac{1}{4}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}})-\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\mathfrak{A}^{2}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{P}})\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}})+\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\mathfrak{A}\hat{\partial}_{\mathbf{Q}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\},

where we have ignored the surface terms. The hyperspin gravigauge field can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{Q}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}[\,\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}})\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{Q}}-\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}})\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{Q}}\,],

(234)

which indicates that
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
concerns both hyper-gravimetric gauge field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
and hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
. It is also manifest that the gravitational interaction of hyperqubit-spinor field always involves the hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
. It is natural to take flowing unitary gauge so as to relate directly the symmetric hyper-gravigauge field to the symmetric hyper-gravimetric field,
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=(\bar{\mathbf{A}})^{2}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
with
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}=\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}
.

Therefore, within the framework of GQFT based on the basic structure of biframe hyper-spacetime, the dynamics of gravitational interaction can be described either by the symmetric hyper-gravigauge field or equivalently by the symmetric hyper-gravimetric gauge field under flowing unitary gauge with maintaining physical degrees of freedom. Such an equivalent description exposits the gauge-geometry duality for the gravitational interaction.

IX.2
Hyperunified field theory with maximal joint symmetry and the entirety unitary gauge in gravitational quantum field theory with fundamental symmetry of nature

The hyperunified field theory in the framework of GQFT is presented to show the gravitational gauge-geometry duality. Before discussing such an action, let us briefly outline the main properties of hyperunified field theory formulated in various formalisms.

As demonstrated in the hidden gauge formalism of the action given in Eq.(
158
), such an action of hyperunified field theory holds in any coordinate system due to the emergence of general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1,R), which does indicate that the laws of nature should be independent of the choice of coordinate systems. Geometrically, the gravitational interactions concerning fundamental bosonic fields are described by the dynamics of emergent Riemann geometry in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime, which is characterized by Christoffel symbols
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}
defined from the hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
. Such a gravitational interaction is governed by the emergent general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1,R) and meanwhile the hyperspin gauge symmetry becomes a hidden gauge symmetry, which shows the gravity-geometry correspondence. As a consequence, the basic action of hyperunified field theory possesses emergent general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1,R) under general coordinate transformation,

\displaystyle G_{S}=\mbox{GL(}1,D_{h}-1,\mbox{R)},

which represents the real Lie group of dimension
N_{D}=D_{h}^{2}
and consists of matrices that have non-zero determinant. It is known that such a general linear group symmetry lays the foundation of Einstein’s general theory of relativity in four dimensional spacetime.

It is proved in the hidden coordinate formalism of the action that, locally, at every point of hyper-spacetime, the basic action of hyperunified field theory possesses inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1). Whereas the general linear group symmetry GL(
D_{h}
,R) goes to be a hidden symmetry. The gravitational interaction is characterized by the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
which behaves as an auxiliary field determined through the constraint equation concerning only basic bosonic fields. Therefore, the gravitational interaction acts as an emergent interaction in correspondence to the emergence of non-commutative geometry in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. The hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
as fundamental gauge field is taken to be dynamic field, so that the basic action exhibits the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry as a maximal gauge symmetry,

\displaystyle G_{S}=\mbox{WS(1},D_{h}\mbox{-1)},

which has been applied to construct the basic action by following along the gauge invariance principle.

It is straightforward to justify that when the action is formulated in light of gravigauge hyper-fiber bundle structure of biframe hyper-spacetime as shown in Eqs.(
228
) and (
233
), it gets a bimaximal gauge symmetry,

\displaystyle G_{S}=\mbox{GL(}1,D_{h}-1,\mbox{R)}\Join\mbox{WS(}1,D_{h}-1),

(235)

which is regarded as a joint Lie group gauge symmetry since they cannot be operated as a direct product group. In general, such a joint gauge symmetry can be adopted as generalized gauge invariance principle to construct the basic action of hyperunified field theory. One is general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R) operating on the coordinates in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime, and the other is inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) operating on the entangled hyperqubit-spinor field and gauge field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

In practice, the basic action of hyperunified field theory is built within the framework of quantum field theory based on the gauge invariance principle and scaling invariance hypothesis in light of gravigauge hyper-fiber bundle structure of biframe hyper-spacetime. Where the free-motion hyper-spacetime described by globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime is taken as a base spacetime in gravigauge hyper-fiber bundle structure, so that the basic action of hyperunified field theory is built to possess global Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,
D_{h}
-1) and inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) as well as global and local scaling symmetries,

\displaystyle G_{S}=\mbox{SC(1)}\ltimes\mbox{PO(1},D_{h}\mbox{-1)}\Join\mbox{WS(1},D_{h}\mbox{-1)}\rtimes\mbox{SG(1)},

(236)

which has a total group dimension
N_{D}=2\times D_{h}(D_{h}-1)/2+2D_{h}+2=D_{h}(D_{h}+1)+2
. Where the Lorentz-type group SO(1,
D_{h}
-1) is a subgroup of GL(
D_{h}
, R). In such a consideration, the global and local scaling symmetries play an essential role in the construction of the basic action for the hyperunified field theory.

From the gauge invariance principle, the gauge field is introduced to reveal the dynamics of fundamental building block of nature. Locally, the gauge symmetry means that the laws of nature should be independent of the choice of field configurations. Namely, the gauge symmetry leads each physically distinct configuration of the system to be as an equivalence class of detailed local field configurations, which indicates that for any two detailed configurations that are in the same equivalence class, they are related by a gauge transformation. Therefore, the gauge symmetry concerns redundant unphysical degrees of freedom.

Physically, all observables should be gauge independent. In order to remove unphysical degrees of freedom arising from local gauge symmetry, it is necessary to fix gauge symmetry by making appropriate gauge prescription. Therefore, a gauge fixing is required to provide a mathematical procedure for dealing with redundant degrees of freedom in field variables caused by gauge symmetry.

In the hidden gauge formalism of the action, it has been shown that it is appropriate to choose a gauge prescription under flowing unitary gauge. This is because there exists always a hyperspin gauge transformation
\bar{\Lambda}_{\;\,\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}}(x)
to transform the hyper-gravigauge field into a symmetric bicovariant vector field at any chosen point of hyper-spacetime,
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)=\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}(x)\bar{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{B}}_{\;\,\mathbf{A}}(x)=\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}(x)
, which is referred to as flowing unitary gauge. In such a flowing unitary gauge, both symmetric hyper-gravigauge field
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)
and hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
involve exactly the same degrees of freedom with a simple relation
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv(\bar{\mathbf{A}})^{2}_{\;\,\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
.

Let us turn to the basic action of the hyperunified field theory with the gauge-geometry duality shown in Eq.(
228
) or (
233
). Such an action built in biframe hyper-spacetime with a gravigauge hyper-fiber bundle structure gets a maximal joint symmetry of two groups GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R)
\Join
WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) which cannot form a direct product group. It is noticed that only when setting up flowing unitary gauge to keep the symmetric property of hyper-gravigauge field at arbitrary point of coordinate systems in hyper-spacetime, we are able to obtain the same physical degrees of freedom to bring on both gauge-gravity and gravity-geometry correspondences. Namely, once the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) which governs the dynamics of hyperunified quibit-spinor field and gauge field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime is fixed appropriately to unitary gauge, the general linear symmetry group GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R) which characterizes the dynamics of Riemann geometry in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime should also be fixed simultaneously to ensure the symmetric property of hyper-gravigauge field. Namely, under the flowing unitary gauge, the basic action (
228
) or (
233
) formulated in light of gravigauge hyper-fiber bundle structure of biframe hyper-spacetime possesses only the following associated symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}=\mbox{SC(1)}\ltimes\mbox{PO(1},D_{h}\mbox{-1)}\,\widetilde{\Join}\,\mbox{WS(1},D_{h}\mbox{-1)},

which brings about the fundamental symmetry achieved from the so-called
{\mathcal{M}}_{c}
-spin charge of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field in free-motion hyper-spacetime.

To freeze over the flowing feature of unitary gauge for the hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) and provide a whole gauge prescription, we should further make a gauge prescription to fix the general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R). From the basic action presented in Eq.(
233
) with gauge-geometry duality, it is reasonable to take the following gauge prescription:

\displaystyle(D_{h}-2)^{-1}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}})-D_{h}^{-1}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathfrak{A}}=0,\;\;\mbox{i.e.},\;\;\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}-\frac{2}{D_{h}}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\ln\mathfrak{A}=0,

(237)

which leads the flowing unitary gauge to be frozen into a usual unitary gauge.

With the above gauge prescription, the action presented in Eq.(
233
) within the framework of GQFT is modified to be as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\mathfrak{A}(x)\{\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(238)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\frac{1}{4}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}})-\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}\mathfrak{A}^{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{P}})\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}})+\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\mathfrak{A}\hat{\partial}_{\mathbf{Q}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{G}}{8}g_{H}^{-2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}[\mathfrak{A}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}})+\frac{D_{h}-2}{D_{h}}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\ln\mathfrak{A}][\mathfrak{A}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}})+\frac{D_{h}-2}{D_{h}}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}\ln\mathfrak{A}]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\},

where we have introduced an additional term with arbitrary constant
\lambda_{G}
to provide the gauge fixing condition.

The above action of hyperunified field theory with imposing a gauge prescription on the general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R) shown in Eq.(
237
) exhibits a manifest gauge-geometry duality within the framework of GQFT. For short, we may call such a gauge prescription as
duality gauge
prescription in hyper-spacetime.

It is clear that the combination of duality gauge for the group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R) in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime with flowing unitary gauge for the gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) in Hilbert space leads to a full gauge prescription, which may be referred to as
entirety unitary gauge
prescription for convenience. By choosing the gauge fixing conditions under entirety unitary gauge, we should be able to remove in principle all unphysical degrees of freedom.

Therefore, to remove all unphysical degrees of freedom, we should take the
entirety unitary gauge
which combines duality gauge and flowing unitary gauge together. Consequently, the fundamental symmetry of hyperunified field theory turns out to be an associated symmetry in which the global inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) is always in association with global Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,
D_{h}
-1) together with global scaling symmetry SC(1), i.e.:

\displaystyle G_{S}=\mbox{SC(1)}\ltimes\mbox{PO(1},D_{h}\mbox{-1)}\,\widetilde{\Join}\,\mbox{WS(1},D_{h}\mbox{-1)},

where the isomorphic group SP(1,
D_{h}
-1)
\cong
SO(1,
D_{h}
-1) must keep coincidental symmetry transformations.

IX.3
Gauge-type and geometric Einstein-type gravitational equations and beyond for the dynamics of gravitational field

Let us first revisit the equation of motion for the hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
. From the hyperunified field theory presented in Eq.(
228
) within the framework of GQFT, we obtain the following equation of motion:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{N}}{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}},

(239)

with the definition,

\displaystyle{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}=-{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-\partial_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}},

(240)

where the tensor
\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}
is defined in Eq.(
V.2
).

We may refer to
{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
as
hyper-gravigauge field strength tensor
.
\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
is called
hyper-gravigauge field current
with the following form:

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\equiv\mathfrak{A}{\mathsf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}-{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\ln\mathfrak{A}\equiv\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}+g_{H}^{2}\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}},

(241)

with
{\mathsf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
the bicovariant vector current defined in Eq.(
116
).
\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
and
\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
are explicitly given as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}})\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}-\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\ln\mathfrak{A},

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\tilde{\mathfrak{L}}+\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\{\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(242)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{B}\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}+\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle g_{W}^{-2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}+\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lambda_{S}^{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}-{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\bar{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}})\,\},

where
\mathsf{G}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
is referred to as
pure hyper-gravigauge field current
caused solely by the hyper-gravigauge field itself.
\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
is referred to as
hyper-gravigauge field bicovariant vector current
resulting from all other basic field in the hyperunified field theory.

We may refer to Eq.(
239
) as
gauge-type gravitational equation
for the hyper-gravigauge field. Such a gauge-type gravitational equation leads to the following conserved current:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}=0,

(243)

due to the antisymmetry property of the hyper-gravigauge field strength tensor. Therefore, in the hyperunified field theory with the action presented in the framework of GQFT, the hyper-gravigauge field does behave as an Abelain gauge field.

To obtain Einstein-type equation in hyper-spacetime, we should utilize Eqs.(
230
) and (
IX.1
) and take into account the gauge-gravity and gravity-geometry correspondences. It can be verified that the gauge-type gravitational equation presented in Eq.(
239
) can be reformulated into the following formalism in light of Ricci curvature tensor
\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
:

\displaystyle-2g_{H}^{-2}\mathfrak{A}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}={\mathsf{J}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}-\mathfrak{A}g_{H}^{-2}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}.

(244)

When projecting the above bicovariant vector tensors into hyper-spacetime tensors by adopting the hyper-gravigauge field as Goldstone-like boson, we are able to obtain two types of dynamic equations of motion.

The first dynamic equation of motion is resulted from the symmetric tensor, which has the following explicit form:

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{R}=-\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{2}\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}},

(245)

and the second dynamic equation of motion is caused from the antisymmetric tensor, which gets the following explicit form:

\displaystyle g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\hat{\nabla}_{\mathbf{P}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}}_{\;\;\;\;\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\mathsf{T}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}.

(246)

It can be checked that the symmetric tensor
\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
presents a generalized Einstein-type symmetric hyper-stress energy-momengtum tensor in hyper-spacetime, which is determined by the hyper-gravigauge field bicovariant vector current
\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}

(247)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}+\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle g_{H}^{-2}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\right)\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\left(\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}-\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\left(\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mathbf{M}}^{(\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q})}\mathbf{A}_{(\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q})\mathbf{N}}-\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\mathbf{M}}^{[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}\mathbf{A}_{[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]\mathbf{N}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\left(\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]\mathbf{N}}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]\mathbf{M}}+2\hat{\nabla}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N})}^{\mathbf{P}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle g_{W}^{-2}\left(\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}-\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}}\right)\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(W_{\mathbf{M}}W_{\mathbf{N}}-\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}W_{\mathbf{P}}W_{\mathbf{Q}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lambda_{S}^{2}\left(\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}-\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}\chi_{s}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}+\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\right)\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}},

where
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
is the hyper-spacetime covariant-gauge field defined in Eq.(
146
). We have used the following notations and definitions,

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{Q}}=-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}},\quad\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{M}}=-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{B}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\;\mathbf{A}})\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad=\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}})-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{P}[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]\mathbf{M}}\equiv\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}}-\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}},\quad\mathbf{A}_{(\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q})\mathbf{M}}\equiv\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\mathbf{M}}^{[\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}]}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}-\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}},\quad\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\mathbf{M}}^{(\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q})}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}}_{\;\;\;\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\hat{\nabla}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}\equiv\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{Q}}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{P}}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathbf{P}}.

(248)

The antisymmetric tensor
\mathsf{T}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}
defines antisymmetric hyper-stress tensor in hyper-spacetime with the following explicit form:

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]}\equiv\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}},

(249)

Therefore, two equations of motion presented in Eqs.(
245
) and (
246
) are referred to as
geometric gravitational equations
. Eq.(
245
) with symmetric tensor is a generalized Einstein-type equation, which is regarded as the extension of general theory of relativity in four-dimensional spacetime to hyperunified field theory in hyper-spacetime. Eq.(
246
) with antisymmetric tensor brings on an additional dynamic equation beyond Einstein-type equation, which arises from hyper-gravigauge field, instead of hyper-gravimetric field, as fundamental gravitational field in the hyperunified field theory with inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry that governs the fundamental interaction of nature.

IX.4
The first oder gravitational differential equation of hyper-gravigauge field from the constraint equation of hyperspin gravigauge field as auxiliary field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime

Let us now turn to the geometry-gauge correspondence in the hidden coordinate formalism of the action for the hyperunified field theory, which leads to the following relations:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}(\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}}+2\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}})=4\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}=-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{A}\,\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\mathfrak{A}\,\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}-2\partial_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}}),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\equiv\mathfrak{A}\,(\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}).

(250)

In such a formalism, the gravitational interaction is solely characterized by the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
which behaves as an auxiliary field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, so that
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
is determined through the constraint equation which concerns only basic bosonic fields as shown in Eqs.(
210
) and (
VIII.3
). Meanwhile, the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
is shown in Eq.(
VIII.3
) to present as the structure factor of non-Abelian Lie group for the hyper-gravicoordinator derivative operator, which brings on the emergence of non-commutative geometry in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. Therefore, the gravitational interaction acts as an emergent interaction in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime characterized by non-commutative geometry.

The hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
is actually presented by Abelian-type gauge field strength
\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}\equiv-\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{D}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}},\quad\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}-\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}.

(251)

When projecting Eq.(
210
) into hyper-spacetime of coordinates by using the hyper-gravigauge field as Goldstone-like bicovariant vector field in biframe hyper-spacetime, we obtain the following constraint equation:

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}-\chi_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}=-\mathsf{T}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{A}},

(252)

where we have introduced the following definitions:

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}[\frac{3}{2}(\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}-\frac{1}{3}\tilde{\chi}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}})

(253)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{B}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}+g_{H}^{2}g_{W}^{-2}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}+g_{H}^{2}g_{W}^{-2}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}})\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}^{\prime}},

and

\displaystyle\chi_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}-2\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}})-\eta_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}-2\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}-\eta_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}.

(254)

In four-dimensional spacetime, such a constraint equation in Eq.(
252
) brings about twenty four first order partial differential equations.

Therefore, we come to the observation that the constraint equation given in Eq.(
210
