) for hyperspin gravigauge field as auxiliary field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime brings on the first order partial differential equation of hyper-gravigauge field in biframe hyper-spacetime, which is regarded as the
first order gravitational differential equation
.

In the special case with keeping only hyperspin gravigauge field by turning away all other fields, the first order gravitational differential equation presented in Eq.(
252
) is simplified to be as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}-\chi_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}]\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}=0,

(255)

which provides the first order gravitational differential equation purely for the hyper-gravigauge field.

IX.5
Dynamic equation of hyper-gravimetric gauge field as geometric gauge-type gravitational equation and the electric-like and magnetic-like gravitational interactions based on gauge-geometry duality

From the gauge-geometry duality, the gravitational interaction is described by Abelian-type hyper-gravigauge field or equivalently by hyper-gravimetric field which can also be regarded as hyper-gravimetric gauge field. We are inspired to investigate the electric-like and magnetic-like gravitational interactions in the hyperunified field theory within the framework of GQFT.

Once regarding
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{P}}
as hyper-gravimetric gauge field, we are able to derive from the action of hyperunified field theory presented in Eq.(
238
) the following
geometric gauge-type gravitational equation
:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{N}}{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}},

(256)

where
{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}.

(257)

which is referred to as
hyper-gravimetric gauge field strength tensor
.
\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
defines the corresponding tensor current,

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\equiv 2\hat{\mathsf{G}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}+2g_{H}^{2}\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}},

(258)

which is referred to as
hyper-gravimetric gauge field current
. Where
\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
is given by the generalized Einstein-type hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
(Eq.(
247
)) as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\equiv\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\;\;\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\;\mathbf{M}},

(259)

and
\hat{\mathsf{G}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
has the following explicit form:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{G}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\ln\mathfrak{A}+\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}

(260)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{M}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{A}^{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}})\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}})+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathfrak{A}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{A}(\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\partial}_{\mathbf{P}}\ln\mathfrak{A}+\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}\ln\mathfrak{A})\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{A}(\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\partial}_{\mathbf{P}}+\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}})\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\mathfrak{A}(\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}+\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{Q}})\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}(\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\hat{\partial}_{\mathbf{P}}+\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{N}}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}})\partial_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{M}}[\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}-2\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{N}}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\mathfrak{A}^{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}})+4\mathfrak{A}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}))

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 4\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{N}}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}(\ln\mathfrak{A})-\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{N}}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{A}}^{2}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{Q}}\partial_{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}})\,],

which defines the
pure hyper-gravimetric gauge field current
resulting just from the hyper-gravimetric gauge field itself.

Such an introduced hyper-gravimetric gauge field current
\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
is a conserved current,

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}=0,

(261)

which is attributed to the antisymmetric property of hyper-gravimetric gauge field strength tensor
{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=-{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{M}}
.

As either geometric gauge-type gravitational equation (
256
) or gauge-type gravitational equation (
239
) is Abelian-type gauge equation in correspondence to hyper-gravimetric gauge field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{P}}
or hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
, we are allowed to introduce electric-like and magnetic-like gravitational field strengths in analogous to Maxwell electromagnetic field strength. For the hyper-gravimetric gauge field strength tensor, let us make the following definitions:

\displaystyle{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{0{\mathbf{I}}}=\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{0\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{{\mathbf{I}}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}=-{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{{\mathbf{I}}0}\equiv-\mathsf{E}_{\mathbf{P}}^{{\mathbf{I}}},

\displaystyle{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{J}}}=\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{{\mathbf{I}}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{{\mathbf{J}}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{P}^{\prime}}\equiv f^{{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{J}}}_{\;\;\;{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{P}}^{{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}},

(262)

where
f^{{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{J}}}_{\;\;{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}
is a constant tensor with the following property:

\displaystyle f^{{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{J}}}_{\;\;{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}=-f^{{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{I}}}_{\;\;{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}=-f^{{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{J}}}_{\;\;\mathbf{L}{\mathbf{K}}},

\displaystyle f^{{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{J}}}_{\;\;{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}f^{{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}_{\;\;{\mathbf{I}}^{\prime}{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{2}(\eta^{{\mathbf{I}}}_{\;{\mathbf{I}}^{\prime}}\eta^{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\;{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}}-\eta^{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\;{\mathbf{I}}^{\prime}}\eta^{{\mathbf{I}}}_{\;{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}}).

(263)

Similar definitions hold for the hyper-gravigauge field strength tensor,

\displaystyle{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{0{\mathbf{I}}}=\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[0{\mathbf{I}}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}=-{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{\mathbf{I}}0}\equiv-\mathsf{E}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{\mathbf{I}}},

\displaystyle{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{J}}}=\tilde{\hat{\mathsf{H}}}^{[{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{J}}]\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\equiv f^{{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{J}}}_{\;\;{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}.

(264)

The gravitational field strengths
\mathsf{E}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{\mathbf{I}}}
and
\mathsf{E}_{\mathbf{P}}^{{\mathbf{I}}}
are regarded as electric-like hyper-gravigauge field strength and hyper-gravimetric gauge field strength, the antisymmetric gravitational field strengths
\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}=-\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{L}{\mathbf{K}}}
and
\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{P}}^{{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}=-\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathbf{L}{\mathbf{K}}}
are viewed as generalized magnetic-like hyper-gravigauge field strength and hyper-gravimetric gauge field strength, respectively.

In terms of the electric-like and magnetic-like gravitational field strengths, the geometric gauge-type gravitational equations can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\partial_{{\mathbf{I}}}\mathsf{E}_{\mathbf{P}}^{{\mathbf{I}}}=-\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{0},

\displaystyle f^{{\mathbf{I}}[{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}]}\partial_{{\mathbf{J}}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{P}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}+f^{{\mathbf{I}}({\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}]}\partial_{{\mathbf{J}}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{P}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}+\partial_{0}\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{P}}^{{\mathbf{I}}}=\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{{\mathbf{I}}},

(265)

and similarly, the gauge-type gravitational equation can be written as follows:

\displaystyle\partial_{{\mathbf{I}}}\mathsf{E}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{\mathbf{I}}}=-\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{0},

\displaystyle f^{{\mathbf{I}}[{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}]}\partial_{{\mathbf{J}}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{A}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}+f^{{\mathbf{I}}({\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}]}\partial_{{\mathbf{J}}}\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{A}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}+\partial_{0}\mathbf{E}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{\mathbf{I}}}=\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{{\mathbf{I}}}.

(266)

We have introduced the following definitions:

\displaystyle f^{{\mathbf{I}}[{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(f^{{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}+f^{{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}{\mathbf{J}}}+f^{{\mathbf{I}}\mathbf{L}{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}}),

\displaystyle f^{{\mathbf{I}}({\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(2f^{{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}}-f^{{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}{\mathbf{J}}}-f^{{\mathbf{I}}\mathbf{L}{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}}),

(267)

where
f^{{\mathbf{I}}[{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}]}
and
f^{{\mathbf{I}}({\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}]}
denote the total antisymmetric tensor and symmetric-antisymmetric mixing tensor, respectively, for the superscripts ‘
{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{K}}\mathbf{L}
’ in the brackets.

As a simple consideration, let us suppose that the hyperunified field theory in hyper-spacetime evolves dynamically into an effective theory in four dimensional spacetime, where the gauge-geometry duality leads the dynamics of gravigauge field to be equivalent to Einstein’s general theory of relativity characterized by gravimetric gauge field. So that, the geometric gauge-type gravitational equation in four dimensional spacetime is simplified to be:

\displaystyle\partial_{i}\mathsf{E}_{\rho}^{i}=-\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\rho}^{0},

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{ij}_{\;\;\;k}\partial_{j}\mathsf{B}_{\rho}^{k}+\partial_{0}\mathsf{E}_{\rho}^{i}=\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\rho}^{i},

(268)

with
i,j,k=1,2,3
and
\varepsilon^{ijk}
the totally antisymmetric constant tensor
\varepsilon^{123}=1
. The electric-like gravimetric field
\mathsf{E}_{\rho}^{i}
and magnetic-like gravimetric field
\mathsf{B}_{\rho}^{i}
are given as follows:

\displaystyle{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\rho}^{0i}=\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{0\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{i\nu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{\rho^{\prime}}\equiv-\mathsf{E}_{\rho}^{i},

\displaystyle{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\rho}^{ij}=\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{i\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{j\nu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{\rho^{\prime}}\equiv\varepsilon^{ij}_{\;\;\;k}\mathsf{B}_{\rho}^{k}.

(269)

By fixing the scaling gauge symmetry to Einstein basis at low energy, the dual gravimetric fields can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{\mu\nu}\equiv M_{\kappa}^{2}(\eta_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{M_{p}}h_{\mu\nu}),\quad M_{p}\equiv\sqrt{2}M_{\kappa}/g_{h},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\nu}=(\mathsf{H}_{\mu\nu})^{-1}=M_{\kappa}^{-2}(\eta^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{M_{p}}\eta^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\eta^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}h_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}+O(\frac{1}{M_{p}^{2}})),

(270)

where
M_{\kappa}
is regarded as
fundamental mass scale
,
M_{p}
and
g_{h}
are considered as the basic mass scale and coupling constant in four-dimensional spacetime.

In an approximation that the inverse of gravimetric gauge field is taken at the leading-order, we obtain the following relations:

\displaystyle{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\rho}^{0i}\simeq M_{\kappa}^{-6}\eta^{0\mu^{\prime}}\eta^{i\nu^{\prime}}\eta_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{\rho^{\prime}}=M_{\kappa}^{-6}\mathsf{F}^{0i}_{\rho}\equiv-M_{\kappa}^{-6}E_{\rho}^{i},

\displaystyle{\hat{\mathsf{F}}}_{\rho}^{ij}\simeq M_{\kappa}^{-6}\eta^{i\mu^{\prime}}\eta^{j\nu^{\prime}}\eta_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{\rho^{\prime}}=M_{\kappa}^{-6}\mathsf{F}^{ij}_{\rho}\equiv M_{\kappa}^{-6}\varepsilon^{ij}_{\;\;\;k}B_{\rho}^{k},

(271)

for the electric-like gravimetric field
E_{\rho}^{i}
and magnetic-like gravimetric field
B_{\rho}^{i}
, and

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\rho}^{\;\mu}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle 2\hat{\mathsf{G}}_{\rho}^{\;\mu}+g_{h}^{2}2\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho}^{\;\mu}\simeq M_{\kappa}^{-6}\hat{J}_{\rho}^{\;\mu},\quad\hat{J}_{\rho}^{\;\mu}=\hat{G}_{\rho}^{\;\mu}+g_{h}^{2}2\hat{T}_{\rho}^{\;\mu},

(272)

for the hyper-gravimetric gauge field current
\hat{J}_{\rho}^{\;\mu}
. Where
\hat{T}_{\rho}^{\;\mu}
represents Einstein-type energy-momentum tensor of matter field in four-dimensional spacetime, which can be deduced from
\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
defined in Eq.(
259
) via the hyper-stress energy-momentum tensor
\mathsf{T}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
given in Eq.(
247
). While
\hat{G}_{\rho}^{\;\mu}
denotes pure gravimetric field tensor, which can be resulted from the tensor
\hat{\mathsf{G}}_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
given in Eq.(
260
) by fixing the scaling gauge symmetry to Einstein basis shown in Eq.(
IX.5
).

In light of the vector notations for both electromagnetic-like gravitational field and current, i.e.,
\mathbf{E}_{\rho}\equiv(E_{\rho}^{1},E_{\rho}^{2},E_{\rho}^{3})
,
\mathbf{B}_{\rho}\equiv(B_{\rho}^{1},B_{\rho}^{2},B_{\rho}^{3})
and
\hat{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\rho}\equiv(\hat{J}_{\rho}^{1},\hat{J}_{\rho}^{2},\hat{J}_{\rho}^{3})
, the geometric gauge-type gravitational equations can be rewritten as follows:

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\mathbf{E}_{\rho}\simeq-\hat{J}_{\rho}^{0},

\displaystyle\nabla\times\mathbf{B}_{\rho}-\partial_{0}\mathbf{E}_{\rho}\simeq-\hat{{\mathbf{J}}}_{\rho}.

(273)

From the Bianchi identity,

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}\mathsf{F}_{\rho}^{\nu\sigma}+\partial_{\nu}\mathsf{F}_{\rho}^{\sigma\mu}+\partial_{\sigma}\mathsf{F}_{\rho}^{\mu\nu}=0,

we obtain the following gauge-type gravitational equations:

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\mathbf{B}_{\rho}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla\times\mathbf{E}_{\rho}+\partial_{0}\mathbf{B}_{\rho}=0.

(274)

Therefore, the Abelian-like geometric gauge-type gravitational equations presented in Eqs.(
IX.5
) and (
IX.5
) in four dimensional spacetime are truly analogous to Maxwell equations of electromagnetic field, which provide electric-like and magnetic-like gravimetric field equations in an appropriate approximation. Unlike the electromagnetic field with source current arising solely from electric charged field, the gravimetric gauge field current
\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\rho}^{\;\mu}
as source current contains not only the current tensor
\hat{\mathsf{T}}_{\rho}^{\;\mu}
arising from all basic matter fields but also from the pure gravimetric gauge field current tensor
\hat{\mathsf{G}}_{\rho}^{\;\mu}
.

X
Conformally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime with evolving graviscalar field as nonsingularity background for evolution of early universe and scaling gauge field and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field as basic constituents of dark universe and quantum cosmic matter in hyperunified field theory

In the previous sections, we have demonstrated that the entirety unitary gauge as an appropriate gauge prescription allows us to remove all unphysical degrees of freedom which are caused from inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) and hidden general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R) in hyperunified field theory built within the framework of gravitational quantum field theory (GQFT)
GQFT
. In this section, we shall discuss various gauge prescriptions for the scaling gauge symmetry in the hyperunified field theory under entirety unitary gauge. In Einstein-type basis, we will show that the conformally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime with evolving graviscalar field provides nonsingularity background spacetime to describe the evolution of the early universe
IE1
;
IE2
;
IE3
;
IE4
. Furthermore, we will demonstrate how the early universe undergoes an inflationary expansion in light of the co-moving cosmic time. In particular, the scaling gauge field is found to be as a dark matter candidate when inflationary universe comes to an end. It is interesting to corroborate that once the potential of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field falls into the minimum, it not only stops inflationary expansion of universe but also provides cosmic energy density as dark energy candidate.

X.1
Hyperunified field theory under entirety unitary gauge with scaling gauge fixing and conformally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime under background basis with fundamental mass scale

It has been shown that the entanglement-correlated and translation-like
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin group symmetry W
{}^{1,D_{h}-1}
is an Abelian-type subgroup of inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) of entangled qubit-spinor field. The
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{A}}
as genesis of hyper-gravigauge field reveals the gauge-gravity correspondence. Meanwhile, the gravity-geometry correspondence in curved Riemannian hyper-spacetime indicates that the gravitational interaction can equivalently be described by the hyper-gravimetric gauge field when fixing the hyperspin gauge symmetry under flowing unitary gauge, so that the hyper-gravimetric gauge field is determined from the symmetric hyper-gravigauge field via the simple relation
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\equiv(\bar{\mathbf{A}})^{2}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
(
\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}=\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}
) in which the symmetric hyper-gravigauge field has the same physical degrees of freedom as hyper-gravimetric gauge field. To remove all unphysical degrees of freedom, the duality gauge prescription for general linear group symmetry GL(1,
D_{h}
-1, R) is proposed to freeze over the flowing unitary gauge for hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,
D_{h}
-1), which leads to the entirety unitary gauge prescription and corroborates the gauge-geometry duality. When taking the gauge fixing conditions under entirety unitary gauge as basic gauge prescription, we are allowed to remove in principle all unphysical degrees of freedom. Eventually, the fundamental symmetry of hyperunified field theory turns out to be an associated symmetry characterized by the global inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,
D_{h}
-1) in association with global Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,
D_{h}
-1) together with scaling symmetry SC(1), where the symmetry transformations of groups SP(1,
D_{h}
-1) and SO(1,
D_{h}
-1) must be coincidental so as to keep the hyperunified field theory under entirety unitary gauge prescription.

The scaling gauge symmetry appears to be a hidden gauge symmetry in the hyperunified field theory expressed in terms of hidden scaling gauge formalism, which indicates that we can in principle choose any convenient scaling gauge fixing condition via appropriate scaling gauge transformation. Namely, under the entirety unitary gauge, we are allowed to further make a typical scaling gauge transformation
\xi_{u}(x)
for both graviscalar field
\phi(x)
and dimensionless symmetric hyper-gravigauge field
\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)
, i.e.:

\displaystyle\phi(x)\to\phi_{u}(x)\equiv\xi_{u}(x)\phi(x),\quad\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}^{u}(x)\equiv\xi_{u}^{-1}(x)\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x),

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}=\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}=\phi(x)\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)\equiv\phi_{u}(x)\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}^{u}(x),

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=(\bar{\mathbf{A}})^{2}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\phi^{2}(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)\equiv\phi_{u}^{2}(x)\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{u}(x),

(275)

so that the determinant of
\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}^{u}(x)
or
\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{u}(x)
is fixed to be the unit,

\displaystyle\chi^{u}\equiv\det(\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}}^{u}(x)\eta^{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}})=\sqrt{\det\left((-1)^{D_{h}-1}\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{u}(x)\right)}=1,

(276)

which is referred to as
unitary basis
for the scaling gauge symmetry.

When fixing the scaling gauge symmetry to be in unitary basis under the entirety unitary gauge, we are able to express the action of the hyperunified field theory given in Eq.(
90
) or Eq.(
228
) and (
233
) into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\{\,\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}^{u}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\phi_{u}\sinh(\chi_{s})\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(277)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\phi_{u}^{D_{h}-4}[-\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4g_{H}^{2}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{C}}^{u\mathbf{M}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{D}}^{u\mathbf{N}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}^{u\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}^{u\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\tilde{\eta}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4g_{H}^{2}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2g_{H}^{2}}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\phi_{u}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4g_{W}^{2}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\phi_{u}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\phi_{u}^{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}\phi_{u}^{4}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})]\,\}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\{\,\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}^{u}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\phi_{u}\sinh(\chi_{s})\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\phi_{u}^{D_{h}-4}[-\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4g_{H}^{2}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle g_{H}^{-2}\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\left(\phi_{u}^{2}\mathsf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{u}-(D_{h}-1)(D_{h}-2)\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\phi_{u}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\phi_{u}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2g_{H}^{2}}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\phi_{u}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4g_{W}^{2}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\phi_{u}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}^{u\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\phi_{u}^{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}\phi_{u}^{4}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})]\,\},

where we have used the following redefinitions:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\to\phi_{u}^{-\frac{D_{h}-1}{2}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\,\partial^{\mathbf{N}}\ln\phi^{u}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\equiv(\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}}^{u\;\mathbf{A}}\hat{\bar{\chi}}^{u\mathbf{P}\mathbf{B}}-\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}}^{u\;\mathbf{B}}\hat{\bar{\chi}}^{u\mathbf{P}\mathbf{A}})\eta_{\mathbf{P}\mathbf{N}},

(278)

and

\displaystyle\chi_{s}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sinh^{-1}\frac{\lambda_{Q}\phi_{1}(x)}{\lambda_{S}\phi_{u}(x)},

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{1}(x)}{\phi_{u}(x)}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}(x),\quad\beta_{Q}\equiv\frac{\lambda_{S}}{\lambda_{Q}}.

(279)

Where
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
is the hyperspin gravigauge field governed by the symmetric hyper-gravigauge field
\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)
in unitary basis. The rank-two tensor
\mathsf{R}^{u}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
is Ricci-type curvature tensor defined from the hyper-gravimetric field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{u}(x)
in unitary basis. In unitary basis, the duality gauge fixing condition given in Eq.(
237
) is simplified to be,

\displaystyle\hat{\partial}^{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{u}=0.

(280)

Such a unitary basis is considered to be a physically meaningful basis in calculating quantum effects within the framework of GQFT.

Alternatively, the scaling gauge invariance also allows us to make a special scaling gauge transformation
\xi_{s}(x)
so as to relate the graviscalar field
\phi(x)
with
fundamental mass scale

M_{\kappa}
as follows:

\displaystyle\phi(x)\to M_{\kappa}\equiv\xi_{s}(x)\phi(x).

(281)

When making the same scaling gauge transformation for
\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)
,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}^{e}(x)\equiv\xi_{s}^{-1}(x)\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x),

(282)

we can rewrite the scaling gauge invariant symmetric hyper-gravigauge field and hyper-gravimetric gauge field as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{M}}(x)\equiv M_{\kappa}\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}^{e}(x),

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\bar{\mathbf{A}})^{2}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)\equiv M_{\kappa}^{2}\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{e}(x),

(283)

which is referred to as Einstein-type basis for the scaling gauge symmetry. Where
\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}^{e}(x)
and
\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{e}(x)
denote the symmetric hyper-gravigauge field and hyper-gravimetric field in
Einstein-type basis
.

When the scaling gauge fixing condition is chosen to be in Einstein-type basis, the action of the hyperunified field theory presented in Eq.(
277
) can be rewritten into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\chi^{e}M_{\kappa}^{D_{h}-4}\{\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\bar{\chi}^{e}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-M_{S}\sinh(\chi_{s})\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(284)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4g_{H}^{2}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{M_{\kappa}^{2}}{g_{H}^{2}}\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{R}^{e}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2g_{H}^{2}}\beta_{G}^{2}M_{S}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4g_{W}^{2}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{Q}^{2}M_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\lambda_{Q}^{2}M_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}M_{\kappa}^{4}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\},

where
\mathsf{R}^{e}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
is Ricci-type curvature tensor defined from the hyper-gravimetric field
\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{e}(x)
in Einstein-type basis. We have also used the following redefinitions:

\displaystyle\chi_{s}(x)\equiv\sinh^{-1}\frac{\phi_{1}(x)}{M_{S}},

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{1}(x)}{M_{S}}\equiv\sinh\chi_{s}(x),

\displaystyle M_{S}\equiv\frac{\lambda_{S}M_{\kappa}}{\lambda_{Q}}=\beta_{Q}M_{\kappa},\quad\beta_{Q}\equiv\frac{\lambda_{S}}{\lambda_{Q}},

(285)

with
M_{S}
regarded as a
fundamental graviscaling mass scale
. Where
\chi_{s}(x)
is characterized by the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field
\phi_{1}(x)
in Einstein-type basis.

X.2
Geometry of gravigauge hyper-spacetime and the dynamics of graviscalar and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar fields with conformally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime as background spacetime

To understand the evolution of universe, let us turn to study the geometry of locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime
{\mathbf{G}}_{h}
. In such a locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, the gravitational interaction is considered as an emergent interaction via non-commutative geometry characterized by the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
shown in Eq.(
VIII.3
), i.e.:

\displaystyle[\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\,,\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{D}}]=\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}\mspace{1mu}\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{A}},

with
\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}
the hyper-gravicoordinate derivative operator in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime
{\mathbf{G}}_{h}
. The hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
as structure factor of non-Abelian Lie algebra is shown to behave as an auxiliary field in the hyperunified field theory formulated in terms of hidden coordinate formalism, which is determined through the constraint equation of hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]}^{\mathbf{A}}
as shown in Eq.(
210
).

Let us now define the gauge and scaling invariant line element in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime with the gravigauge hyper-basis
\{\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}\}
or
\{\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}\}
as follows:

\displaystyle\delta^{2}{\mathcal{S}}\equiv M_{\kappa}^{-2}\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\delta\varkappa^{\mathbf{B}}=\frac{\phi^{2}}{M_{\kappa}^{2}}\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\delta\zeta^{\mathbf{B}},

(286)

where the fundamental mass scale
M_{\kappa}
as multiplying factor is introduced to make the gauge and scaling invariant line element to have the length dimension. Based on the biframe hyper-spacetime structure and biframe displacement correspondence from which the hyper-gravicoordinate displacement for hyper-gravivector field
\varkappa^{\mathbf{A}}(x)
in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime is associated to the ordinary coordinate displacement in globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime through
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field as shown in Eq.(
III.4
), we can express the above invariant line element in terms of the coordinate hyper-basis as follows:

\displaystyle\delta^{2}{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{\kappa}^{-2}dx^{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}dx^{\mathbf{N}}=M_{\kappa}^{-2}dx^{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}dx^{\mathbf{N}}

(287)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\phi^{2}}{M_{\kappa}^{2}}dx^{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}dx^{\mathbf{N}}=\frac{\phi^{2}}{M_{\kappa}^{2}}dx^{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}dx^{\mathbf{N}}\equiv d^{2}l_{S},

with
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
(or
\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}
) the hyper-gravimetric tensor field,

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}=\phi^{2}\chi_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\chi_{\mathbf{N}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}=\phi^{2}\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}.

By taking the special scaling gauge transformation
\xi_{s}(x)
as scaling gauge fixing condition, we can relate the graviscalar field
\phi(x)
to the fundamental mass scale
M_{\kappa}
as follows:

\displaystyle\phi(x)\to\xi_{s}(x)\phi(x)=M_{\kappa},

and meanwhile, the dimensionless symmetric hyper-gravigauge field under the entirety unitary gauge is fixed to be in Einstein-type basis,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)\to\xi^{-1}_{s}(x)\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)=\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}}^{e\;\mathbf{A}}(x).

So that the line element can be rewritten into the following form:

\displaystyle\delta^{2}{\mathcal{S}}\equiv d^{2}l_{S}=\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{e}dx^{\mathbf{M}}dx^{\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{e}(x)=\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}}^{e\,\mathbf{A}}(x)\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{N}}^{e\,\mathbf{B}}(x),

(288)

which defines the line element in Einstein-type basis.

To study the evolution of early universe with the hypothesis of conformally flat hyper-spacetime as background gravigauge hyper-spacetime, we shall analyze the basic properties of background fields and find out their solutions by solving their field equations of motion. It is familiar and useful to work in Einstein-type basis with the following background structure:

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)\rangle=0,\quad\langle\mathsf{W}_{\mu}\rangle=0,\quad\langle\chi_{s}(x)\rangle=\nu_{S}(x),

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)\rangle\equiv M_{\kappa}\langle\bar{\chi}^{e}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}}(x)\rangle=M_{\kappa}a_{s}(x)\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}},

\displaystyle\langle\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)\rangle\equiv M_{\kappa}^{2}\langle\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{e}(x)\rangle=M_{\kappa}^{2}a_{s}^{2}(x)\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)\rangle=\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}^{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}},\quad\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}^{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\equiv\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}-\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{P}}^{\;\mathbf{B}},

(289)

where
a_{s}(x)
is regarded to be a
background graviscaling field
and
\nu_{S}(x)
characterizes the scaling invariant
background
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
. From the gravitational origin of hyperspin gauge symmetry through the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
, we can obtain the following relations:

\displaystyle\langle\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)\rangle=\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}^{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}},\quad\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}\equiv\partial^{\mathbf{P}}\ln a_{s}(x),

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}\equiv\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}+\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}}=\partial^{\mathbf{P}}\ln a_{s}(x)+\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}},

(290)

where
\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{P}}
reflects the background feature of hyperspin gauge field and
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}}
denotes a possible difference from the background feature of hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}
.

Such a background structure forms
background gravigauge hyper-spacetime
with the following background line element:

\displaystyle\langle d^{2}l_{S}\rangle=\eta_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\langle\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{M}}^{e\,\mathbf{A}}(x)\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{N}}^{e\,\mathbf{B}}(x)\rangle dx^{\mathbf{M}}dx^{\mathbf{N}}=a_{s}^{2}(x)\,\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\,dx^{\mathbf{M}}dx^{\mathbf{N}},

(291)

which brings on
conformally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime
governed by the background graviscaling field
a_{s}(x)
. Such a background gravigauge hyper-spacetime distinguishes from globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime introduced as inertial reference frame of coordinates. Both Ricci-type tensor and Ricci-type scalar tensor are found to have the following forms:

\displaystyle\langle\mathsf{R}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{e}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(D_{h}-2)d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}+\left(d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}+(D_{h}-1)\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}\right)\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(D_{h}-2)\left(\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\ln a_{s}-\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\ln a_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\ln a_{s}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(\partial^{2}\ln a_{s}+(D_{h}-2)\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\ln a_{s}\partial^{\mathbf{P}}\ln a_{s}\right)\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle\langle\mathsf{R}^{e}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(D_{h}-1)d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}+D_{h}(D_{h}-1)\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(D_{h}-2)\partial^{2}\ln a_{s}+(D_{h}-1)(D_{h}-2)\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\ln a_{s}\partial^{\mathbf{P}}\ln a_{s},

\displaystyle d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}=\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\ln a_{s}-\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\ln a_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\ln a_{s},

(292)

and the background field strength of hyperspin gauge field is given by,

\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\rangle=d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}\eta_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}}^{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-d_{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}^{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]},

\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\rangle=4(D_{h}-2)d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}d^{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{N}}+4(d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{M}})^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\;\;+8(D_{h}-1)d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{N}}+2D_{h}(D_{h}-1)(\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{N}})^{2},

\displaystyle d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}\equiv\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}-\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}.

(293)

From the equation of motion presented in Eq.(
102
) for the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)
, we arrive at the following equation of motion for the background field
\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)\rangle=\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}^{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}
:

\displaystyle[d_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}-d_{\mathbf{Q}}d^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}-2\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}d^{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{Q}}-(D_{h}-4)\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{Q}}d^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}+(D_{h}-4)\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{Q}}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}}]\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}^{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}

\displaystyle+[d^{\mathbf{N}}d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}-\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}d^{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}-(D_{h}-4)d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathbf{N}}]\eta_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{P}}^{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}=\beta_{G}^{2}M_{S}^{2}a_{s}^{2}\sinh^{2}\nu_{S}\,\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}}\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{P}}^{[\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}.

(294)

Following along the equation of motion presented in Eq.(
105
) or Eq.(
239
) for the hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}(x)
and also the alternative equation of motion shown in Eq.(
245
) or Eq.(
256
) for the hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}(x)
, we obtain the following equation of motion for the background field:

\displaystyle M_{\kappa}^{2}a_{s}^{2}\{(D_{h}-2)d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}-[(D_{h}-2)d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}+\frac{1}{2}(D_{h}-1)(D_{h}-2)\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}]\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\}

\displaystyle\quad=(D_{h}-3)d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{P}}d_{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}-d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}d^{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}+(d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{N}}+d_{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{M}})\left(d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}+(D_{h}-2)\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-[\,\frac{1}{2}(D_{h}-4)d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}d^{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{Q}}+(D_{h}-2)d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{Q}})+\frac{1}{4}(D_{h}+1)(D_{h}-4)(\mathsf{A}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}(d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}})^{2}]\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{G}^{2}M_{S}^{2}\sinh^{2}\nu_{S}\,[\left(2d_{\mathbf{P}}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}}+(D_{h}-1)\tilde{\mathsf{A}}_{\mathbf{P}}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}}+(D_{h}-2)\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{P}}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}}\right)\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle\quad-d_{\mathbf{M}}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}_{\mathbf{N}}-d_{\mathbf{N}}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}-(D_{h}-1)\left(\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}_{\mathbf{N}}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}-2\tilde{\mathsf{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}\tilde{\mathsf{A}}_{\mathbf{N}}\right)]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{2}M_{\kappa}^{2}a_{s}^{2}[\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\nu_{S}\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\nu_{S}-(\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\nu_{S}\partial^{\mathbf{P}}\nu_{S}-\lambda_{D}^{2}M_{\kappa}^{2}a_{s}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S}))\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}],

(295)

where
{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})
represents the total dimensionless potential function.

The equation of motion for the dimensionless scaling field
\chi_{s}(x)
presented in Eq.(
110
) leads to the following equation of motion for the background
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\nu_{S}(x)
:

\displaystyle\partial^{2}\nu_{S}+(D_{h}-2)\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\nu_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\lambda_{D}^{2}\lambda_{S}^{-2}M_{\kappa}^{2}a_{s}^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu_{S}}{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})

(296)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2(D_{h}-1)g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\lambda_{Q}^{-2}\sinh\nu_{S}\cosh\nu_{S}\,\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{2}.

It is still hard to solve analytically the above equations of motion in the general case. In particular, the equations of motion for the background hyperspin gauge field and hyper-gravigauge field remain to be quite complicated. We should examine the most interesting case by firstly concentrating on the equation of motion for the background
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\nu_{S}(x)
. For that, let us begin with considering rational hypotheses for the total dimensionless potential function and background hyperspin gauge field
\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}
.

For the potential function
{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})
of background
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\nu_{S}(x)
, it is proposed to have the following feature:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu_{S}}{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})\to 0,\quad{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})\to 1,\quad\nu_{S}\gg 1,

(297)

which indicates that
{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})
is expected to vary slowly as the function of background
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\nu_{S}(x)
for
\nu_{S}\gg 1
, so that the potential function can approximately be regarded as a slowly varying cosmological constant.

For the background hyperspin gauge field
\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}
, it is simply supposed to be the same as the background hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}
based on the gravitational origin of hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
, which indicates the following relation:

\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\rangle\to 0,\quad\mbox{i.e.}\quad\mathsf{A}^{\mathbf{P}}-\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}\equiv\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}}\to 0.

(298)

From the above hypotheses and analyses, we arrive at the following simplified equation for the background
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\nu_{S}(x)
:

\displaystyle\partial^{2}\nu_{S}+(D_{h}-2)\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\nu_{S}\simeq 0,

(299)

which brings on the following solution in a good approximation,

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\nu_{S}\simeq 0.

(300)

Meanwhile, the equation of motion for the background hyperspin gauge field
\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(x)\rangle
presented in Eq.(
X.2
) is simplified for
\tilde{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathbf{P}}\to 0
to be as follows:

\displaystyle d_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}-d_{\mathbf{Q}}d^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}-2\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{Q}}d^{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{Q}}-(D_{h}-4)\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{Q}}d^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}\to 0,

(301)

which can be rewritten into the following form:

\displaystyle 3\partial^{2}a_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}a_{s}-a_{s}\partial^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}a_{s}-(D_{h}-3)(a_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}a_{s}-2\partial_{\mathbf{P}}a_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}a_{s})\partial^{\mathbf{Q}}\ln a_{s}=0.

(302)

It can be checked that the equation of motion for the background hyper-gravigauge field presented in Eq.(
X.2
) can be simplified in a good approximation into the following two equations:

\displaystyle M_{\kappa}^{2}a_{s}^{2}(D_{h}-2)d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}\simeq(D_{h}-3)d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{P}}d_{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}-d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}}d^{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle\quad+(d_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{N}}+d_{\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}})\left(d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}+(D_{h}-2)\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}\right)

\displaystyle M_{\kappa}^{2}a_{s}^{2}[(D_{h}-2)d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}+\frac{1}{2}(D_{h}-1)(D_{h}-2)\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}]

\displaystyle\quad\simeq\frac{1}{2}(D_{h}-4)d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{Q}}d^{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{Q}}+(D_{h}-2)d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{Q}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4}(D_{h}+1)(D_{h}-4)(\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}})^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}})^{2}+\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{D}^{2}M_{\kappa}^{4}a_{s}^{4}.

(303)

To obtain an analytic solution, let us first consider the simplified equation (
302
). Suppose that Eq.(
302
) holds in any dimension
D_{h}
of hyper-spacetime, which leads the background scaling factor
a_{s}(x)
to satisfy the following equations:

\displaystyle a_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}a_{s}-2\partial_{\mathbf{P}}a_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}a_{s}=0,

\displaystyle 3\partial^{2}a_{s}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}a_{s}-a_{s}\partial^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{P}}a_{s}=0,

(304)

which are found to be equivalent to the following two identities:

\displaystyle d_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{Q}}=0,\quad 2\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\mathbf{Q}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{Q}}-\partial^{2}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}=0.

(305)

With these two identities, two equations in Eq.(
X.2
) can be simplified further into the following single equation:

\displaystyle M_{\kappa}^{2}a_{s}^{2}(D_{h}-1)(D_{h}-2)\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}}\simeq g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{D}^{2}M_{\kappa}^{4}a_{s}^{4}+\frac{1}{2}(D_{h}+1)(D_{h}-4)(\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathsf{\Omega}^{\mathbf{P}})^{2}.

(306)

A general solution for the background scaling factor
a_{s}(x)
is found to be as follows:

\displaystyle a_{s}(x)=\pm\frac{\alpha_{\kappa}m_{\kappa}/M_{\kappa}}{1\mp\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}(x^{\mathbf{M}}-x_{o}^{\mathbf{M}})},

(307)

where
\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}
is regarded as
basic cosmic vector
, which defines
fundamental cosmic mass scale

m_{\kappa}
via the Lorentz-type invariant scalar product as follows:

\displaystyle\kappa^{2}=\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}\kappa^{\mathbf{M}}\equiv m_{\kappa}^{2}c_{\kappa}^{2},

(308)

where
c_{\kappa}
may be referred to as
invariant proper speed
for convenience of mention (we will take it as a basic unit with the convention
c_{\kappa}=1
).
x_{o}^{\mathbf{M}}
is viewed as a reference coordinate position chosen appropriately by an observer in conformally flat background hyper-spacetime, which leads the solution to be translational invariant,

\displaystyle x^{\mathbf{M}}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}=x^{\mathbf{M}}+\varpi^{\mathbf{M}},\quad x_{o}^{\mathbf{M}}\to x_{o}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}=x_{o}^{\mathbf{M}}+\varpi^{\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle a_{s}(x)\to a_{s}(x+\varpi)=a_{s}(x).

(309)

It can be verified that the general solution presented in Eq.(
307
) becomes consistent when the constant
\alpha_{\kappa}
meets to the constraint obtained from the equation given in Eq.(
306
), i.e.:

\displaystyle 4g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{D}^{2}\alpha_{\kappa}^{4}-4(D_{h}-1)(D_{h}-2)\alpha_{\kappa}^{2}+(D_{h}+1)(D_{h}-4)=0,

(310)

which implies that the constant
\alpha_{\kappa}
depends not only on the coupling constants but also on the dimension of hyper-spacetime. For convenience of mention,
\alpha_{\kappa}
is referred to as
conformal scaling constant
.

It is easy to check that the equations of motion have a discrete symmetry under
Z_{2}
transformation:
a_{s}(x)\to-a_{s}(x)
, and also reflection symmetry for coordinates:
x^{\mathbf{M}}\to-x^{\mathbf{M}}
,
a_{s}(x)\to a_{s}(-x)
. So that there are in general four solutions for the background graviscalar field,

\displaystyle\phi_{o}(x)\equiv M_{\kappa}a_{s}(x)\to\pm\phi_{o}^{\pm}(x)=\pm\frac{\alpha_{\kappa}m_{\kappa}}{1\mp\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}(x^{\mathbf{M}}-x_{o}^{\mathbf{M}})},

(311)

where the conformal scaling constant
\alpha_{\kappa}
is easily obtained from Eq.(
310
) as follows:

\displaystyle\alpha_{\kappa}^{2}=\frac{(D_{h}-1)(D_{h}-2)}{2g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{D}^{2}}\{1\pm\left(1-\frac{(D_{h}+1)(D_{h}-4)g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{D}^{2}}{(D_{h}-1)^{2}(D_{h}-2)^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\}.

(312)

It is obvious that when hyper-spacetime is reduced to be four-dimensional one, the conformal scaling constant
\alpha_{\kappa}
gets the usual solution in four dimensional spacetime
GQFT
,

\displaystyle\alpha_{\kappa}=\frac{\sqrt{6}}{(g_{H}\lambda_{D})}.

(313)

It is interesting to notice that for hyper-spacetime beyond four dimensions (
D-h>4
), the general solution Eq.(
307
) for the background scaling factor is still valid even if the potential of background
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field is absent (
\lambda_{Q}=0
). In this case, the conformal scaling constant
\alpha_{\kappa}
is simply given by,

\displaystyle\alpha_{\kappa}^{2}=\frac{(D_{h}+1)(D_{h}-4)}{4(D_{h}-1)(D_{h}-2)}.

(314)

In fact, for
g_{H}\lambda_{D}\lesssim O(1)
, the two solutions in Eq.(
312
) can be expressed in a good approximation as follows:

\displaystyle\alpha_{\kappa+}^{2}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{(D_{h}-1)(D_{h}-2)}{g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{D}^{2}}[\,1-\frac{(D_{h}+1)(D_{h}-4)g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{D}^{2}}{2(D_{h}-1)^{2}(D_{h}-2)^{2}}\,],

\displaystyle\alpha_{\kappa-}^{2}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{(D_{h}+1)(D_{h}-4)}{4(D_{h}-1)(D_{h}-2)}[\,1+\frac{(D_{h}+1)(D_{h}-4)g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{D}^{2}}{2(D_{h}-1)^{2}(D_{h}-2)^{2}}\,].

(315)

For the hyper-spacetime dimension
D_{h}=19
in the hyperunified field theory, we can write down the conformal scaling constant
\alpha_{\kappa}
as follows:

\displaystyle\alpha_{\kappa+}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{3\sqrt{34}}{g_{H}\lambda_{D}}\left(\,1-\frac{150g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{D}^{2}}{18^{2}\cdot 17^{2}}\,\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\alpha_{\kappa-}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{5\sqrt{3}}{3\sqrt{34}}\left(\,1+\frac{150g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{D}^{2}}{18^{2}\cdot 17^{2}}\,\right)^{1/2}.

(316)

X.3
Evolution of early universe with evolving graviscalar and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field in conformally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime as nonsingularity background spacetime

To study the evolution of early universe, let us demonstrate firstly the basic property of background gravigauge hyper-spacetime with the following background line element:

\displaystyle\langle d^{2}l_{S}\rangle=a_{s}^{2}(x)\,dx^{\mathbf{M}}\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}dx^{\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle a_{s}^{2}(x)\to(a_{s}^{\pm}(x))^{2}=\frac{\alpha_{\kappa}^{2}m_{\kappa}^{2}/M_{\kappa}^{2}}{\left(1\mp\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}(x^{\mathbf{M}}-x_{o}^{\mathbf{M}})\right)^{2}},

(317)

which is invariant under the following Poincaré-type transformation:

\displaystyle x^{\mathbf{M}}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}=L^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\,x^{\mathbf{N}},\quad x_{o}^{\mathbf{M}}\to x_{o}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}=L^{\mathbf{M}}_{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\,x_{o}^{\mathbf{N}},\quad\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}\to\kappa^{\prime}_{\mathbf{M}}=L_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\;\mathbf{N}}\,\kappa_{\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle x^{\mathbf{M}}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}=x^{\mathbf{M}}+\varpi^{\mathbf{M}},\quad x_{o}^{\mathbf{M}}\to x_{o}^{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{M}}=x_{o}^{\mathbf{M}}+\varpi^{\mathbf{M}}.

(318)

In general, the existence of basic cosmic vector
\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}
indicates that the background gravigauge hyper-spacetime is not isometric and homogeneous.

The Poincaré-type invariance of the scalar product
\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}(x^{\mathbf{M}}-x_{o}^{\mathbf{M}})
enables us to define a
conformal proper time

\hat{t}
as follows:

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}(x^{\mathbf{M}}-x_{o}^{\mathbf{M}})\equiv\hat{m}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\hat{t}-\hat{t}_{o}),

(319)

where
\hat{m}_{\kappa}
is regarded as
conformal proper cosmic mass scale
,
\hat{t}_{o}
is considered as
reference conformal proper time
. In general, the definitions of
\hat{t}
(
\hat{t}_{o}
) and
\hat{m}_{\kappa}
allow to have the following intrinsic conformal scaling invariant transformation:

\displaystyle\hat{t}\to\hat{t}^{\prime}=\lambda^{-\alpha}\,\hat{t},\quad\hat{t}_{o}\to\hat{t}^{\prime}_{o}=\lambda^{-\alpha}\,\hat{t}_{o},\quad\hat{m}_{\kappa}\to\hat{m}_{\kappa}^{\prime}=\lambda^{\alpha}\hat{m}_{\kappa},

(320)

where the power
\alpha
is constant parameter. For
\kappa^{2}=\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}\kappa^{\mathbf{M}}=m_{\kappa}^{2}>0
, one can always choose a typical reference frame by making a special Lorentz-type transformation, so that

\displaystyle\kappa_{{\mathbf{I}}}=0,\quad{\mathbf{I}}=1,2,\cdots,D_{h},

\displaystyle\hat{m}_{\kappa}=\kappa_{0}=m_{\kappa},\quad\hat{t}=\eta,\;\hat{t}_{o}=\eta_{o},\quad\alpha=0,

(321)

which is referred to as
cosmic co-moving reference frame
. Where
\eta
is referred to as
cosmic co-moving Lorentz time
and
\eta_{o}
as
cosmic co-moving reference Lorentz time
. Only in such a cosmic co-moving reference frame, the intrinsic conformal scaling transformation is fixed with
\alpha=0
.

In light of conformal proper time
\hat{t}
, the temporal coordinate
x^{0}
can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle x^{0}=\frac{\hat{m}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}}{\kappa_{0}}\,c_{\kappa}\hat{t}-\frac{\kappa_{I}}{\kappa_{0}}x^{I}\equiv\frac{1}{u_{0}}(c_{\kappa}\hat{t}-u_{I}x^{I}),

\displaystyle u_{0}\equiv\frac{\kappa_{0}}{\hat{m}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}}\,,\quad u_{I}\equiv\frac{\kappa_{I}}{\hat{m}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}},

(322)

so that the coordinates
x^{\mathbf{M}}
and displacement
dx^{0}
are replaced by,

\displaystyle x^{\mathbf{M}}\to\hat{x}^{\mathbf{M}}=(c_{\kappa}\hat{t},x^{I}),

\displaystyle dx^{0}=\frac{1}{u_{0}}(c_{\kappa}d\hat{t}-u_{I}dx^{I}).

(323)

The line element can be rewritten as follows:

\displaystyle\langle d^{2}l_{S}\rangle=a_{s}^{2}(\hat{t})d\hat{x}^{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{c}d\hat{x}^{\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle a_{s}^{2}(x)\to\left(a_{s}^{\pm}(\hat{t})\right)^{2}=\frac{\alpha_{\kappa}^{2}m_{\kappa}^{2}/M_{\kappa}^{2}}{\left(1\mp\hat{m}_{\kappa}(\hat{t}-\hat{t}_{o})c_{\kappa}^{2}\right)^{2}},

(324)

where
a_{s}(\hat{t})
evolves as the graviscaling field of conformal proper time
\hat{t}
with
\hat{x}^{\mathbf{M}}
as the coordinates in terms of conformal proper time
\hat{t}
.
\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{c}
is the
conformal proper time background metric
with the following form:

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{c}=\frac{1}{u_{0}^{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&-u_{J}\\
-u_{I}&u_{0}^{2}\chi_{IJ}^{c}\end{array}\right),\quad\chi_{IJ}^{c}=\eta_{IJ}+\frac{u_{I}u_{J}}{u_{0}^{2}},

which is determined by the basic cosmic vector
\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}
.

In general, the conformal proper time background metric is non-diagonal. Only in the cosmic co-moving reference frame, the background gravigauge hyper-spacetime becomes isotropic and homogenous with the following features:

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}\to\kappa_{0}=m_{\kappa}c_{\kappa},\quad\hat{t}\to\eta,\quad\hat{x}^{\mathbf{M}}\to x_{\eta}^{\mathbf{M}}=(c_{\kappa}\eta,x^{I}),\quad d\hat{x}^{\mathbf{M}}\to dx_{\eta}^{\mathbf{M}},

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{c}\to\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}},\quad\langle d^{2}l_{S}\rangle=a_{s}^{2}(\hat{t})d\hat{x}^{\mathbf{M}}\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{c}d\hat{x}^{\mathbf{N}}=a_{s}^{2}(\eta)dx_{\eta}^{\mathbf{M}}\eta_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}dx_{\eta}^{\mathbf{N}},

(328)

where the Lorentz-type symmetry is fixed to specify both Lorentz time
t
and conformal proper time
\hat{t}
to be the same as cosmic co-moving Lorentz time, i.e.,
\hat{t}=t=\eta
.

Let us now investigate the feature of background graviscaling field in the case
\nu_{S}(x)\gg 1
. Formally, it appears to have a singularity when the conformal proper time or cosmic co-moving Lorentz time relative to its reference time approaches to the epoch given by the inverse of conformal proper cosmic mass scale or fundamental cosmic mass scale. Namely, when the conformal proper time or cosmic co-moving Lorentz time goes to the following critical point:

\displaystyle\hat{t}\to\hat{t}_{c}^{\pm}\equiv\hat{t}_{o}\pm\frac{1}{\hat{m}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}}\equiv\hat{t}_{o}\pm\hat{t}_{\kappa},\quad\hat{t}_{\kappa}\equiv\frac{1}{\hat{m}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}},\quad\mbox{or}

\displaystyle\eta\to\eta_{c}^{\pm}\equiv\eta_{o}\pm\frac{1}{m_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}}\equiv\eta_{o}\pm\eta_{\kappa},\quad\eta_{\kappa}\equiv\frac{1}{m_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}},

(329)

the conformal size of background gravigauge hyper-spacetime becomes infinitely large,

\displaystyle a_{s}(\eta)\to a_{s}^{\pm}(\eta_{c}^{\pm}),\;\;a_{s}^{\pm}(\hat{t}_{c}^{\pm})\rightarrow\infty.

(330)

In this situation, the distance relative to a reference point traveled by a field with invariant proper speed
c_{\kappa}
is given as follows:

\displaystyle L_{\kappa}=\eta_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}=\frac{1}{m_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}},\quad\mbox{or}\quad\hat{L}_{\kappa}=\hat{t}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}=\frac{1}{\hat{m}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}},

(331)

where
L_{\kappa}
is referred to as
co-moving cosmic horizon
or
\hat{L}_{\kappa}
as
conformal proper cosmic horizon
in background gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

Nevertheless, we will show that in view of
conformal proper cosmic time
or
co-moving cosmic time
, it takes an infinitely long time to reach the conformal proper cosmic horizon
\hat{L}_{\kappa}
or co-moving cosmic horizon
L_{\kappa}
. Let us now introduce
conformal proper cosmic time

\hat{\tau}
and
co-moving cosmic time

\tau
as follows:

\displaystyle d\hat{\tau}\equiv a_{s}(\hat{t})d\hat{t},\quad\mbox{or}\quad d\tau=a_{s}(\eta)d\eta,

\displaystyle a_{s}(\hat{t})\to a_{s}^{\pm}(\hat{t})=\frac{\alpha_{\kappa}m_{\kappa}/M_{\kappa}}{1\mp\hat{m}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\hat{t}-\hat{t}_{o})}=\frac{\alpha_{\kappa}m_{\kappa}/M_{\kappa}}{1\mp c_{\kappa}(\hat{t}-\hat{t}_{o})/\hat{L}_{\kappa}},

\displaystyle a_{s}(\eta)\to a_{s}^{\pm}(\eta)=\frac{\alpha_{\kappa}m_{\kappa}/M_{\kappa}}{1\mp m_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\eta-\eta_{o})}=\frac{\alpha_{\kappa}m_{\kappa}/M_{\kappa}}{1\mp c_{\kappa}(\eta-\eta_{o})/L_{\kappa}},

(332)

which enables us to obtain the following relation between the conformal proper time
\hat{t}
and conformal proper cosmic time
\hat{\tau}
or between the cosmic co-moving Lorentz time
\eta
and co-moving cosmic time
\tau
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\hat{m}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\hat{t}-\hat{t}_{o})}=e^{\hat{H}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\hat{\tau}-\hat{\tau}_{o})},\;\;-\infty\leq c_{\kappa}(\hat{t}-\hat{t}_{o})\leq\hat{L}_{\kappa},\;\;-\infty\leq\hat{\tau}-\hat{\tau}_{o}\leq\infty,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-m_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\eta-\eta_{o})}=e^{H_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\tau-\tau_{o})},\;\;-\infty\leq c_{\kappa}(\eta-\eta_{o})\leq L_{\kappa},\;\;-\infty\leq\tau-\tau_{o}\leq\infty,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\hat{m}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\hat{t}-\hat{t}_{o})}=e^{-\hat{H}_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\hat{\tau}-\hat{\tau}_{o})},\;\;-\hat{L}_{\kappa}\leq c_{\kappa}(\hat{t}-\hat{t}_{o})\leq\infty,\;\;-\infty\leq\hat{\tau}-\hat{\tau}_{o}\leq\infty,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+m_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\eta-\eta_{o})}=e^{-H_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\tau-\tau_{o})},\;\;-L_{\kappa}\leq c_{\kappa}(\eta-\eta_{o})\leq\infty,\;\;-\infty\leq\tau-\tau_{o}\leq\infty,

(333)

where the normalization is fixed by the integration condition at initial points, i.e,
\hat{t}=\hat{t}_{o}
and
\hat{\tau}=\hat{\tau}_{o}
or
\eta=\eta_{o}
and
\tau=\tau_{o}
when carrying out the integrations over
\hat{t}
and
\hat{\tau}
or
\eta
and
\tau
.
\tau_{o}
is referred to as
co-moving reference cosmic time
and
\hat{\tau}_{o}
as
conformal proper reference cosmic time
.

The conformal scaling factor in light of conformal proper cosmic time or co-moving cosmic time is found to have the following form:

\displaystyle a_{s}(\hat{\tau})\to a_{s}^{\pm}(\hat{\tau})=a_{o}e^{\pm\hat{H}_{\kappa}c(\hat{\tau}-\hat{\tau}_{o})}=a_{o}e^{\pm H_{\kappa}c(\tau-\tau_{o})}=a_{s}^{\pm}(\tau),

\displaystyle a_{o}=\alpha_{\kappa}\frac{m_{\kappa}}{M_{\kappa}}\equiv\frac{m_{\kappa}}{H_{\kappa}}=\frac{l_{\kappa}}{L_{\kappa}}\equiv\frac{\hat{m}_{\kappa}}{\hat{H}_{\kappa}}=\frac{\hat{l}_{\kappa}}{\hat{L}_{\kappa}},

(334)

where
a_{o}
is regarded as
reference conformal scaling factor
at
co-moving reference cosmic time
. We have introduced the following definitions:

\displaystyle H_{\kappa}\equiv\frac{M_{\kappa}}{\alpha_{\kappa}},\quad l_{\kappa}\equiv\frac{1}{H_{\kappa}},

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\kappa}\equiv\frac{M_{\kappa}}{\alpha_{\kappa}}\frac{\hat{m}_{\kappa}}{m_{\kappa}},\quad\hat{l}_{\kappa}\equiv\frac{1}{\hat{H}_{\kappa}},

(335)

where
H_{\kappa}
is referred to as
primordial Hubble-type mass scale
and
\hat{H}_{\kappa}
as
conformal proper primordial Hubble-type mass scale
, and
l_{\kappa}
is referred to as
primordial Hubble-type length scale
and
\hat{l}_{\kappa}
as
conformal proper primordial Hubble-type length scale
.

It is noted that the conformal scaling factor
a_{s}(x)
is regarded as
intrinsic scaling invariant quantity
with
a_{s}(\hat{\tau})=a_{s}(\tau)
, while the conformal proper primordial Hubble-type mass scale
\hat{H}_{\kappa}
is not an intrinsic scaling invariant quantity as its value depends on the choice of conformal proper cosmic time
\hat{\tau}
. Only in the cosmic co-moving reference frame, the conformal proper primordial Hubble-type mass scale
\hat{H}_{\kappa}
turns to be the primordial Hubble-type mass scale
H_{\kappa}=M_{\kappa}/\alpha_{\kappa}
which is regarded as
primordial Hubble-type constant
.

It is clear that when the distance traveled via the invariant proper speed
c_{\kappa}
in background spacetime is closing to the co-moving cosmic horizon
c_{\kappa}|\eta-\eta_{o}|\to L_{\kappa}
, it needs to take an infinitely large co-moving cosmic time
|\tau-\tau_{o}|\to\infty
.

The line element in light of the conformal proper cosmic time can be expresses as follows:

\displaystyle\langle d^{2}l_{S}\rangle=\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}}^{c}(\hat{\tau})d\hat{x}_{\tau}^{\mathbf{M}}d\hat{x}_{\tau}^{\mathbf{N}},\quad\hat{x}_{\tau}^{\mathbf{M}}=(c\hat{\tau},x^{I}),

(336)

where the metric tensor
\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{c}(\hat{\tau})
is given by,

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{c}(\hat{\tau})=\frac{1}{u_{0}^{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&-a_{s}(\hat{\tau})\,u_{J}\\
-a_{s}(\hat{\tau})\,u_{I}&a_{s}^{2}(\hat{\tau})\,u_{0}^{2}\,\chi_{IJ}^{c}\end{array}\right),\quad\chi_{IJ}^{c}=\eta_{IJ}+\frac{u_{I}u_{J}}{u_{0}^{2}},

which indicates that the background gravigauge hyper-spacetime is in general not isotropic in terms of the conformal proper cosmic time
\hat{\tau}
.

Only in the cosmic co-moving reference frame with
u_{I}=0
and
u_{0}=1
, the co-moving cosmic time
\tau
defined from the cosmic co-moving Lorentz time
\eta
describes an isotropic and homogeneous background gravigauge hyper-spacetime with the following line element:

\displaystyle\langle d^{2}l_{S}\rangle=\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{c}(\tau)dx_{\tau}^{\mathbf{M}}dx_{\tau}^{\mathbf{N}}=c_{\kappa}^{2}d\tau^{2}-a_{s}^{2}(\tau)(dx^{I})^{2},

(340)

with the definitions,

\displaystyle x_{\tau}^{\mathbf{M}}=(c_{\kappa}\tau,x^{I}),\quad\chi_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}^{c}(\tau)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\
0&a_{s}^{2}(\tau)\eta_{IJ}\end{array}\right).

For the positive graviscaling factor,

\displaystyle a_{s}^{+}(\tau)=a_{o}\,e^{H_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\tau-\tau_{o})},

(344)

which brings on the well-known Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker (FLRW) metric for describing inflationary expansion of early universe as the time arrow from the past or reference point to the future. Obviously, the negative solution for the graviscaling factor
a_{s}^{-}(\tau)=a_{o}\,e^{-H_{\kappa}c_{\kappa}^{2}(\tau-\tau_{o})}
characterizes deflationary expansion of early universe when the time arrow from the past or reference point to the future. Whereas once regarding such a negative solution as a time reversal one, it reflects inflationary expansion of universe for the time arrow from the future or reference point to the past.

In general, such an inflationary expansion of early universe is going to break down and end up eventually only when the potential
{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})
of background
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\nu_{S}(x)
falls into a minimum value, where the background field
\nu_{S}(x)
decreases and approaches to its minimum point
\nu_{S}(x)\to\nu_{0}
, i.e.:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\nu_{S})\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu_{S}}{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})=0,\quad\mbox{at}\quad\nu_{S}=\nu_{0},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S}=\nu_{0})\ll 1.

(345)

So that the background graviscaling field as conformal scaling factor will not get into an infinitely large value since an inflationary expansion of early universe will stop before the conformal proper time (or cosmic co-moving Lorentz time) relative to its reference time reaches to the singularity point characterized by the conformal proper cosmic horizon (or co-moving cosmic horizon).

Let us suppose that the universe evolves from conformally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime as nonsingularity background spacetime in which the potential of background
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field varies slowly for a large value
\langle\chi_{s}(x)\rangle=\nu_{S}(x)\gg 1
. Begin with such a nonsingularity background hyper-spacetime as inflationary early universe, the hyperunified qubit-spinor field and hyperspin gauge field as well as
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field and scaling gauge field behave as very heavy massive particles, so that all fundamental particles except hyper-gravigauge field are considered to decouple from interactions at the very beginning of universe. Nevertheless, when approaching to the end of inflationary expansion of early universe once the potential reaches to the minimum point
\nu_{S}=\nu_{0}
with
\nu_{0}\ll 1
, the hyperunified qubit-spinor field and hyperspin gauge field are expected to receive negligible masses in proportional to an extra small factor
\sinh\nu_{S}\simeq\nu_{S}=\nu_{0}\ll 1
.

It is noted that the above analytic solution for the background graviscaling field
a_{s}(x)
is obtained in a good approximation for the slowly varying potential of background
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field, which describes the evolution of early universe and will be discussed further below. Similar inflationary scenarios with two or more general scalar fields based on the scaling gauge invariance have been studied extensively in refs.
TW1
;
TW2
;
TW3
, which demonstrated that such kind of inflationary scenarios can provide a consistent explanation for the observed data. We will examine below an explicit potential of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field to verify all required slow-roll conditions.

X.4
Evolution of early universe with inflationary expansion governed by the potential energy of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field

At the very beginning of inflationary expansion of universe, all fundamental fields except the hyper-gravigauge field appears as very heavy particles with masses characterized by the fundamental graviscaling mass scale
M_{S}
, such an early universe is governed by the slowly varying potential energy of background scaling field
\nu_{S}
. The kinetic terms of all heavy massive particles are expected to be negligible smaller in comparison with their mass-like terms, so that all such basic fields are thought to decouple from interactions at the beginning of early universe. The slow-roll conditions are generally characterized by the following slow-roll parameters:

\displaystyle\epsilon

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{M_{\kappa}^{2}}{g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{Q}^{2}M_{S}^{2}}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\nu_{S})}{{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})}\right)^{2}\ll 1,

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\kappa}^{2}}{g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{Q}^{2}M_{S}^{2}}\frac{{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}(\nu_{S})}{{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})}\ll 1.

(346)

When the background field
\nu_{S}
goes through down to the magnitude which makes
\epsilon\sim 1
and
|\eta|\sim 1
, the kinetic energy of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field becomes compatible with the potential energy, which invalidates the slow-roll conditions. In this situation, the inflationary expansion of early universe starts to break down and the potential of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field is quickly falling into the minimum point at
\nu_{S}=\nu_{0}
with
\nu_{0}\ll 1
. Meanwhile, the varying masses of hyperunified qubit-spinor field and hyperspin gauge field as well as
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field become negligible small as their masses are proportional to the small factor
\sinh\nu_{0}\simeq\nu_{0}\ll 1
, only the scaling gauge field obtains a finite mass characterized by the fundamental mass scale
M_{\kappa}
.

To see explicitly, let us rewrite the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field around the minimum point
\nu_{0}
as follows:

\displaystyle\chi_{s}(x)\equiv\nu_{0}+\frac{\Phi(x)}{M_{S}},

(347)

where
\Phi(x)
is regarded as quantized
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field. In this case, the action in Einstein-type basis is easily read from the action given in Eq.(
284
) to be as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\chi^{e}M_{\kappa}^{D_{h}-4}{\mathcal{L}}_{D_{h}}\equiv\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\chi^{e}M_{\kappa}^{D_{h}-4}\{\,\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\bar{\chi}^{e}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{N}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(348)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle m_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-2M_{S}\sinh(\frac{\Phi}{2M_{S}})\cosh(\nu_{0}+\frac{\Phi}{2M_{S}})\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4g_{H}^{2}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\frac{1}{2g_{H}^{2}}M_{\kappa}^{2}\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{R}^{e}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2g_{H}^{2}}\mu_{\mathbf{A}}^{2}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2g_{H}^{2}}\beta_{G}^{2}M_{S}^{2}[\sinh^{2}(\frac{\Phi}{M_{S}})+2\sinh(\nu_{0})\sinh(\frac{\Phi}{M_{S}})\cosh(\nu_{0}+\frac{\Phi}{M_{S}})\,]

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}})\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{N}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4g_{W}^{2}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\mathbf{N}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\mu_{\mathsf{W}}^{2}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{Q}^{2}M_{S}^{2}[\sinh^{2}(\frac{\Phi}{M_{S}})+2\sinh(\nu_{0})\sinh(\frac{\Phi}{M_{S}})\cosh(\nu_{0}+\frac{\Phi}{M_{S}})\,]\,\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{N}}\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}^{e\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}\lambda_{Q}^{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\Phi\partial_{\mathbf{N}}\Phi-\Lambda_{Q}^{4}-\frac{1}{2}\mu_{Q}^{2}\Phi^{2}

\displaystyle-
