\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{D}^{2}}{\beta_{Q}^{4}}M_{S}^{4}[\,{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{0}+\frac{\Phi}{M_{S}})-{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{0})-\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}(\nu_{0})\frac{\Phi^{2}}{M_{S}^{2}}\,]\},

with the following definitions:

\displaystyle m_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}=M_{S}\sinh(\nu_{0}),\quad\mu_{\mathbf{A}}=\beta_{G}M_{S}\sinh(\nu_{0}),

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathsf{W}}=\lambda_{Q}M_{S}(1+\sinh^{2}(\nu_{0}))^{1/2},\quad\Lambda_{Q}^{4}=\lambda_{D}^{2}M_{\kappa}^{4}{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{0}),

\displaystyle\mu_{Q}^{2}=\frac{\lambda_{D}^{2}}{\beta_{Q}^{4}}M_{S}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}(\nu_{0}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}(\nu_{0})\equiv\frac{\partial^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})}{\partial^{2}{\chi_{s}}}|_{\chi_{s}=\nu_{0}},

(349)

where
m_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
is regarded as the mass of hyperunified qubit-spinor field in Einstein-type basis.
\mu_{\mathbf{A}}
,
\mu_{\mathsf{W}}
and
\mu_{Q}
characterize the masses of hyperspin gauge field, scaling gauge field and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field in hyper-spacetime, respectively.
{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{0}+\frac{\Phi}{M_{S}})
is the potential of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field. The
\Lambda_{Q}^{4}
term which arises from the minimum potential reflects
cosmic energy density
in Einstein-type basis after inflation.

For a more concrete and realistic consideration, let us examine the following simple potential
{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})
of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\chi_{s}(x)
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})=\frac{\sinh^{4}(\chi_{s})+\lambda_{\kappa}^{2}}{(\lambda_{\chi}^{-2}+\sinh^{2}(\chi_{s}))^{2}},

(350)

with
\lambda_{\kappa}
and
\lambda_{\chi}
two constant parameters. The minimal condition of the potential is found to be as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\chi_{s}}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})=\frac{2\sinh(2\chi_{s})(\lambda_{\chi}^{-2}\sinh^{2}(\chi_{s})-\lambda_{\kappa}^{2})}{(\lambda_{\chi}^{-2}+\sinh^{2}(\chi_{s}))^{3}}=0,

(351)

which leads to the following solution on the minimum point
\chi_{s}\equiv\nu_{0}
:

\displaystyle\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}=\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2},\quad\chi_{s}\equiv\nu_{0}=\pm\sinh^{-1}(\lambda_{\chi}\lambda_{\kappa}).

(352)

For the background
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field
\langle\chi_{s}(x)\rangle=\nu_{S}(x)
, it can be checked that the above simple potential can satisfy all required properties shown in Eqs.(
297
) and (
X.3
) for inflationary expansion of early universe as long as the constant parameters
\lambda_{\kappa}
and
\lambda_{\chi}
meet to the following conditions:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\kappa}\ll 1,\quad\lambda_{\chi}>1,

(353)

which brings on the needed feature as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu_{S}}{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})\to 0,\;\;{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})\to 1,\quad\nu_{S}\gg 1,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{0})=\frac{\lambda_{\chi}^{4}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2}}{1+\lambda_{\chi}^{4}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2}}\ll 1.

(354)

In general, let us verify the slow-roll conditions by considering the so-called slow-roll parameters
\epsilon
and
\eta
(it should not be confused with the cosmic co-moving Lorentz time
\eta
defined in Eq.(
X.3
) for
\nu_{S}\gg 1
,

\displaystyle\epsilon

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{M_{\kappa}^{2}}{g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{Q}^{2}M_{S}^{2}}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\nu_{S})}{{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})}\right)^{2}=\frac{2}{g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}}\frac{\sinh^{2}(2\nu_{S})\left(\sinh^{2}\nu_{S}-\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\right)^{2}}{\left(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\sinh^{2}\nu_{S}\right)^{2}\left(\sinh^{4}(\nu_{S})+\lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\right)^{2}},

(355)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{8\cosh^{2}(\nu_{S})}{g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\left(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\sinh^{2}\nu_{S}\right)^{2}\sinh^{2}\nu_{S}}\simeq\frac{8\coth^{2}\nu_{S}}{g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\lambda_{\chi}^{4}(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\sinh^{2}\nu_{S})^{2}}\sim 128g_{H}^{-2}\lambda_{S}^{-2}\lambda_{\chi}^{-4}e^{-4\nu_{S}},

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\kappa}^{2}}{g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{Q}^{2}M_{S}^{2}}\frac{{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}(\nu_{S})}{{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})}

(356)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\sinh^{2}(\nu_{S}))\cosh(2\nu_{S})(\sinh^{2}(\nu_{S})-\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2})+2\sinh^{2}(2\nu_{S})(1-2\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\sinh^{2}(\nu_{S})+3\lambda_{\chi}^{4}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2})}{g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\sinh^{2}(\nu_{S}))^{2}(\sinh^{4}(\nu_{S})+\lambda_{\kappa}^{2})},

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{4\cosh(2\nu_{S})(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\sinh^{2}(\nu_{S}))+8\cosh^{2}(\nu_{S})(1-2\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\sinh^{2}(\nu_{S}))}{g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\sinh^{2}(\nu_{S}))^{2}\sinh^{2}(\nu_{S})}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{4\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\cosh(2\nu_{S})-16\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\cosh^{2}\nu_{S}}{g_{H}^{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\sinh^{2}\nu_{S})^{2}}\sim-32g_{H}^{-2}\lambda_{S}^{-2}\lambda_{\chi}^{-2}e^{-2\nu_{S}},

The slow-roll process holds for the case
\epsilon\ll 1
and
|\eta|\ll 1
, which corresponds to the following conditions:

\displaystyle e^{2\nu_{S}}\lambda_{\chi}^{2}g_{H}\lambda_{S}\gg 8\sqrt{2},\quad\epsilon\ll 1,

\displaystyle e^{\nu_{S}}\lambda_{\chi}g_{H}\lambda_{S}\gg 4\sqrt{2},\quad|\eta|\ll 1,

(357)

which show that when the slow-roll parameter
\eta
satisfies the slow-roll condition
|\eta|\ll 1
, the slow-roll parameter
\epsilon
automatically fits to the condition
\epsilon\ll 1
as
e^{\nu_{S}}\lambda_{\chi}>1
.

When the slow-roll parameters increase to close to the order of one, i.e.,
\epsilon\sim 1
and
|\eta|\sim 1
, the slow-roll conditions become invalid and the inflationary expansion of early universe starts to break down. As a consequence, the potential of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field quickly runs into the minimum value around the minimum point
\nu_{S}=\nu_{0}\ll 1
. In this case, the relevant cosmic energy density
\Lambda_{Q}^{4}
after inflation is given by,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{Q}^{4}\equiv\lambda_{D}^{2}M_{\kappa}^{4}{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{0})=\frac{\lambda_{\chi}^{4}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2}}{1+\lambda_{\chi}^{4}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2}}\lambda_{D}^{2}M_{\kappa}^{4}\ll M_{\kappa}^{4},\quad\mbox{for}\quad\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\lambda_{\kappa}\ll 1.

(358)

The relevant masses of hyperunified qubit-spinor field and hyperspin gauge field as well as
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field and scaling gauge field in hyper-spacetime are characterized as follows:

\displaystyle m_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}=\lambda_{\chi}\lambda_{\kappa}M_{S}\ll M_{S},

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathbf{A}}=\lambda_{\chi}\lambda_{\kappa}\beta_{G}M_{S}\ll M_{S},

\displaystyle\mu_{Q}=2\sqrt{2}\frac{\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\lambda_{\kappa}(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2})^{1/2}}{(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{4}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2})}\frac{\lambda_{D}}{\beta_{Q}^{2}}M_{S}\simeq 2\sqrt{2}\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\lambda_{\kappa}\frac{\lambda_{D}}{\beta_{Q}^{2}}M_{S}\ll M_{S},

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathsf{W}}=\lambda_{Q}M_{S}(1+(\lambda_{\chi}\lambda_{\kappa})^{2})^{1/2}\simeq\lambda_{Q}M_{S}\sim M_{S},

(359)

where we have used the following relation in obtaining the relevant mass of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar boson:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}(\nu_{0})\equiv\frac{\partial^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{S})}{\partial\nu_{S}}|_{\nu_{S}=\nu_{0}}=\frac{8\lambda_{\chi}^{4}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2}(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2})}{(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{4}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2})^{2}}.

(360)

It is seen that the hyperunified qubit-spinor field and hyperspin gauge field as well as
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field get relevant masses much smaller than the fundamental graviscaling mass scale
M_{S}
, while the scaling gauge field can receive a very heavy mass characterized by the mass scale
M_{S}
.

Therefore, when the inflation ends up and the potential falls into the minimum, the initial high potential energy characterized by the fundamental mass scale
M_{\kappa}
is transmuted into kinetic energies of fundamental fields which have much smaller masses in comparison with the fundamental mass scale
M_{\kappa}
. So that the gauge interactions of inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge field and hyperunified qubit-spinor field together with the interactions of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field become active as fundamental interactions of nature, which are thought to play a significant role in radiative and matter dominated evolutions of the universe. In general, the scaling gauge field may still be out of the thermal equilibrium due to its very heavy mass.

X.5
Evolving universe with scaling gauge field as dark matter candidate and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field as quantum cosmic matter and dark energy candidate

Let us suppose that all fundamental fields as functionals of coordinates in hyper-spacetime have
localizing distribution amplitudes
in extra spatial dimensions beyond four-dimensional spacetime. In general, the fundamental fields as functionals of coordinates are distributed with different
localizing length scales

l_{A}
(
A=5,\cdots,D_{h}
) in different extra spatial dimensions of hyper-spacetime. These localizing length scales in extra spatial dimensions are thought to form a manifold with a finite integrable volume
{\mathcal{V}}_{D_{h}-4}\propto l_{1}\times l_{2}\times\cdots\times l_{D_{h}-4}
. As the present consideration is focusing on the evolution of universe, we shall not investigate extensively on the localizing distribution amplitudes of fundamental fields.

To simplify the discussion, let us consider the case that all fundamental fields will run from the high energy scale characterized by the fundamental mass scale
M_{\kappa}
down to the mass scale
\mu_{M}
determined by the
maximal localizing length scale

\l_{M}=\mbox{Max}.\{\l_{A}\}=1/\mu_{M}
. When the running energy scale undergoes to be below the
minimal localizing energy scale

\mu_{M}
, i.e.,
\mu<\mu_{M}
, all fundamental fields as effective fields appear to have motions and interactions in four-dimensional spacetime, which leads in principle the hyperunified field theory in hyper-spacetime to be effective hyperunified field theory at low energies in four-dimensional spacetime. In formal, we may display such an effective hyperunified field theory in Einstein-type basis as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{D_{h}}x]\,\chi^{e}(x)M_{\kappa}^{D_{h}-4}{\mathcal{L}}_{D_{h}}=\int[d^{4}x]\,\chi^{e}(x){\mathcal{V}}_{D_{h}-4}M_{\kappa}^{D_{h}-4}{\mathcal{L}}_{4D}

(361)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\,\chi^{e}(x){\mathcal{L}}_{4D}^{eff},

where
{\mathcal{L}}_{4D}^{eff}
represents the effective Lagrangian at low energies in four-dimensional spacetime.

It is useful to introduce a
characteristic localizing length scale

l_{\alpha}
or
characteristic localizing mass scale

M_{\alpha}=1/l_{\alpha}
to characterize the whole volume
{\mathcal{V}}_{D_{h}-4}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{D_{h}-4}\equiv l_{\alpha}^{D_{h}-4}=\frac{1}{M_{\alpha}^{D_{h}-4}}\propto l_{1}\times l_{2}\times\cdots\times l_{D_{h}-4},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{D_{h}-4}M_{\kappa}^{D_{h}-4}\equiv\left(\frac{M_{\kappa}}{M_{\alpha}}\right)^{D_{h}-4}\equiv\xi_{\alpha}^{-(D_{h}-4)},\quad\xi_{\alpha}\equiv\frac{M_{\alpha}}{M_{\kappa}},

(362)

where
\xi_{\alpha}
is regarded as
dimension-reduction scaling factor
, which enables us to define, from the basic coupling constants
g_{H}
,
g_{W}
,
\lambda_{S}
and
\lambda_{Q}
of hyperunified field theory in hyper-spacetime, the effective coupling constants
g_{h}
,
g_{w}
,
\lambda_{s}
and
\lambda_{q}
in four-dimensional spacetime as follows:

\displaystyle g_{h}^{2}\equiv g_{H}^{2}\left(\frac{M_{\alpha}}{M_{\kappa}}\right)^{D_{h}-4}\equiv g_{H}^{2}\xi_{\alpha}^{D_{h}-4},

\displaystyle g_{w}^{2}\equiv g_{W}^{2}\left(\frac{M_{\alpha}}{M_{\kappa}}\right)^{D_{h}-4}\equiv g_{W}^{2}\xi_{\alpha}^{D_{h}-4},

\displaystyle\lambda_{s}^{2}\equiv\lambda_{S}^{2}\left(\frac{M_{\kappa}}{M_{\alpha}}\right)^{D_{h}-4}\equiv\lambda_{S}^{2}\xi_{\alpha}^{-(D_{h}-4)},

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}^{2}\equiv\lambda_{Q}^{2}\left(\frac{M_{\kappa}}{M_{\alpha}}\right)^{D_{h}-4}\equiv\lambda_{S}^{2}\xi_{\alpha}^{-(D_{h}-4)},

\displaystyle\lambda_{d}^{2}\equiv\lambda_{D}^{2}\left(\frac{M_{\kappa}}{M_{\alpha}}\right)^{D_{h}-4}\equiv\lambda_{D}^{2}\xi_{\alpha}^{-(D_{h}-4)}.

(363)

It is manifest that the following combinations of coupling constants are independent of the dimension-reduction scaling factor:

\displaystyle g_{H}\lambda_{S}=g_{h}\lambda_{s},\quad g_{H}\lambda_{Q}=g_{h}\lambda_{q},\quad g_{H}\lambda_{D}=g_{h}\lambda_{d},

\displaystyle g_{W}\lambda_{S}=g_{w}\lambda_{s},\quad g_{W}\lambda_{Q}=g_{w}\lambda_{q},

\displaystyle\beta_{Q}\equiv\frac{\lambda_{S}}{\lambda_{Q}}=\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda_{q}},\quad\frac{\lambda_{D}}{\lambda_{Q}}=\frac{\lambda_{d}}{\lambda_{q}},

(364)

which are referred to as
combined dimension-independent coupling constant
.

It is noticed that the slow-roll conditions presented in Eqs.(
355
)-(
X.4
) are spacetime dimension independent as they are given by the combined dimension-independent coupling constant
g_{H}\lambda_{S}=g_{h}\lambda_{s}
.

To formulate the effective Lagrangian
{\mathcal{L}}_{4D}^{eff}
in four-dimensional spacetime, the kinetic terms of hyperspin gauge field and scaling gauge field as well as
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field are normalized via the following replacements:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(x)\to g_{h}{\mathcal{A}}(x),\quad\mathsf{W}(x)\to g_{w}\mathsf{W}(x),

\displaystyle\chi_{s}(x)\to\lambda_{s}^{-1}\chi_{s}(x)\quad\text{or}\;\;\Phi(x)\to\lambda_{q}^{-1}\Phi(x),

(365)

and the gravitational coupling
G_{N}
in four-dimensional spacetime is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}}\equiv\frac{M_{\kappa}^{2}}{g_{h}^{2}}=\frac{M_{\kappa}^{2}}{g_{H}^{2}}\xi_{\alpha}^{4-D_{h}}\equiv\frac{M_{P}^{2}}{16\pi}=\frac{1}{2}M_{p}^{2},

(366)

which indicates that the gravitational coupling
G_{N}
or Planck mass
M_{P}
( reduced Planck mass
M_{p}
) presented in four-dimensional spacetime depends in general on the dimension-reduction scaling factor
\xi_{\alpha}
and hyper-spacetime dimension
D_{h}
when relating Planck mass
M_{P}
to the fundamental mass scale
M_{\kappa}
and basic coupling constant
g_{H}
in the hyperunified field theory in hyper-spacetime.

With the above considerations and analyses, we now turn to study the scaling gauge field and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field as basic constituents of universe and discuss their roles for the evolution of universe in an effective hyperunified field theory at low energies in four-dimensional spacetime.

Let us discuss firstly the property of scaling gauge field
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}
. It is noticed that the scaling gauge field has no direct interactions with hyperunified qubit-spinor field and hyperspin gauge field. In the hidden scaling gauge formalism of hyperunified field theory as shown in Eq.(
90
) with the redefinition
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\to\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mathbf{M}}\ln(\lambda_{S}^{2}\phi^{2}+\lambda_{Q}^{2}\phi_{1}^{2})
, the scaling gauge field interacts only with the scaling field
\chi_{s}(x)
and hyper-gravigauge field. As the action of Eq.(
90
) or Eq.(
348
) possesses a discrete symmetry under
Z_{2}
transformation, i.e.,
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}\to-\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}
, so that
\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{M}}
becomes stable and survives to present once it is produced in the early universe. Therefore, the scaling gauge field is expected to be as
dark matter candidate
in the hyperunified field theory.

It can be checked that the primordial Hubble-type mass scale
H_{\kappa}
and scaling gauge boson mass
M_{W}
are given by,

\displaystyle H_{\kappa}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\kappa}}{\alpha_{\kappa}}\simeq(g_{H}\lambda_{D})\left((D_{h}-1)(D_{h}-2)\right)^{-1/2}M_{\kappa}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(g_{h}\lambda_{d})\left((D_{h}-1)(D_{h}-2)\right)^{-1/2}M_{\kappa},

\displaystyle M_{W}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(g_{w}\lambda_{q})(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2})^{1/2}M_{S}\simeq(g_{w}\lambda_{q})M_{S}

(367)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(g_{W}\lambda_{Q})M_{S}=(g_{W}\lambda_{S})M_{\kappa}=(g_{w}\lambda_{s})M_{\kappa},

which indicates that both
H_{\kappa}
and
M_{W}
are independent of the dimension-reduction scaling factor
\xi_{\alpha}
as they are given by the combined dimension-independent coupling constants
g_{h}\lambda_{q}=g_{H}\lambda_{Q}
and
g_{w}\lambda_{q}=g_{W}\lambda_{Q}
or
g_{w}\lambda_{s}=g_{W}\lambda_{S}
, respectively. It is clear that the magnitudes for both
H_{\kappa}
and
M_{W}
are characterized by the fundamental mass scale
M_{\kappa}
.

Let us examine a possible mechanism to produce the heavy scaling gauge boson
\mathsf{W}(x)
via the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar annihilation, i.e.,
\Phi+\Phi\rightarrow\mathsf{W}+\mathsf{W}
, after inflation in the early universe. As analyzed in ref.
TW3
, the cross section of the annihilation process dominated by the longitudinal mode of scaling gauge boson is given by,

\displaystyle\sigma\sim\frac{g_{w}^{4}}{E^{2}}\frac{E^{4}}{M_{W}^{4}}=\frac{E^{2}}{M_{\kappa}^{4}\lambda_{s}^{4}},

with
E
as the typical energy of final scaling gauge boson
\mathsf{W}(x)
.

To obtain the correct relic density, a high reheating temperature
T_{R}
is expected to be reached when the potential energy is transmuted into the kinetic energies of fundamental fields which get very small masses after inflation. It was demonstrated in ref.
TW3
that in the instantaneous reheating limit, the reheating temperature
T_{R}
is related to the primordial Hubble-type mass scale
H_{\kappa}
through the relation,
T_{R}\sim\sqrt{H_{\kappa}M_{p}}
. The reheating temperature
T_{R}
can maximally reach to be
T_{R}\sim 4\times 10^{15}\;\text{GeV}
, which can produce abundantly the heavy scaling gauge boson
\mathsf{W}(x)
with the mass up to
M_{W}\sim 5\times 10^{16}\;\text{GeV}
.

It is attributed to the discrete symmetry
Z_{2}
with
\mathsf{W}(x)\to-\mathsf{W}(x)
that the scaling gauge boson
\mathsf{W}(x)
becomes stable, which brings naturally such a heavy scaling gauge boson to be as
dark matter candidate
in the hyperunified field theory.

We now come to investigate basic properties arising from
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field. Let us consider a simple case that the smallness of constant
\lambda_{\kappa}
is characterized just by two fundamental mass scales
M_{S}
and
m_{\kappa}
in the present theory with the following relation:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\kappa}=\frac{m_{\kappa}}{M_{S}}=\frac{\lambda_{Q}m_{\kappa}}{\lambda_{S}M_{\kappa}}=\frac{\lambda_{q}m_{\kappa}}{\lambda_{s}M_{\kappa}},

(368)

where the fundamental graviscaling mass scale
M_{S}
reflects
ultraviolet energy scale
in early universe and the fundamental cosmic mass scale
m_{\kappa}
describes
infrared energy scale
of present universe. Such a constant
\lambda_{\kappa}
is regarded as
basic cosmic constant
.

The cosmic energy density
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{4}
in four-dimensional spacetime is given as follows:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{4}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\xi_{\alpha}^{D_{h}-4}\Lambda_{Q}^{4}=\lambda_{d}^{2}M_{\kappa}^{4}{\mathcal{F}}(\nu_{0})=\frac{\lambda_{d}^{2}M_{\kappa}^{4}\lambda_{\chi}^{4}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2}}{1+\lambda_{\chi}^{4}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2}}

(369)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{q}^{2}}{\lambda_{s}^{2}}\lambda_{d}^{2}\lambda_{\chi}^{4}M_{\kappa}^{2}m_{\kappa}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\lambda_{q}^{2}}{\lambda_{s}^{2}}(\lambda_{d}g_{h})^{2}\lambda_{\chi}^{4}M_{p}^{2}m_{\kappa}^{2}\equiv\frac{\lambda_{q}^{2}}{\lambda_{s}^{2}}\lambda_{d}^{2}\lambda_{\chi}^{4}M_{\kappa}^{2}L_{\kappa}^{-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\lambda_{q}^{2}}{\lambda_{s}^{2}}(\lambda_{d}g_{h})^{2}\lambda_{\chi}^{4}M_{p}^{2}L_{\kappa}^{-2}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\lambda_{S}^{2}}(\lambda_{D}g_{H})^{2}\lambda_{\chi}^{4}M_{p}^{2}L_{\kappa}^{-2}\equiv 3d_{\lambda}^{2}M_{p}^{2}L_{\kappa}^{-2},

which indicates that the cosmic energy density
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{4}
in four dimensional spacetime depends on the dimension-reduction scaling factor
\xi_{\alpha}
. Such a dependence is the same as that of Planck mass square in four-dimensional spacetime. Note that
\lambda_{d}g_{h}=\lambda_{D}g_{H}
and
\lambda_{q}/\lambda_{s}=\lambda_{Q}/\lambda_{S}
are the combined dimension-independent coupling constants.

As the fundamental cosmic mass scale
m_{\kappa}
reflects the co-moving cosmic horizon of the universe, i.e.,
m_{\kappa}=\frac{1}{L_{\kappa}}=\frac{1}{c_{\kappa}\eta_{\kappa}}
, it is natural to expect that the cosmic energy density
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{4}
brings on the basic constituent of universe as
dark energy candidate
, which can be achieved when the cosmic energy scale
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
fits to the current experimental observation with the magnitude
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}\sim 10^{-2.5}
eV.

In fact, it was shown extensively in ref.
eHDE
that the dark energy characterized by the total co-moving cosmic horizon is viewed as
holographic dark energy
, which can provide a consistent explanation on the accelerated expansion of present universe and meanwhile overcome the so-called fine-tuning problem and coincidence problem
SW
. When applying a similar analysis to the present consideration, the cosmic energy density
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{4}
can present a consistent dark energy candidate as long as the combination of coupling constants satisfies the following condition:

\displaystyle d_{\lambda}\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\frac{\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{s}}(\lambda_{d}g_{h})\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\gtrsim 10^{1}\sim 10^{2},

(370)

which may be referred to as
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin cosmic dark energy
for a short of mention.

As the scaling gauge field can serve as dark matter candidate in the hyperunified field theory, so that
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin cosmic dark energy not only becomes compatible with the so-called
\Lambda
CDM model but also enables us to understand both the fine-tuning problem and coincidence problem directly in connection with the inflationary expansion of early universe.

When the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin cosmic dark energy
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{4}
becomes dominant as it is just the case in the present universe, the cosmic energy scale
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
can be regarded as an ultraviolet (UV) cutoff of effective hyperunified field theory at low energies of present universe, which is proposed to meet a relationship between UV cutoff and infrared (IR) cutoff (
1/L
) with
L
the size of volume
L^{3}
such that an effective field theory should be a good description of Nature
CKN
. Such a relationship indicates that all physical states except those that have already collapsed to a black hole should be described by conventional quantum field theory in four-dimensional spacetime. From such a relationship, we arrive at the following condition in our present considerations:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{4}=3d_{\lambda}^{2}M_{p}^{2}L_{\kappa}^{-2}\equiv M_{p}^{2}L_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{-2}<M_{p}^{2}L^{-2},

(371)

which implies that the IR cutoff
1/L
is constrained to be,

\displaystyle L<L_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}\equiv\frac{M_{p}}{\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{2}}\sim 10^{33}\,\mbox{eV}^{-1},

\displaystyle L_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}\equiv\frac{L_{\kappa}}{\sqrt{3}d_{\lambda}}\lesssim(10^{-1}-10^{-2})L_{\kappa},

(372)

where
L_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
is regarded as the size of visible universe within the gravitational quantum field theory, which is shown to be less than the co-moving cosmic horizon
L_{\kappa}
by one to two orders of magnitude. In general,
L_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
may be referred to as
visible co-moving cosmic horizon
of present universe. It is clear that the effective hyperunified field theory as effective local gravitational quantum field theory can provide a good approximate description of present universe within the visible co-moving cosmic horizon
L_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}
. Meanwhile, such a cosmic energy density
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{4}
as
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin cosmic dark energy matches to the spirit of holographic principle
HP1
;
HP2
in four-dimensional spacetime.

As the cosmic energy scale
\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}\sim 10^{-2.5}
eV presents an UV cutoff of present universe, we are able to determine the fundamental cosmic mass scale
m_{\kappa}
characterized by the co-moving cosmic horizon
L_{\kappa}^{-1}=(c_{\kappa}\eta_{\kappa})^{-1}=m_{\kappa}
from both
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin cosmic dark energy and coupling constant parameter presented in Eqs.(
369
) and (
370
). The magnitude value of
m_{\kappa}
is given as follows:

\displaystyle m_{\kappa}=\frac{1}{L_{\kappa}}=\frac{1}{c\eta_{\kappa}}=\frac{\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{2}}{\sqrt{3}d_{\lambda}M_{p}}\lesssim(10^{-34}-10^{-35})\mbox{eV}.

(373)

The mass of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar boson
\Phi(x)
is found to be,

\displaystyle m_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}\frac{\lambda_{d}}{\lambda_{s}}M_{\kappa}^{2}\sqrt{{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}(\nu_{0})}=2\sqrt{2}\frac{\lambda_{d}}{\lambda_{s}}\frac{\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\lambda_{\kappa}(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2})^{1/2}}{(1+\lambda_{\chi}^{4}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2})}M_{\kappa},

(374)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}\frac{\lambda_{d}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{s}^{2}}\lambda_{\chi}^{2}m_{\kappa}=2\sqrt{2}\frac{\lambda_{D}\lambda_{Q}}{\lambda_{S}^{2}}\lambda_{\chi}^{2}m_{\kappa},

which is independent of the dimension-reduction scaling factor
\xi_{\alpha}
as it is given by the combined dimension-independent coupling constants
\lambda_{d}/\lambda_{s}=\lambda_{D}/\lambda_{S}
and
\lambda_{q}/\lambda_{s}=\lambda_{Q}/\lambda_{S}
. The magnitude of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar boson mass is determined by the fundamental cosmic mass scale
m_{\kappa}
and combined dimension-independent coupling constants as follows:

\displaystyle m_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}\simeq 2\sqrt{2}\frac{\lambda_{d}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{s}^{2}}\lambda_{\chi}^{2}m_{\kappa}=\frac{4\Lambda_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}}^{2}}{\lambda_{s}g_{h}M_{p}}\sim\frac{4}{\lambda_{s}g_{h}}\times 10^{-33}\mbox{eV}.

(375)

which indicates that the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar boson
\Phi(x)
receives a tiny
cosmic mass
.

We may refer to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar boson
\Phi(x)
as
quantum cosmic matter
, which is expected to play an essential role on the evolution of universe. This is because the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar boson
\Phi(x)
as quantum cosmic matter not only has universal gravitational interaction with the hyper-gravigauge field but also interacts with the scaling gauge boson as dark matter candidate, and meanwhile it couples directly to the hyperspin gauge field and hyperunified qubit-spinor field.

It is interesting to notice that the hyperunified qubit-spinor field and hyperspin gauge field also receive tiny masses:

\displaystyle m_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}=M_{S}\sinh\nu_{0}=M_{S}\lambda_{\chi}\lambda_{\kappa}=\lambda_{\chi}m_{\kappa}\leq\lambda_{\chi}(10^{-34}-10^{-35})\mbox{eV},

\displaystyle m_{\mathbf{A}}=\beta_{G}M_{S}\sinh\nu_{0}=\beta_{G}M_{S}\lambda_{\chi}\lambda_{\kappa}=\beta_{G}\lambda_{\chi}m_{\kappa}\leq\beta_{G}\lambda_{\chi}(10^{-34}-10^{-35})\mbox{eV},

(376)

which are independent of dimension-reduction scaling factor.

We would like to address that all vector-like leptons and quarks contained in the hyperunified qubit-spinor field obtain a tiny cosmic mass
m_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}
determined by the fundamental cosmic mass scale
m_{\kappa}
and coupling constant
\lambda_{\chi}
. Similarly, the hyperspin gauge field also acquires a tiny cosmic mass
m_{\mathbf{A}}
governed by the fundamental cosmic mass scale
m_{\kappa}
and combined coupling constant
\beta_{G}\lambda_{\chi}
, which means that all gauge fields including electromagnetic field and strong gauge fields receive tiny cosmic masses.

It is intriguing that the gluons and photon in such a hyperunified field theory become massive gauge bosons although their tiny cosmic masses appear unobservable small. The current experiment only provides the upper limits on the mass of photon to be around
PDG
:

\displaystyle m_{\gamma}<10^{-18}\mbox{eV}\sim 10^{-26}\mbox{eV}.

(377)

Therefore, the evolving
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field in the hyperunified field theory plays a significant role for the evolution of universe. The initial high energy potential of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field is characterized by the fundamental mass scale
M_{\kappa}
and brings on an inflationary expansion of early universe when the background
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field undertakes slowly varying extra large values. Meanwhile, after the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field falls into the minimum point of the potential, the resulting cosmic energy density as
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin cosmic dark energy leads to an accelerated expansion of present universe. In addition, the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar boson
\Phi(x)
as quantum cosmic matter with tiny cosmic mass is expected to be still essential for the future evolution of universe. Furthermore, the heavy scaling gauge field produced by the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar boson
\Phi(x)
is regarded as dark matter candidate. In particular, all vector-like leptons and quarks contained in the hyperunified qubit-spinor field and all gauge fields including strong gauge field (gluons) and electromagnetic field (photon) all acquire tiny cosmic masses and interact with the
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar boson
\Phi(x)
.

XI
Symmetry structure and symmetry breaking mechanism in hyperunified field theory and comprehension on the presence of Higgs boson and three families of leptons and quarks

To realize the SM in four dimensional spacetime from the hyperunified field theory in hyper-spacetime, it is inevitable to understand the presence of Higgs boson and three families of leptons and quarks. Following along the gauge invariance principle and scaling invariance hypothesis, we have demonstrated in previous sections how the inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry and scaling gauge symmetry govern the fundamental interactions of nature. We have also verified that when making gauge fixing conditions to remove all unphysical degrees of freedom for gauge fields, the fundamental symmetry of hyperunified field theory in 19-dimensional hyper-spacetime keeps only global inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,18)=SP(1,18)
\rtimes
W
1,18
in association with inhomogeneous Lorentz-type/Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,18)=P
{}^{1,18}\ltimes
SO(1,18) together with global scaling symmetry SC(1). As such a hyperunified qubit-spinor field contains four families of vector-like lepton-quark states with both chiral type lepton-quark states and corresponding mirror lepton-quark states, the subgroup symmetry SP(5)
\cong
SO(5) of WS(1,18) reflects the maximal family-spin symmetry of four families. Nevertheless, the current experiments only observed three families of chiral type leptons and quarks with a single light Higgs boson in SM and provided the lower mass limits up to 1370 GeV for the vector-like fourth family quarks
PDG
, which motivates us to examine the hyperunified symmetry structure with the presence of Higgs-like scalar fields and illustrate the appearance of three families of leptons and quarks in the chiral spinor representation with negative U-parity and the existence of the fourth family of vector-like lepton-quark state.

XI.1
Hyperunified field theory with inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,18) as hyperunified symmetry in gravigauge hyper-spacetime

To demonstrate the hyperunified symmetry structure with Higgs-like fields and possible symmetry breaking mechanisms for realizing three families of leptons and quarks in light of the qubit-spinor structure of hyperunified qubit-spinor field
\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}(x)
presented in Eqs.(
66
)-(
IV.1
), let us express the action in gravigauge hyper-spacetime as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[\delta^{D_{h}}\varkappa]\{\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(378)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}({\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}[\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}+{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]})-g_{H}^{-2}\eta^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,({\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]})({\mathcal{A}}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,({\mathcal{A}}_{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-\mathbf{\Omega}_{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]})({\mathcal{A}}^{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}-\mathbf{\Omega}^{(\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}\mathsf{F}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\mathsf{F}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\,\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\},

with
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{M}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,19
. Where the definitions for gauge fields and field strengths in gravigauge hyper-spacetime are presented in Eqs.(

)-(
VIII.1
) and Eqs.(
VIII.4
)-(
VIII.4

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}\equiv i\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\hat{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}(i\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{M}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}}),\quad{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\hat{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}},

(379)

which implies that the hyperspin gauge interaction with hyperunified qubit-spinor field only concerns the totally antisymmetric hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}
in gravigauge hyper-spacetime.

It can be verified from Eq.(

) that all
\varGamma
-matrices

with
\mathbf{A}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,20
are anticommuting, which can form in general a symmetry group SP(1,19)
\cong

\varGamma

\displaystyle\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}=\frac{i}{4}[\varGamma^{\mathbf{A}},\varGamma^{\mathbf{B}}],\quad\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,20.

(380)

which can be decomposed into the following subgroup symmetries:

\displaystyle SP(1,19)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle SP(1,3)\times SP(16)\supset SP(1,3)\times SP(10)\times SP(6)

(381)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle SO(1,3)\times SO(16)\supset SO(1,3)\times SO(10)\times SO(6)

\displaystyle\supset

with subgroup algebras corresponding to the following group generators:

\displaystyle\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}=0,1,2,3)\in sp(1,3)\cong so(1,3)

\displaystyle\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}=5,\cdots,20)\in sp(16)\cong so(16),

\displaystyle\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}=5,\cdots,14)\in sp(10)\cong so(10),

\displaystyle\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}=15,\cdots,20)\in sp(6)\cong so(6)\cong su_{F}(4),

\displaystyle\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}=5,\cdots,10)\in sp(6)\cong so(6)\cong su_{C}(4),

\displaystyle\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}=11,\cdots 14)\in sp(4)\cong so(4)\cong su_{L}(2)\times su_{R}(2).

Where the subgroup symmetry SU

\cong

F

still possesses the family-spin symmetry SO
F
(5) which contains the maximal subgroup family-spin symmetry SO

(4),

\displaystyle SO_{F}(5)\supset SO_{F}(4)\cong SU_{F}(2)\times SU_{\hat{F}}(2).

(383)

In the above qubit-spinor structure, SU

{}_{\hat{F}}
(2) reflects the family-spin symmetry between the third and fourth families.

In general, the action presented in Eq.(
378
) with gauge fixing conditions possesses the global inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,18) in association with inhomogeneous Lorentz-type/Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,18) in hidden coordinate Minkowski hyper-spacetime,

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle PO(1,18)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,WS(1,18)

(384)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{1,18}\ltimes SO(1,18)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,SP(1,18)\rtimes W^{1,18},

\,\widetilde{\Join}\,
”. Note that WS(1,18)=SP(1,18)
\rtimes
W
1,18
and PO(1,18) = P
{}^{1,18}\ltimes

1,18
is translation-like
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin Abelian group symmetry in Hilbert space and P
1,18
is ordinary translational Abelian group symmetry in Minkowski hyper-spacetime. The group generators of inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry WS(1,18) are directly given by the commutators of
\varGamma
-matrices,

\displaystyle\varSigma^{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}=\frac{i}{4}[\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}},\varGamma^{\mathsf{B}}]\in sp(1,18),

\displaystyle\varSigma_{-}^{\mathsf{A}}=\frac{1}{2}\varGamma^{\mathsf{A}}\varGamma_{-}\in w^{1,18},

(385)

with
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,19
.

The hyperspin symmetry SP(1,18) of the action in Eq.(
378
) as the subgroup symmetry of SP(1,19) has a natural decomposition into the following subgroup symmetry:

\displaystyle SP(1,18)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle SP(1,3)\times SP(15)\supset SP(1,3)\times SP(10)\times SP(5)

(386)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle SO(1,3)\times SO(15)\supset SO(1,3)\times SO(10)\times SO_{F}(5)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle SO(1,3)\times SO(6)\times SO(4)\times SO_{F}(4)

\displaystyle SO(1,3)\times SU_{C}(4)\times SU_{L}(2)\times SU_{R}(2)\times SU_{F}(2)\times SU_{\hat{F}}(2)

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle SO(1,3)\times SU_{C}(3)\times SU_{L}(1)\times U_{Y}(1),

where the four color-spin symmetry SU
C
(4), left-right isospin symmetry SU
L
(2)
\times
SU

(2) and heavy-light family-spin symmetry SU

(2)
\times

XI.2
Hyperspin gauge fields beyond four-dimensional gravigauge spacetime as the Higgs-like scalar fields and presence of Higgs-like bosons in hyperunified field theory

It can be seen from the action shown in Eq.(
378
) and covariant derivative given in Eq.(
XI.1
) that the hyperspin gauge field defined in gravigauge hyper-spacetime couples to the self-conjugated hyperunified qubit-spinor field only via its totally antisymmetric tensor form
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}
. When taking scaling gauge fixing condition to be in Einstein-type basis, we can express such a totally antisymmetric hyperspin gauge field as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}})

(387)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}(\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{C}}^{e\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{e\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}}+\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{B}}^{e\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}}),

where
\hat{\chi}_{\mathbf{A}}^{e\mathbf{M}}
is hyper-garvigauge field in Einstein-type basis and also regarded as Goldstone-like bicovariant vector field, and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
is hyperspin gauge field in biframe hyper-spacetime.

It can be verified that the action presented in Eq.(
378
) with scaling gauge prescription in Einstein-type basis possesses inhomogeneous hyperspin gauge symmetry WS(1,18) in Hilbert space jointly together with inhomogeneous Lorentz-type/Poincaré-type group symmetry PO(1,18) in hidden coordinate Minkowski hyper-spacetime,

\displaystyle G_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle PO(1,18)\Join WS(1,18)=P^{1,18}\ltimes SO(1,18)\Join SP(1,18)\rtimes W^{1,18},

(388)

where WS(1,18)=SP(1,18)
\rtimes
W
1,18
and PO(1,18) = P
{}^{1,18}\ltimes
SO(1,18) are semidirect product group symmetries with W
1,18
the translation-like
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin Abelian group symmetry in Hilbert space and P
1,18
the ordinary translational Abelian group symmetry in Minkowski hyper-spacetime.

The hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,18) contains the following maximal subgroup symmetry:

\displaystyle SP(1,18)\supset SP(1,3)\times SP(10)\times SP(5)\cong SO(1,3)\times SO(10)\times SO(5),

(389)

where SP(1,3) is relevant to the usual spin and boost-spin symmetry in four-dimensional gravigauge spacetime, while SP(10)
\times
SP(5) are regarded as internal symmetries besides four-dimensional gravigauge spacetime. In light of the above subgroup symmetry, it is useful to decompose the totally antisymmetric hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}
into the following forms:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\mathcal{A}}_{[cab]},{\mathcal{A}}_{[c\bar{A}\bar{B}]},{\mathcal{A}}_{[c\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]},{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}ab]},{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}\bar{A}\bar{B}]},

(390)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]},{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{C}ab]},{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{C}\bar{A}\bar{B}]},{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{C}\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]},{\mathcal{A}}_{[c\bar{A}\tilde{A}]}),

with the notations:

\displaystyle\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B},\mathbf{C}\equiv(a,\bar{A},\tilde{A}),(b,\bar{B},\tilde{B}),(c,\bar{C},\tilde{C}),

\displaystyle a,b,c=0,1,2,3,\quad\bar{A},\bar{B},\bar{C}=5,6,\cdots,14,

\displaystyle\tilde{A},\tilde{B},\tilde{C}=15,\cdots,19.

(391)

It is noticed that as the decomposed hyperspin gauge fields
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}\bar{A}\bar{B}]}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{C}\bar{A}\bar{B}]}
and

concern only the internal hyperspin gauge symmetries SP(10)
\times

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[c\bar{A}\bar{B}]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[c\;I+4\;J+4]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{cIJ},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[c\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[c\;\tilde{a}+14\;\tilde{b}+14]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{c\tilde{a}\tilde{b}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[c\bar{A}\tilde{A}]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[c\;I+4\;\tilde{a}+14]}\equiv{\mathcal{V}}_{cI\tilde{a}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}ab]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[K+4\;ab]}\equiv\mathcal{T}_{Kab},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{C}ab]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{c}+14\;ab]}\equiv\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{c}\,ab},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}\bar{A}\bar{B}]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[K+4\;I+4\;J+4]}\equiv\mathcal{H}_{KIJ},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[K+4\;\tilde{a}+14\;\tilde{b}+14]}\equiv\mathcal{H}_{K\tilde{a}\tilde{b}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{C}\bar{A}\bar{B}]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{c}+14\;I+4\;J+4]}\equiv\varPhi_{\tilde{c}IJ},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{C}\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{c}+14\;\tilde{a}+14\;\tilde{b}+14]}\equiv\varPhi_{\tilde{c}\tilde{a}\tilde{b}},

(392)

with the definitions:

\displaystyle\bar{A},\bar{B},\bar{C}\equiv I+4,\,J+4,\,K+4,\quad I,J,K=1,2,\cdots,10,

\displaystyle\tilde{A},\tilde{B},\tilde{C}\equiv\tilde{a}+14,\,\tilde{b}+14,\,\tilde{c}+14,\quad\tilde{a},\tilde{b},\tilde{c}=1,2,\cdots,5.

(393)

In view of four-dimensional gravigauge spacetime,
{\mathcal{A}}_{cIJ}
,

,

,

and

are considered as Higgs-like scalar fields. The representation dimensions of all totally antisymmetric hyperspin gauge fields are listed in the table 1.

From the above decomposition of hyperspin gauge field, the action for the hyperspin gauge interaction of hyperunified qubit-spinor field can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{G}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int d^{19}x\chi^{e}(x)\,\{\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}[\,\varSigma_{-}^{cab}{\mathcal{A}}_{[cab]}+\varSigma_{-}^{c\bar{A}\bar{B}}{\mathcal{A}}_{[c\bar{A}\bar{B}]}+\varSigma_{-}^{c\tilde{A}\tilde{B}}{\mathcal{A}}_{[c\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]}\,]\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}[\,\varSigma_{-}^{\tilde{C}ab}{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{C}ab]}+\varSigma_{-}^{\tilde{C}\bar{A}\bar{B}}{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{C}\bar{A}\bar{B}]}+\varSigma_{-}^{\tilde{C}\tilde{A}\tilde{B}}{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{C}\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]}\,]\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\},

with the definitions:

\displaystyle\varSigma_{-}^{cab}\equiv\varSigma_{-}^{c}\varSigma^{ab},\quad\varSigma_{-}^{c\bar{A}\bar{B}}\equiv\varSigma_{-}^{c}\varSigma^{\bar{A}\bar{B}},\quad\varSigma_{-}^{c\tilde{A}\tilde{B}}\equiv\varSigma_{-}^{c}\varSigma^{\tilde{A}\tilde{B}},\quad\varSigma_{-}^{c\bar{A}\tilde{A}}\equiv\varSigma_{-}^{c}\varSigma^{\bar{A}\tilde{A}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{G}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{-}}\varGamma^{K}\mathcal{H}_{K}^{p}\frac{1}{4}(\lambda_{2}\lambda_{p})_{ff^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f^{\prime}}}^{-}}+\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f^{\prime}}}^{+}}\varGamma^{K}\mathcal{H}_{K}^{p}\frac{1}{4}(\lambda_{2}\lambda_{p})_{ff^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f^{\prime}}}^{+}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\lambda_{2})_{ff^{\prime}}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{-}}\varGamma^{K}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{ab}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{ab}+\mathcal{H}_{KIJ}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{IJ})\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f^{\prime}}}^{-}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\lambda_{2})_{ff^{\prime}}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{+}}\varGamma^{K}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{ab}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{ab}+\mathcal{H}_{KIJ}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{IJ})\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f^{\prime}}}^{+}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{-}}\varGamma^{c}\varGamma_{I}{\mathcal{V}}_{c}^{I\tilde{a}}\frac{1}{4}(\lambda_{2}\eta_{\tilde{a}})_{ff^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f^{\prime}}}^{+}}+\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{+}}\varGamma^{c}\varGamma_{I}{\mathcal{V}}_{c}^{I\tilde{a}}\frac{1}{4}(\lambda_{2}\eta_{\tilde{a}})_{ff^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f^{\prime}}}^{-}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{-}}\eta^{\tilde{c}}_{ff^{\prime}}(\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{c}}^{ab}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{ab}+\varPhi_{\tilde{c}}^{IJ}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{IJ})\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f^{\prime}}}^{+}}+\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{+}}\eta^{\tilde{c}}_{ff^{\prime}}(\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{c}}^{ab}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{ab}+\varPhi_{\tilde{c}}^{IJ}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma_{IJ})\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f^{\prime}}}^{-}}

with

It is clear that the decomposed hyperspin gauge fields
{\mathcal{A}}_{[cab]}
,

{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]}\equiv\mathcal{H}_{K}^{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\equiv\mathcal{H}_{K}^{p}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}\bar{A}\bar{B}]}\equiv\mathcal{H}_{KIJ}
are Higgs-like scalar fields which provide scalar-type interactions either for lepton-quark states as grand unified qubit-spinor fields with negative U-parity or for mirror lepton-quark states as grand unified qubit-spinor fields with positive U-parity. The decomposed hyperspin gauge fields
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{C}\bar{A}\bar{B}]}\equiv\varPhi_{\tilde{c}}^{IJ}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\tilde{C}\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]}\equiv\varPhi_{[\tilde{c}\tilde{a}\tilde{b}]}\equiv\varPhi_{p}
are regarded as scalar-like fields, they bring on scalar-type interactions between lepton-quark and mirror lepton-quark states which are regarded as grand unified qubit-spinor fields with negative and positive U-parities, respectively.

As the subgroup hyperspin symmetry SP(10) leads to the usual grand unified symmetry SO(10)
\cong
SP(10) of leptons and quarks in SM, it contains the well-known subgroup symmetries as follows:

\displaystyle SP(10)\supset SP(6)\times SP(4)\cong SO(6)\times SO(4)\cong SU_{C}(4)\times SU_{L}(2)\times SU_{R}(2),

(406)

with SU
C
(4) the four color-spin symmetry of lepton-quark states and SU
L
(2)
\times
SU
R
(2) the left-right isospin symmetry of up-type and down-type lepton-quark states.

For our current purpose, let us pay attention to the properties of hyperspin gauge fields as Higgs-like scalar fields. From the above decomposition of SP(10) symmetry, the Higgs-like scalar field
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}\bar{A}\bar{B}]}\equiv\mathcal{H}_{KIJ}
belongs to the 120-dimensional representation of SO(10) and concerns the left-handed and right-handed triplet Higgs-like scalar fields of subgroups SU
L
(2) and SU
R
(2), respectively, which can bring about Majorana masses of neutrinos once the symmetry gets breaking down. The Higgs-like scalar field
{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]}\equiv\mathcal{H}_{K}^{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\equiv\mathcal{H}_{K}^{p}
is in both basic representation of SP(10) symmetry with
K=1,\cdots,10
and adjoint representation of SP(5) family-spin symmetry with
p=1,\cdots,10
. Since each basic representation of grand unified symmetry SP(10)
\cong
SO(10) contains one Higgs doublet, there are in general ten Higgs doublets corresponding to ten elements of SP(5) family-spin symmetry in 19-dimensional hyper-spacetime.

To be more explicit, let us write down the Yukawa-type coupling for ten Higgs-like scalar fields
\mathcal{H}_{K}^{p}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{Y}=\frac{1}{4}\bar{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{-}}\varGamma^{I}(\mathbf{H}_{I})_{ff^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f^{\prime}}}^{-}},

(407)

where the Higgs-like scalar matrix
(\mathbf{H}_{I})_{ff^{\prime}}
(
I=1,2,\cdots,10
) is given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{I}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{2}-i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{1}\gamma_{11}&i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{3}\gamma_{11}&\mathcal{H}_{I}^{8}-i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{7}\gamma_{11}&\mathcal{H}_{I}^{10}+i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{9}\gamma_{11}\\
i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{3}\gamma_{11}&\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{2}+i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{1}\gamma_{11}&-\mathcal{H}_{I}^{10}+i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{9}\gamma_{11}&\mathcal{H}_{I}^{8}+i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{7}\gamma_{11}\\
\mathcal{H}_{I}^{8}-i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{7}\gamma_{11}&-\mathcal{H}_{I}^{10}+i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{9}\gamma_{11}&\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{5}-i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{4}\gamma_{11}&i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{6}\gamma_{11}\\
\mathcal{H}_{I}^{10}+i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{9}\gamma_{11}&\mathcal{H}_{I}^{8}+i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{7}\gamma_{11}&i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{6}\gamma_{11}&\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{5}-i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{4}\gamma_{11}\end{pmatrix},

(408)

with the definitions

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{1}=\mathcal{H}^{1}+\mathcal{H}^{4},\quad\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{4}=\mathcal{H}^{1}-\mathcal{H}^{4},

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{2}=\mathcal{H}^{2}+\mathcal{H}^{5},\quad\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{5}=\mathcal{H}^{2}-\mathcal{H}^{5},

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{3}=\mathcal{H}^{3}+\mathcal{H}^{6},\quad\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{6}=\mathcal{H}^{3}-\mathcal{H}^{6}.

(409)

To be more explicit, let us express the grand unified qubit-spinor fields for the chiral type lepton-quark states appearing in SM as the westward entangled hyperqubit-spinor fields

presented in Eq.(
IV.1

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{-}}^{T}(x)\equiv\Psi_{W_{f}}^{T}(x)\equiv(\Psi_{fL},\Psi_{fR})^{T}

so that the above Yukawa-type coupling for Higgs-like scalar fields can be rewritten into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{Y}=\frac{1}{4}\bar{\Psi}_{fL}\Gamma_{I}(\mathsf{H}_{I})_{ff^{\prime}}\Psi_{f^{\prime}R}+H.c.,

(411)

with the Higgs-like scalar matrix in four-family Hilbert space,

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{I}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{1}-i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{2}&-\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{3}&\mathcal{H}_{I}^{7}-i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{8}&-\mathcal{H}_{I}^{9}-i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{10}\\
-\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{3}&-\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{1}-i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{2}&-\mathcal{H}_{I}^{9}+i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{10}&-\mathcal{H}_{I}^{7}-i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{8}\\
\mathcal{H}_{I}^{7}-i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{8}&-\mathcal{H}_{I}^{9}+i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{10}&\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{4}-i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{5}&-\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{6}\\
-\mathcal{H}_{I}^{9}-i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{10}&-\mathcal{H}_{I}^{7}-i\mathcal{H}_{I}^{8}&-\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{6}&-\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{4}-i\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{I}^{5}\end{pmatrix}.

Where

(

It can be checked that the Higgs-like scalar fields
\mathcal{H}_{I}^{p}(x)
with

bring on ten Higgs-like isospin doublets (

). Where

Note that as the Higgs-like scalar fields
\mathcal{H}_{I}^{p}(x)
arise from hyperspin gauge field with vector representations beyond four-dimensional spacetime in 19-dimensional locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, it is useful to revisit the action of hyperspin gauge field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime, which can easily be read from Eq.(
208
) as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}^{{\mathcal{A}}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[\delta^{D_{h}}\varkappa]\{\,-\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}

(414)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{B}}{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\mathbf{\Omega}^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}\,\}

which shows that such an action differs from the action of ordinary gauge field in globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime with absence of gravitational interaction. It is clear that once the locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime approaches to a globally flat hyper-spacetime in the limit case, i.e.,
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}\to\eta_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
and
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\to 0
, the above action gets to have only the first term which turns to be an action of ordinary gauge field in globally flat Minkowski hyper-spacetime.

It is verified in Eq.(
208
) that the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
appears as an auxiliary field due to the absence of its kinetic term in the action formulated in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. So that
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
satisfies a constraint equation presented in Eq.(
210
). Let us suppose that the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
is resolved from the constraint equation to be as a function of hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
together with other basic bosonic fields, so that we may formally express its solution as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}\equiv\Phi_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}({\mathcal{A}},\mathsf{W},\chi_{s}).

(415)

The action of hyperunified field theory in Eq.(
208
) can be rewritten into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[\delta^{D_{h}}\varkappa]\{\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-\beta_{Q}\sinh\chi_{s}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(416)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\tilde{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}+\frac{1}{4}g_{H}^{-2}\Phi_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}({\mathcal{A}},\mathsf{W},\chi_{s})\Phi^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{B}}({\mathcal{A}},\mathsf{W},\chi_{s}){\mathcal{A}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{B}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle g_{H}^{-2}(1+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})(\tilde{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}+\bar{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}})\Phi_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}({\mathcal{A}},\mathsf{W},\chi_{s})\Phi^{[\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{A}},\mathsf{W},\chi_{s})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{H}^{-2}\beta_{G}^{2}\beta_{Q}^{2}\sinh^{2}\chi_{s}\,{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}{\mathcal{A}}^{[\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}]\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}\bar{\eta}_{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}}^{\;\;\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}\Phi_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}({\mathcal{A}},\mathsf{W},\chi_{s})\Phi^{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{B}}({\mathcal{A}},\mathsf{W},\chi_{s})\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{A}}\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{B}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}(1+\sinh^{2}\chi_{s})\mathsf{W}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathsf{W}^{\mathbf{C}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g_{W}^{-2}\tilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}\tilde{\mathsf{F}}^{\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{S}^{2}\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}\hat{\eth}^{\mathbf{C}}\chi_{s}-\lambda_{D}^{2}{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{s})\,\},

which indicates that the hyperunified field theory formulated in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime does generate an effective potential of hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
when integrating out hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
. Such an action appears to involve no dynamical gravitational field, all dynamical fields get emergent gravitational interaction via non-commutative geometry described by the hyperspin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{D}]\mathbf{A}}
which behaves as auxiliary field and emerges as structure factor of non-Abelian Lie algebra for hyper-gravicoordinate derivative operator
\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}
as shown in Eq.(
VIII.3
).

Such an emergent gravitational interaction due to non-commutative geometry in 19-dimensional locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime is expected to bring on a distinguishing dynamical symmetry breaking mechanism for the Higgs-like bosons arising from hyperspin gauge field associated to vector representations of extra spatial dimensions. To study further the possible quantum contribution to effective potential of Higgs-like bosons, it needs to develop a new mathematical method for calculating quantum effect in the hyperunified field theory formulated in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime with emergent non-commutative geometry.

On the other hand, let us turn to the hyperunified field theory formulated equivalently within the framework of gravitational quantum field theory based on the concept of biframe hyper-spacetime as shown in Eqs.(
228
)-(
238
), where the hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
(or hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}
) become independent fundamental gauge fields. In such a framework of hyperunified field theory, the Higgs-like scalar fields are composed of two gauge fields
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
. To be explicit, let us examine the Higgs-like bosons
\mathcal{H}_{KIJ}
and
\mathcal{H}_{K\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\equiv\mathcal{H}_{K}^{p}
which are presented as totally antisymmetric hyperspin gauge field in locally flat gravigauge hyper-spacetime. In gravitational quantum field theory based on the biframe hyper-spacetime, the Higgs-like bosons
\mathcal{H}_{KIJ}
and
\mathcal{H}_{K\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}
can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{KIJ}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[K+4\;I+4\;J+4]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}\bar{A}\bar{B}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}(\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\bar{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\bar{A}\bar{B}}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\bar{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\bar{B}\bar{C}}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\bar{B}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\bar{C}\bar{A}})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{3}[\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{K}^{\;\mu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu IJ}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{I}^{\;\mu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu JK}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{J}^{\;\mu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu KI}

\displaystyle\quad+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{K}^{\;\bar{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{M}IJ}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{I}^{\;\bar{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{M}JK}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{J}^{\;\bar{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{M}KI}

\displaystyle\quad+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{K}^{\;\tilde{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{M}IJ}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{I}^{\;\tilde{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{M}JK}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{J}^{\;\tilde{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{M}KI}\,],

(417)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{K\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[K+4\;\tilde{a}+14\;\tilde{b}+14]}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[\bar{C}\tilde{A}\tilde{B}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}(\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\bar{C}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\tilde{A}\tilde{B}}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\tilde{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\tilde{B}\bar{C}}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\tilde{B}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}\bar{C}\tilde{A}})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{3}[\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{K}^{\;\mu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\tilde{a}}^{\;\mu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\tilde{b}K}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\tilde{b}}^{\;\mu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu K\tilde{a}}

\displaystyle\quad+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{K}^{\;\bar{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{M}\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\tilde{a}}^{\;\bar{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{M}\tilde{b}K}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\tilde{b}}^{\;\bar{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{M}K\tilde{a}}

\displaystyle\quad+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{K}^{\;\tilde{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{M}\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\tilde{a}}^{\;\tilde{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{M}\tilde{b}K}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\tilde{b}}^{\;\tilde{M}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{M}K\tilde{a}}\,],

(418)

with

\displaystyle\mu=0,1,2,3;\quad\bar{M}=5,\cdots,14;\quad\tilde{M}=15,\cdots,19.

(419)

which shows that the Higgs-like bosons may be regarded as composed fields of two fundamental fields
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
(dual to
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
) within the framework of gravitational quantum field theory. In such a theoretical framework, it allows us to analyze how hyperspin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
and hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
play the roles in connection to different extra dimensions when they are associated with Higgs-like bosons. As the hyper-gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}
not only characterizes geometric properties of hyper-spacetime via the hyper-gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mathbf{M}\mathbf{N}}=\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{M}}^{\;\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{N}\mathbf{A}}
but also governs hyperspin gauge transformations based on the gravitational origin of hyperspin gauge symmetry as shown in Eqs.(
47
)-(
III.2
), the gauge symmetries associated with Higgs-like bosons can be made to be broken down through geometric symmetry breaking mechanisms
GGFT6D
. Therefore, from the gravity-gauge and gauge-geometry correspondences shown in the hyperunified field theory, some geometric symmetry breaking scenarios proposed in extra dimension model
RS
may also be applicable to the gauge symmetry breaking associated with Higgs-like bosons, which is usually carried out by first compacting the extra dimensions and then taking proper boundary conditions for both hyper-gravigauge and hyperspin gauge fields associated with Higgs-like bosons.

To produce the mass spectrum and mixings of leptons and quarks determined in SM, it is essential to investigate the gauge symmetry breaking in connection with grand unified gauge symmetry SP(10) and family-spin gauge symmetry SP(5), which are the subgroup symmetries of hyperspin gauge symmetry SP(1,18), i.e., SP(1,3)
\times
SP(10)
\times
SP(5)
\in
SP(1,18). It is noted that the hyperspin gauge symmetry is a unified gauge symmetry which involves only one gauge coupling constant, thus a basic question arises naturally how the Higgs-like bosons associated with gauge fields obtain heavy masses and meanwhile the leptons and quarks in SM achieve correct hierarchical mass spectrum through Yukawa-type couplings of Higgs-like bosons, in particular, for the heavy top quark mass. To make a general issue for such a question, it is interesting to observe the fact that the extra dimensions of hyper-spacetime in the hyperunified field theory are all physically meaningful since they are correlated to the hyperspin charges which represent the intrinsic quantum numbers of hyperunified qubit-spinor field, such a feature is different from the usual extra dimension models and superstring theory. So that the hierarchical mass spectrum and mixings of leptons and quarks in SM should be related to the localizing distribution amplitudes of fundamental fields and characterized from gauge symmetry breaking of family-spin gauge symmetry SP(5) as well as reflected by geometric properties of extra dimensions described by relevant hyper-gravimetric field
\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\tilde{M}\tilde{N}}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\tilde{M}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\;\tilde{N}}\eta^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}
(
\tilde{M},\tilde{N}=15,\cdots,19
) and also by relevant dual hyper-gravigauge fields
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\tilde{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\bar{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
. As shown explicitly in Eqs.(
XI.2
) and (
XI.2
), the Higgs-like bosons
\mathcal{H}_{K\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}\equiv\mathcal{H}_{K}^{p}
and
\mathcal{H}_{KIJ}
rely on the dual hyper-gravigauge fields
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\tilde{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\tilde{a}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{\bar{A}}^{\;\mathbf{M}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{I}^{\;\mathbf{M}}
.

In general, the hyperspin gauge symmetry including family-spin gauge symmetry and isospin gauge symmetry is expected to be broken down dynamically and geometrically via the effective potential of gauge fields associated with Higgs-like bosons and localizing distribution amplitudes of fundamental fields as well as the compactification of bulk space in hyper-spacetime, so as to generate the standard model and achieve the correct masses and mixings of leptons and quarks as well as sources of CP violation
SCPV1a
;
SCPV1b
;
SCPV2
including CP-violating KM phase
KM
in SM. Theoretically, to reproduce the observed Higgs boson of SM in four-dimensional spacetime, it requires to solve the dynamical evolutions of hyperspin gauge field and hyper-gravigauge field as well as hyperunified qubit-spinor field in hyper-spacetime. As such dynamical evolutions concern highly nonlinear equations and intrinsic properties of 15-dimensional compact bulk space in 19-dimensional hyper-spacetime, it is beyond the scope of present paper. Nevertheless, it should be interesting to figure out some reasonable phenomenological models at low energies, which is going to be investigated in detail elsewhere based on the above analyses and discussions.

XI.3
Appearance of three families of chiral type leptons and quarks in SM and property of the fourth family of lepton-quark state with chirality-correlation discrete symmetry of bulk space

The standard model with three families of chiral type leptons and quarks has been tested up to 1.4 TeV energy scale at LHC, while the hyperunified qubit-spinor field in 19-dimensional hyper-spacetime contains four families of vector-like lepton-quark states. Namely, there exist in general four families of chiral type lepton-quark states with negative U-parity and the corresponding four families of mirror lepton-quark states with positive U-parity in 19-dimensional hyper-spacetime. To comprehend current experimental observations on three families of chiral type leptons and quarks in SM, the inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry and Poincaré-type symmetry must be broken down to the symmetry of SM in four-dimensional spacetime at low energies. In general, one may consider several alternative scenarios in treating the fourth family of vector-like lepton-quark states. A naive scenario is that there might exist the fourth family of chiral type leptons and quarks with very heavy masses and small mixings to other leptons and quarks in three families, so that they are still beyond the current experimental detections. Another simple scenario is that the fourth family is composed of vector-like lepton-quark states with masses above the current experimental lower limit around 1370 GeV
PDG
. We will illustrate a possible scenario with the appearance of three families of chiral type leptons and quarks in SM and the presence of the fourth family of vector-like lepton-quark states.

To realize three families of chiral type leptons and quarks in SM, it needs to appropriately explore symmetry breaking mechanisms and scenarios of hyperunified qubit-spinor field. For such a purpose, we are going to focus on the action of hyperunified qubit-spinor field after inflation and investigate the property of hyperunified qubit-spinor field in bulk space. From the actions presented in Eq.(
378
) and Eq.(
348
), it is straightforward to write down the action of hyperunified qubit-spinor field after inflation in Einstein type basis as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{H}\mathsf{U}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{19}x]\,\chi^{e}\{\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\varSigma_{-}^{\mathbf{C}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}-m_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}

(420)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle[M_{S}\sqrt{1+\lambda_{\chi}^{2}\lambda_{\kappa}^{2}}\sinh\frac{\Phi}{M_{S}}+2\lambda_{\chi}m_{\kappa}\sinh^{2}\frac{\Phi}{2M_{S}}]\,\bar{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\tilde{\varSigma}_{-}\mathbf{\Psi}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{H}}}\},

with the covariant derivative:

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{C}}\equiv i\hat{\eth}_{\mathbf{C}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{C}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}\equiv\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{C}}^{e\mathbf{M}}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbf{M}}=\bar{\chi}_{\mathbf{C}}^{e\mathbf{M}}(i\partial_{\mathbf{M}}+{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mathbf{M}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}]}\frac{1}{2}\varSigma^{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}}).

When the potential energy of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scaling field is transmuted into kinetic energies of fundamental fields after inflation, the universe is going to undergo the epoch of radiative and matter dominated expansions. The hyperunified qubit-spinor field and hyperspin gauge field as well as hyper-gravigauge field and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}
-spin scalar field will evolve from high energy scale characterized by the fundamental mass scale
M_{\kappa}
to low energies. The localizing distribution amplitudes of fundamental fields in hyper-spacetime are considered to appear as effective fields at low energies. Meanwhile, the inhomogeneous hyperspin symmetry is expected to be broken down to its subgroup symmetries, so that the standard model can consistently be realized in four-dimensional spacetime.

For illustration, we will examine a simple symmetry breaking scenario for hyperspin symmetry. It has been shown in ref.
FHUFT-I
that the dimension of hyper-spacetime is correlated to the hyperspin charge of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field, so that the local distribution amplitudes of entangled hyperqubit-spinor field not only exhibit the hyperspin symmetry in Hilbert space but also reflect the motion-correlation symmetry in Minkowski hyper-spacetime. To arrive at three families of chiral type leptons and quarks in SM, it is necessary to bring local distribution amplitudes corresponding to three families of chiral type leptons and quarks of hyperunified qubit-spinor field in hyper-spacetime to be distinguishable from three families of mirror lepton-quark states and also from the fourth family of lepton-quark states. For simplicity, let us take the nineteenth spatial dimension of hyper-spacetime as a bulk dimension with its coordinate regarded as bulk-coordinate, so that the coordinates in 19-dimensional hyper-spacetime are decomposed into two parts:

\displaystyle x^{\mathbf{M}}=(x^{\mathsf{M}},x^{19})\equiv(x,z),\quad\mathsf{M}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,18,

(421)

with
x^{\mathsf{M}}
denoting the coordinates in 18-dimensional hyper-spacetime and
x^{19}\equiv z
representing the bulk-coordinate.

Let us consider chirality-correlation discrete symmetry of bulk dimension under
Z_{2}
reflection operation. Namely, the local distribution amplitudes of grand unified qubit-spinor fields for four families of vector-like lepton-quark states
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}}(x,z)
(
f=1,2,3,4
) are proposed to obey the following chirality-correlation discrete
Z_{2}
-parity under reflection operation on bulk-coordinates
z\to-z
:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{i}}}(x,-z)=-\gamma_{15}\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{i}}}(x,z),\quad i=1,2,3,

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{4}}}(x,-z)=-\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{4}}}(x,z),\quad\mbox{or}\quad\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{4}}}(x,-z)=\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{4}}}(x,z),

\displaystyle\gamma_{15}=\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes\sigma_{0}.

(422)

Explicitly, we can express the chirality-correlation discrete
Z_{2}
-parity for four families of chiral type lepton-quark states
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{-}}(x,z)
and mirror lepton-quark states
\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{f}}^{+}}(x,z)
as follows:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{i}}^{-}}(x,-z)=+\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{i}}^{-}}(x,z),\;\;\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{i}}^{+}}(x,-z)=-\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{i}}^{+}}(x,z),\;\;i=1,2,3,

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{4}}^{+}}(x,-z)=\pm\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{4}}^{+}}(x,z),\quad\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{4}}^{-}}(x,-z)=\pm\Psi_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathsf{G}_{4}}^{-}}(x,z),

which indicates that the distribution amplitudes for three families of chiral type lepton-quark states with negative U-parity are even functions under
Z_{2}
reflection operation on the bulk coordinate
z
and meanwhile the distribution amplitudes for three families of mirror lepton-quark states with positive U-parity are odd functions. Whereas the distribution amplitudes for both chiral type lepton-quark states and mirror lepton-quark states in the fourth family are considered to have the same
Z_{2}
-parity under
Z_{2}
reflection operation.

The symmetric hyper-gravigauge field

\displaystyle\hat{\bar{\chi}}_{\mathsf{A}}^{e\,\mathsf{M}}(x,-z)=+\hat{\bar{\chi}}_{\mathsf{A}}^{e\,\mathsf{M}}(x,z),\quad\hat{\bar{\chi}}_{19}^{e\,19}(x,-z)=+\hat{\bar{\chi}}_{19}^{e\,19}(x,z),

\displaystyle\hat{\bar{\chi}}_{19}^{e\,\mathsf{M}}(x,-z)=-\hat{\bar{\chi}}_{19}^{e\,\mathsf{M}}(x,z),\quad\hat{\bar{\chi}}_{\mathsf{A}}^{e\,19}(x,-z)=-\hat{\bar{\chi}}_{\mathsf{A}}^{e\,19}(x,z),

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}^{e}(x,-z)=+\bar{\chi}^{e}(x,-z),\quad\Phi(x,-z)=+\Phi(x,z),

\displaystyle\mathsf{A},\mathsf{M}=0,1,2,3,5,\cdots,18.

\displaystyle-
